## Act I / 第一幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
A Chocolate-house.
***Mirabell** and **Fainall** rising from cards. **Betty** waiting.*

===Chinese===
一间巧克力屋。

*巧克力屋里。**米兰芒特（Mirabell）**与**费诺（Fainall）**打完一副牌起身，**贝蒂（Betty）**在旁伺候。*

===Original===
Mirabell
You are a fortunate man, Mr. Fainall.
Fainall
Have we done?
Mirabell
What you please. I’ll play on to entertain you.
Fainall
No, I’ll give you your revenge another time, when you are not so indifferent; you are thinking of something else now, and play too negligently: the coldness of a losing gamester lessens the pleasure of the winner. I’d no more play with a man that slighted his ill fortune than I’d make love to a woman who undervalued the loss of her reputation.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 费诺先生，你是个走运的人。

**费诺.** 咱们这就散了？

**米兰芒特.** 随你的便。我接着陪打，权当给你解闷。

**费诺.** 不，改天你心无旁骛时，再让你扳回一局。这会儿你心里另有所思，牌出得懒洋洋——输家意兴阑珊，赢家的兴致也要跟着打折。跟一个把自己霉运不当回事的人打牌，我是决计不干的，正如我决计不向一个把丢了名誉不当回事的女人求爱。

===Original===
Mirabell
You have a taste extremely delicate, and are for refining on your pleasures.
Fainall
Prithee, why so reserved? Something has put you out of humour.
Mirabell
Not at all: I happen to be grave today, and you are gay; that’s all.
Fainall
Confess, Millamant and you quarrelled last night, after I left you; my fair cousin has some humours that would tempt the patience of a Stoic. What, some coxcomb came in, and was well received by her, while you were by?
Mirabell
Witwoud and Petulant, and what was worse, her aunt, your wife’s mother, my evil genius﻿—or to sum up all in her own name, my old Lady Wishfort came in.
Fainall
Oh, there it is then: she has a lasting passion for you, and with reason.﻿—What, then my wife was there?
Mirabell
Yes, and Mrs. Marwood and three or four more, whom I never saw before; seeing me, they all put on their grave faces, whispered one another, then complained aloud of the vapours, and after fell into a profound silence.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 你的口味真是精致入微，连寻欢作乐也讲究提纯。

**费诺.** 嗳，何必这样讳莫如深？总有件事惹你不痛快了。

**米兰芒特.** 哪里：不过今天碰巧我庄重、你轻快，如此而已。

**费诺.** 招了吧，昨晚我走以后，你和米勒曼特（Millamant）拌嘴了；我那位漂亮表妹的脾气，连斯多噶哲人的耐性也要被磨光。怎么，当着你的面来了个绣花枕头，她还待他十分亲热？

**米兰芒特.** 来的是威特伍德（Witwoud）和佩特兰特（Petulant），更糟的是她那位姨妈——你太太的娘、我的灾星——干脆用她本人的名号把这一切总括：我那位威什福特夫人（Lady Wishfort）老太太也大驾光临。

**费诺.** 哦，病根原来在这儿：她对你一往情深、历久弥坚，倒也事出有因。——怎么，我太太当时也在场？

**米兰芒特.** 在，还有玛伍德夫人（Mrs. Marwood），外加三四位我素未谋面的女士。她们一见我，个个端起严肃面孔，交头接耳，继而高声抱怨忧郁气上涌，随后便落入一片意味深长的寂静。

===Original===
Fainall
They had a mind to be rid of you.
Mirabell
For which reason I resolved not to stir. At last the good old lady broke through her painful taciturnity with an invective against long visits. I would not have understood her, but Millamant joining in the argument, I rose and with a constrained smile told her, I thought nothing was so easy as to know when a visit began to be troublesome; she reddened and I withdrew, without expecting her reply.
Fainall
You were to blame to resent what she spoke only in compliance with her aunt.
Mirabell
She is more mistress of herself than to be under the necessity of such a resignation.
Fainall
What! though half her fortune depends upon her marrying with my lady’s approbation?
Mirabell
I was then in such a humour, that I should have been better pleased if she had been less discreet.
Fainall
Now I remember, I wonder not they were weary of you; last night was one of their cabal nights: they have ’em three times a week and meet by turns at one another’s apartments, where they come together like the coroner’s inquest, to sit upon the murdered reputations of the week. You and I are excluded, and it was once proposed that all the male sex should be excepted; but somebody moved that to avoid scandal there might be one man of the community, upon which motion Witwoud and Petulant were enrolled members.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 她们是想撵你走。

**米兰芒特.** 所以我打定主意偏不动窝。末了，好心的老太太总算熬过了那份吃力的缄默，就冗长拜访发起一场声讨。我本可以只当没听懂，偏偏米勒曼特也加入战团，我只好起身，强堆出笑脸对她说：依我看，要辨出一个拜访从哪一刻起成了打扰，实在是天下顶容易的事。她红了脸，我不等她回话便抽身而去。

**费诺.** 她那些话不过顺着她姨妈说的，你竟当真计较，就是你的不是了。

**米兰芒特.** 她是个极有主意的人，断不至于非做这样的屈从不可。

**费诺.** 什么！她一半身家可都拴在讨我岳母的欢心上，这也顾不得了？

**米兰芒特.** 我彼时正上着火，她若少些乖觉，我倒痛快些。

**费诺.** 这么一提我想起来了，难怪她们嫌你烦：昨晚是她们的密社之夜。每礼拜三回，轮流在各家宅子里聚齐。她们凑作一团，活脱一座验尸陪审团，专就本礼拜被害死的诸般名誉开庭验尸。你我皆在摒除之列；本来还动议把须眉男子一概逐出，后来有人说，为避物议，社内不妨收留一名男性——凭这一动议，威特伍德和佩特兰特便双双入社。

===Original===
Mirabell
And who may have been the foundress of this sect? My Lady Wishfort, I warrant, who publishes her detestation of mankind, and full of the vigour of fifty-five, declares for a friend and ratafia;9 and let posterity shift for itself, she’ll breed no more.
Fainall
The discovery of your sham addresses to her, to conceal your love to her niece, has provoked this separation. Had you dissembled better, things might have continued in the state of nature.10

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 那么这门宗派的开山祖师又是哪一位？我敢打保票，是威什福特夫人：她昭告天下与人类不共戴天，怀揣五十五岁的充沛元气，宣言此生只认朋友与拉塔菲亚甜酒（9）；后代自有后代福，她是一概不生了。

**费诺.** 你假意向她献殷勤、好遮掩你对她外甥女的爱情——这把戏一拆穿，便惹出这场男女分立。当初你若装得再像些，万事兴许还停在自然状态（10）。

===Original===
Mirabell
I did as much as man could, with any reasonable conscience; I proceeded to the very last act of flattery with her, and was guilty of a song in her commendation. Nay, I got a friend to put her into a lampoon, and compliment her with the imputation of an affair with a young fellow, which I carried so far, that I told her the malicious town took notice that she was grown fat of a sudden; and when she lay in of a dropsy, persuaded her she was reported to be in labour. The devil’s in’t, if an old woman is to be flattered further, unless a man should endeavour downright personally to debauch her: and that my virtue forbade me. But for the discovery of this amour, I am indebted to your friend, or your wife’s friend, Mrs. Marwood.
Fainall
What should provoke her to be your enemy, unless she has made you advances which you have slighted? Women do not easily forgive omissions of that nature.
Mirabell
She was always civil to me, till of late. I confess I am not one of those coxcombs who are apt to interpret a woman’s good manners to her prejudice, and think that she who does not refuse ’em everything can refuse ’em nothing.
Fainall
You are a gallant man, Mirabell; and though you may have cruelty enough not to satisfy a lady’s longing, you have too much generosity not to be tender of her honour. Yet you speak with an indifference which seems to be affected, and confesses you are conscious of a negligence.
Mirabell
You pursue the argument with a distrust that seems to be unaffected, and confesses you are conscious of a concern for which the lady is more indebted to you than is your wife.
Fainall
Fie, fie, friend, if you grow censorious I must leave you.﻿—I’ll look upon the gamesters in the next room.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 凭着一个体面人的良知，我把能做的都做尽了：对她的谀奉一路推进到最后一幕，还作词一曲为她标榜。非但如此，我托一位朋友把她写进讽刺小诗，恭维她与一位少年郎有染——我一路捧到底，甚至告诉她：刻薄的城里人已经留意到她骤然发福；她害水肿卧床，我还劝解她说，外面风传她是有喜了。一个老太太若还要往上奉承，除非那男人索性亲身出马、直截了当去勾引她本人——这一层，我的道德不许。至于这桩风流韵事如何败露，我得领你那位朋友、或者说你太太那位朋友的情：玛伍德夫人。

**费诺.** 她与你为敌，图的是什么？除非她曾向你递过情意，却被你轻轻搁下。这路怠慢，女人可不肯轻易饶恕。

**米兰芒特.** 她从前待我一向客气，只是近来才变卦。我承认我不是那种绣花枕头，动辄把女人的客气解作她的失足，以为她既不肯把什么都回绝，便必然什么都不回绝。

**费诺.** 你是位护花的君子，米兰芒特：尽管你狠得下心，不肯慰帖一位女士的渴慕，却也慷慨得紧，必不忍教她的名节受损。只是你说这话时的那份淡漠，未免做作，倒招认了你自知有亏。

**米兰芒特.** 你顺着话头步步设防，那份疑心倒不像装出来的，恰恰招认了你心里有一桩牵挂——为着这桩牵挂，那位太太欠你的情，只怕比你的太太还多。

**费诺.** 呸，呸，朋友，你再这样苛察，我只得躲开你了。——我去隔壁瞧瞧那几位赌客。

===Original===
Mirabell
Who are they?
Fainall
Petulant and Witwoud.﻿—*To **Betty**.* Bring me some chocolate. *Exit.*
Mirabell
Betty, what says your clock?
Betty
Turned of the last canonical hour,11 sir. *Exit.*
Mirabell
How pertinently the jade answers me! *Looking on his watch.* Ha! almost one a’ clock!﻿—Oh, y’are come!

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 都是谁？

**费诺.** 佩特兰特和威特伍德。——*对**贝蒂**。*给我端份巧克力来。*下。*

**米兰芒特.** 贝蒂，你的钟怎么说？

**贝蒂.** 划过最后一个正典时辰（11）了，先生。*下。*

**米兰芒特.** 这丫头答得可真严丝合缝！*看表。*哈！都快一点钟了！——哦，你来了！

===Original===
*Enter **Footman**.*
Mirabell
Well, is the grand affair over? You have been something tedious.
Footman
Sir, there’s such coupling at Pancras12 that they stand behind one another, as ’twere in a country-dance. Ours was the last couple to lead up; and no hopes appearing of dispatch, besides, the parson growing hoarse, we were afraid his lungs would have failed before it came to our turn; so we drove round to Duke’s Place,13 and there they were riveted in a trice.
Mirabell
So, so, you are sure they are married?
Footman
Married and bedded, sir; I am witness.
Mirabell
Have you the certificate?
Footman
Here it is, sir.
Mirabell
Has the tailor brought Waitwell’s clothes home, and the new liveries?
Footman
Yes, sir.
Mirabell
That’s well. Do you go home again, d’ye hear, and adjourn the consummation till farther order. Bid Waitwell shake his ears, and Dame Partlet14 rustle up her feathers, and meet me at one o’clock by Rosamond’s Pond,15 that I may see her before she returns to her lady. And, as you tender your ears, be secret.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
*男仆上。*

**米兰芒特.** 怎么样，那桩大事办完了？你可真有点磨蹭。

**男仆.** 先生，圣潘克拉斯（12）那儿配对的挤成一团，一对贴着一对站，简直像跳乡村舞。我们那对排在压轴领舞，眼见一时半会儿轮不上；牧师的嗓子又喊哑了，我们生怕还没轮到，他的肺先告罢工。于是绕道赶去杜克广场13，一眨眼就铆得结结实实。

**米兰芒特.** 好，好，你拿得准他们成了亲？

**男仆.** 成了亲，还上了床，先生；我是见证。

**米兰芒特.** 婚书拿到了？

**男仆.** 在这儿，先生。

**米兰芒特.** 裁缝把韦特韦尔（Waitwell）的衣裳送来了没有？新号衣呢？

**男仆.** 送来了，先生。

**米兰芒特.** 很好。你再跑一趟家里，听着：圆房一事暂行搁置，听候后命。叫韦特韦尔抖擞起耳朵，叫帕特利特大娘（14）扑棱扑棱她的羽毛，一点钟到罗莎蒙德池（15）边会我，趁她回主子跟前之前，我得见她一面。还有——疼你那对耳朵的话，把嘴闭牢。

*同下。*

### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
The same.
***Mirabell**, **Fainall**, and **Betty**.*
Fainall
Joy of your success, Mirabell; you look pleased.
Mirabell
Aye; I have been engaged in a matter of some sort of mirth, which is not yet ripe for discovery. I am glad this is not a cabal-night. I wonder, Fainall, that you who are married, and of consequence should be discreet, will suffer your wife to be of such a party.
Fainall
Faith, I am not jealous. Besides, most who are engaged are women and relations; and for the men, they are of a kind too contemptible to give scandal.

===Chinese===
同前。

*巧克力屋里。**米兰芒特**、**费诺**与**贝蒂**。*

**费诺.** 恭喜你大功告成，米兰芒特；瞧你一脸喜色。

**米兰芒特.** 可不是；我手头正办着一桩颇有些滑稽意味的事，只是火候未到，还揭不得盅。幸而今晚不是密社之夜。费诺，我就不明白：你是个有妇之夫，照理该知道避嫌，怎么容你太太入那样的社？

**费诺.** 实话实说，我不吃醋。再说，入社的大半是女眷亲戚；至于男的，都属不值一提之流，闹不出风化案子来。

===Original===
Mirabell
I am of another opinion: the greater the coxcomb, always the more the scandal; for a woman who is not a fool can have but one reason for associating with a man who is one.
Fainall
Are you jealous as often as you see Witwoud entertained by Millamant?
Mirabell
Of her understanding I am, if not of her person.
Fainall
You do her wrong; for, to give her her due, she has wit.
Mirabell
She has beauty enough to make any man think so, and complaisance enough not to contradict him who shall tell her so.
Fainall
For a passionate lover methinks you are a man somewhat too discerning in the failings of your mistress.
Mirabell
And for a discerning man somewhat too passionate a lover, for I like her with all her faults; nay, like her for her faults. Her follies are so natural, or so artful, that they become her, and those affectations which in another woman would be odious serve but to make her more agreeable. I’ll tell thee, Fainall, she once used me with that insolence that in revenge I took her to pieces, sifted her, and separated her failings: I studied ’em and got ’em by rote. The catalogue was so large that I was not without hopes, one day or other, to hate her heartily. To which end I so used myself to think of ’em, that at length, contrary to my design and expectation, they gave me every hour less and less disturbance, till in a few days it became habitual to me to remember ’em without being displeased. They are now grown as familiar to me as my own frailties, and in all probability in a little time longer I shall like ’em as well.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 我却另有所见：绣花枕头越大，风声越紧；因为一个不傻的女人肯跟傻瓜周旋，缘由只会有一个。

**费诺.** 每回见威特伍德在米勒曼特跟前凑趣，你可会吃味？

**米兰芒特.** 吃味倒不在她的人，在她的见识。

**费诺.** 你冤枉她了；凭心而论，她是有才情的。

**米兰芒特.** 她的姿色足以教任何男人这么想，她的温存又足以教这么说她的男人不受驳斥。

**费诺.** 照我看，你这个热恋中人，对情人的短处未免明察得太细。

**米兰芒特.** 而你这个明察之人，爱得又未免太热：我连她的短处一并喜欢；不，正是喜欢她的短处。她的痴态那样自然，或者说那样巧妙，生在她身上恰到好处；那些做作换了别的女人教人齿冷，在她身上只添风致。告诉你，费诺，她有一回待我那般无礼，我赌气把她整个拆开、过了筛，把毛病一桩桩分拣出来，潜心研读，背得烂熟。那份目录长得可观，我一度指望迟早有一天能把她恨个透彻。为达此目的，我日日拿来温习，谁知事与愿违，它们时时刻刻给我的气恼越来越轻，不出几日，想起它们而不动气竟成了习惯。如今它们于我熟稔如自家的毛病，多半再过些时日，我会把它们爱得一样深。

===Original===
Fainall
Marry her, marry her; be half as well acquainted with her charms as you are with her defects, and, my life on’t, you are your own man again.
Mirabell
Say you so?
Fainall
Aye, aye; I have experience. I have a wife, and so forth.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 娶她吧，娶她吧；你对她的好处只要有对她的毛病一半熟，我拿性命打赌，你又是条好汉。

**米兰芒特.** 此话当真？

**费诺.** 当真，当真；我有经验。我有一房太太，如此等等。

===Original===
*Enter **Messenger**.*
Messenger
Is one squire Witwoud here?
Betty
Yes; what’s your business?
Messenger
I have a letter for him, from his brother Sir Wilfull, which I am charged to deliver into his own hands.
Betty
He’s in the next room, friend. That way.
*Exit **Messenger**.*

===Chinese===
*送信人上。*

**送信人.** 这里有位威特伍德乡绅吗？

**贝蒂.** 有；你找他何事？

**送信人.** 我带了一封信给他，是他兄弟威尔福爵士写来的，嘱我务必当面递交。

**贝蒂.** 他在隔壁屋里，朋友。往那边走。

*送信人下。*

===Original===
Mirabell
What, is the chief of that noble family in town, Sir Wilfull Witwoud?
Fainall
He is expected today. Do you know him?
Mirabell
I have seen him; he promises to be an extraordinary person. I think you have the honour to be related to him.
Fainall
Yes; he is half-brother to this Witwoud by a former wife, who was sister to my Lady Wishfort, my wife’s mother. If you marry Millamant, you must call cousins too.
Mirabell
I had rather be his relation than his acquaintance.
Fainall
He comes to town in order to equip himself for travel.
Mirabell
For travel! Why the man that I mean is above forty.
Fainall
No matter for that; ’tis for the honour of England that all Europe should know we have blockheads of all ages.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 怎么，那个高贵门第的当家老爷进城了？威尔福·威特伍德爵士？

**费诺.** 就是今日可到。你认识他？

**米兰芒特.** 见过；照那苗头，是个非同凡响的人物。我记得你有幸与他沾着亲。

**费诺.** 正是；他是这位威特伍德的同父异母兄长，前头太太所生——那位前太太又是威什福特夫人、也就是我岳母的姐妹。你若娶米勒曼特，少不得也要攀这门表亲。

**米兰芒特.** 我宁可做他的亲戚，不愿做他的相识。

**费诺.** 他此番进城，为的是置办行装，预备游历。

**米兰芒特.** 游历！我说的那个人可是年过四十了。

**费诺.** 那有什么打紧；为了英格兰的体面，正该让全欧洲知道：咱们的蠢材，老幼齐备，各档都有。

===Original===
Mirabell
I wonder there is not an act of parliament to save the credit of the nation and prohibit the exportation of fools.
Fainall
By no means, ’tis better as ’tis; ’tis better to trade with a little loss, than to be quite eaten up with being overstocked.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 我纳闷国会怎么不立一项法案，保全国家的信誉，禁止蠢材出口。

**费诺.** 万万不可，眼下这样就很好：做买卖折些小本，总强过存货壅塞、坐吃山空。

===Original===
Mirabell
Pray, are the follies of this knight-errant and those of the squire, his brother, anything related?
Fainall
Not at all: Witwoud grows by the knight like a medlar grafted on a crab. One will melt in your mouth and t’other set your teeth on edge; one is all pulp and the other all core.
Mirabell
So one will be rotten before he be ripe, and the other will be rotten without ever being ripe at all.
Fainall
Sir Wilfull is an odd mixture of bashfulness and obstinacy. But when he’s drunk, he’s as loving as the monster in *The Tempest*,16 and much after the same manner. To give t’other his due, he has something of good-nature, and does not always want wit.
Mirabell
Not always: but as often as his memory fails him and his commonplace of comparisons.17 He is a fool with a good memory and some few scraps of other folks’ wit. He is one whose conversation can never be approved, yet it is now and then to be endured. He has indeed one good quality: he is not exceptious, for he so passionately affects the reputation of understanding raillery that he will construe an affront into a jest, and call downright rudeness and ill language satire and fire.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 请问，这位游侠骑士的糊涂，跟他的乡绅兄弟的糊涂，可有什么亲缘？

**费诺.** 全无亲缘：威特伍德长在骑士身上，好比欧楂嫁接在酸苹果树上。一个入口即化，一个酸倒牙齿；一个通身是肉，一个通身是核。

**米兰芒特.** 这么说，一个不到熟就烂，一个压根不熟也烂。

**费诺.** 威尔福爵士是羞怯加固执的怪味合剂。可他一灌下黄汤，就跟《暴风雨》（16）里那怪物一般恩爱缠绵，路数也如出一辙。凭心替另一位讲句公道话，他有几分好脾气，也不至于全无才思。

**米兰芒特.** 未必全无：只是他的记性同他那本比喻的花名册（17）一样，动不动就告罄。他是个记性不坏、肚里攒着几片人家才思碎屑的傻子。同他交谈永难称许，间或倒还耐得住。他确乎有一桩长处：不较真；他一心贪着「解得妙语」的名声，肯把当面冒犯解作玩笑，把彻头彻尾的粗鲁恶语唤作讽刺与锋芒。

===Original===
Fainall
If you have a mind to finish his picture, you have an opportunity to do it at full length. Behold the original.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 你若有兴致替这幅画像收笔，眼下正好有个真人等身的机会。请看原本。

*威特伍德上。*

===Original===
*Enter **Witwoud**.*
Witwoud
Afford me your compassion, my dears! Pity me, Fainall, Mirabell, pity me.
Mirabell
I do from my soul.
Fainall
Why, what’s the matter?
Witwoud
No letters for me, Betty?
Betty
Did not a messenger bring you one but now, sir?
Witwoud
Aye, but no other?
Betty
No, sir.
Witwoud
That’s hard, that’s very hard.﻿—A messenger! A mule, a beast of burden, he has brought me a letter from the fool my brother, as heavy as a panegyric in a funeral sermon, or a copy of commendatory verses from one poet to another. And what’s worse, ’tis as sure a forerunner of the author as an epistle dedicatory.

===Chinese===
**威特伍德.** 施舍点怜悯吧，诸位亲爱的！可怜我，费诺，米兰芒特，可怜我。

**米兰芒特.** 我打心眼里可怜你。

**费诺.** 咦，出了什么事？

**威特伍德.** 没有我的信，贝蒂？

**贝蒂.** 方才不是才有个送信的给您送了一封吗，先生？

**威特伍德.** 是啊，可再没别的了？

**贝蒂.** 没了，先生。

**威特伍德.** 这就狠了，这就太狠了。——送信的！一头骡子，一头驮货的畜生，他捎来我那傻瓜兄弟的一封信，沉得像安魂布道里的颂词，又像诗人写给诗人的捧场诗。更糟的是，这信预兆作者必至，同卷首献函一样灵验。

===Original===
Mirabell
A fool, and your brother, Witwoud!
Witwoud
Aye, aye, my half-brother. My half-brother he is, no nearer, upon honour.
Mirabell
Then ’tis possible he may be but half a fool.
Witwoud
Good, good, Mirabell, *le drôle*! Good, good, hang him, don’t let’s talk of him.﻿—Fainall, how does your lady? Gad, I say anything in the world to get this fellow out of my head. I beg pardon that I should ask a man of pleasure and the town a question at once so foreign and domestic. But I talk like an old maid at a marriage, I don’t know what I say: but she’s the best woman in the world.
Fainall
’Tis well you don’t know what you say, or else your commendation would go near to make me either vain or jealous.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 又是傻瓜，又是你兄弟，威特伍德！

**威特伍德.** 是啊是啊，我那同父异母的兄弟。同父异母的兄弟，再近一分都不是，我以名誉担保。

**米兰芒特.** 那么他兴许只是半个傻瓜。

**威特伍德.** 妙，妙，米兰芒特，好个促狭鬼！妙，妙，去他的，咱们别提他。——费诺，尊夫人安好？老天，为了把这家伙从脑子里撵出去，我什么话都说得出口。恕我失仪，竟拿这么一句既涉外又涉内的话，去问一位风月场上的老手。瞧我这话赶话的，活像婚礼上的老处女，不知自己嘴里冒的什么——可尊夫人是天底下头一号的好人。

**费诺.** 幸亏你不知自己嘴里冒的什么，不然你这一夸，险些要夸得我或者虚荣、或者吃醋。

===Original===
Witwoud
No man in town lives well with a wife but Fainall.﻿—Your judgment, Mirabell.
Mirabell
You had better step and ask his wife, if you would be credibly informed.
Witwoud
Mirabell!
Mirabell
Aye.
Witwoud
My dear, I ask ten thousand pardons﻿—gad, I have forgot what I was going to say to you.
Mirabell
I thank you heartily, heartily.
Witwoud
No, but prithee excuse me:﻿—my memory is such a memory.
Mirabell
Have a care of such apologies, Witwoud; for I never knew a fool but he affected to complain either of the spleen or his memory.
Fainall

===Chinese===
**威特伍德.** 满城里同太太过得和睦的，除了费诺别无二人。——请米兰芒特下断语。

**米兰芒特.** 想要个可靠的说法，你不如移步去问他太太。

**威特伍德.** 米兰芒特！

**米兰芒特.** 嗯。

**威特伍德.** 亲爱的，千万恕罪——老天，我把要跟你说的事忘了。

**米兰芒特.** 那我可感激不尽，感激不尽。

**威特伍德.** 不不，请你海涵：——我这条记性，真是好一条记性。

**米兰芒特.** 这类道歉可要少来，威特伍德；据我所知，天下的傻瓜无不惯于诉苦，不是怨忧郁症，就是怨记性糟。

===Original===
What have you done with Petulant?
Witwoud
He’s reckoning his money﻿—my money it was﻿—I have no luck today.
Fainall
You may allow him to win of you at play, for you are sure to be too hard for him at repartee: since you monopolise the wit that is between you, the fortune must be his of course.
Mirabell
I don’t find that Petulant confesses the superiority of wit to be your talent, Witwoud.
Witwoud
Come, come, you are malicious now, and would breed debates.﻿—Petulant’s my friend, and a very honest fellow, and a very pretty fellow, and has a smattering﻿—faith and troth, a pretty deal of an odd sort of a small wit: nay, I’ll do him justice. I’m his friend, I won’t wrong him neither.﻿—And if he had any judgment in the world, he would not be altogether contemptible. Come, come, don’t detract from the merits of my friend.
Fainall
You don’t take your friend to be over-nicely bred?
Witwoud
No, no, hang him, the rogue has no manners at all, that I must own; no more breeding than a bum-baily, that I grant you﻿—’tis pity; the fellow has fire and life.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 佩特兰特呢？

**威特伍德.** 他在数钱——原是我的钱——我今天手气背透了。

**费诺.** 牌桌上你尽可以让他赢，反正斗起嘴来你稳占他上风：你们俩合起来那点才思既归你独占，财运当然全归他。

**米兰芒特.** 我可没见佩特兰特承认，才高一筹是你的天赋，威特伍德。

**威特伍德.** 得了，得了，你这是存心不善，要挑起论战。——佩特兰特是我的朋友，顶老实的人，顶体面的人，还有那么一点——凭真心实意，有那么一小股别致的小聪明：不，我得秉公而论。我是他的朋友，不能亏待他。——他但凡世上有点见识，也就不至于全然上不得台面。得了，得了，别糟践我朋友的功德。

**费诺.** 你不觉得你这位朋友，教养上欠些琢磨？

**威特伍德.** 不不，去他的，这流氓半点礼数没有，这我认；教养比官差法警还欠一截，这我也认——可惜归可惜；这家伙有火气，有活气。

===Original===
Mirabell
What, courage?
Witwoud
Hum, faith, I don’t know as to that, I can’t say as to that. Yes, faith, in a controversy he’ll contradict anybody.
Mirabell
Though ’twere a man whom he feared or a woman whom he loved.
Witwoud
Well, well, he does not always think before he speaks. We have all our failings; you are too hard upon him, you are, faith. Let me excuse him﻿—I can defend most of his faults, except one or two; one he has, that’s the truth on’t; if he were my brother I could not acquit him﻿—that, indeed, I could wish were otherwise.
Mirabell
Aye, marry, what’s that, Witwoud?
Witwoud
Oh, pardon me!﻿—Expose the infirmities of my friend? No, my dear, excuse me there.
Fainall
What, I warrant he’s unsincere, or ’tis some such trifle.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 哦，有胆量？

**威特伍德.** 唔，这个嘛，说不好，真说不好。倒也是，真的，遇上抬杠，他谁都敢驳。

**米兰芒特.** 哪怕是他怕的男人，或是他爱的女人。

**威特伍德.** 话说回来，他也未必句句想好了才开口。人各有短；你对他太苛刻了，真是。让我替他分说——他的毛病我大半都辩护得下，除了一两条；有一条是千真万确的，他是我亲兄弟我也替他开脱不了——那一条，我真盼着不是那样。

**米兰芒特.** 哦，着啊，是哪一条，威特伍德？

**威特伍德.** 哦，恕不奉告！——抖落我朋友的隐疾？不，亲爱的，此处休提。

**费诺.** 怎么，我料着了：无非是他为人不实，之类的小事。

===Original===
Witwoud
No, no; what if he be? ’Tis no matter for that, his wit will excuse that. A wit should no more be sincere than a woman constant: one argues a decay of parts, as t’other of beauty.
Mirabell
Maybe you think him too positive?
Witwoud
No, no; his being positive is an incentive to argument, and keeps up conversation.
Fainall
Too illiterate?
Witwoud
That? That’s his happiness. His want of learning gives him the more opportunities to show his natural parts.
Mirabell
He wants words?
Witwoud
Aye; but I like him for that now: for his want of words gives me the pleasure very often to explain his meaning.
Fainall
He’s impudent?
Witwoud
No that’s not it.
Mirabell
Vain?
Witwoud
No.
Mirabell
What! He speaks unseasonable truths sometimes, because he has not wit enough to invent an evasion?
Witwoud
Truths? Ha, ha, ha! No, no, since you will have it﻿—I mean he never speaks truth at all﻿—that’s all. He will lie like a chambermaid, or a woman of quality’s porter. Now that is a fault.

===Chinese===
**威特伍德.** 不不；就算不实，又何妨？不打紧，他的才情自会代为开脱。才子本不该诚实，正如美人本不该贞专：前者表明才思衰退，后者表明颜色衰减。

**米兰芒特.** 兴许你觉得他太武断？

**威特伍德.** 不不；他一武断，正好激人抬杠，谈话才接得上气。

**费诺.** 太没学问？

**威特伍德.** 那个？那是他的福气。学问一缺，他天生的机锋反倒多了露脸的机会。

**米兰芒特.** 他词穷？

**威特伍德.** 是啊；如今我倒就爱他这一条：他词一穷，就常轮到我来代他阐明本意，其乐无穷。

**费诺.** 他脸皮厚？

**威特伍德.** 不，不是这条。

**米兰芒特.** 虚荣？

**威特伍德.** 不是。

**米兰芒特.** 难道是！他有时说出不合时宜的实话，只因才情不够，编不出遁词？

**威特伍德.** 实话？哈，哈，哈！不不，你非要问个水落石出——我是说他压根从来不说实话——就这么回事。他撒起谎来，赛过闺房侍女，赛过贵妇宅门的门房。这条才算毛病。

===Original===
*Enter **Coachman**.*
Coachman
Is Master Petulant here, mistress?
Betty
Yes.
Coachman
Three gentlewomen in a coach would speak with him.
Fainall
O brave Petulant! Three!
Betty
I’ll tell him.
Coachman
You must bring two dishes of chocolate and a glass of cinnamon water.18
*Exeunt **Betty** and **Coachman**.*

===Chinese===
*车夫上。*

**车夫.** 佩特兰特老爷在这儿吗，大姐？

**贝蒂.** 在。

**车夫.** 车里三位太太要同他讲话。

**费诺.** 好个神气的佩特兰特！一气三位！

**贝蒂.** 我去回他。

**车夫.** 还得端两碟巧克力、一杯桂皮水（18）。

*贝蒂与车夫下。*

===Original===
Witwoud
That should be for two fasting strumpets, and a bawd troubled with wind. Now you may know what the three are.
Mirabell
You are very free with your friend’s acquaintance.
Witwoud
Aye, aye, friendship without freedom is as dull as love without enjoyment or wine without toasting: but to tell you a secret, these are trulls whom he allows coach-hire, and something more by the week, to call on him once a day at public places.
Mirabell
How!
Witwoud
You shall see he won’t go to ’em because there’s no more company here to take notice of him. Why, this is nothing to what he used to do:﻿—before he found out this way, I have known him call for himself﻿—
Fainall
Call for himself? What dost thou mean?
Witwoud
Mean! Why he would slip you out of this chocolate-house,19 just when you had been talking to him. As soon as your back was turned﻿—whip he was gone; then trip to his lodging, clap on a hood and scarf and a mask, slap into a hackney-coach, and drive hither to the door again in a trice; where he would send in for himself; that I mean, call for himself, wait for himself, nay, and what’s more, not finding himself, sometimes leave a letter for himself.
Mirabell
I confess this is something extraordinary. I believe he waits for himself now, he is so long a coming; oh, I ask his pardon.

===Chinese===
**威特伍德.** 那该是给两个空着肚子的婊子，外加一位胀了气的鸨母预备的。这下你们可知道那三位是什么货色了。

**米兰芒特.** 你谈论起朋友的相好，可真够口无遮拦。

**威特伍德.** 是是，友谊而没有放肆，就同恋爱而不曾缠绵、喝酒而不行祝酒一样无味：可告诉你们一桩秘密，这几个娘们是他按周贴钱、连车马费一并开销的粉头，每天到公共场所去拜望他一回。

**米兰芒特.** 什么！

**威特伍德.** 你们瞧着吧，他偏不去，因为这屋里人不多，没人看他受用。嗐，比起他从前的作为，这还不算什么：——没悟出这条道儿之前，我见过他自己拜望自己——

**费诺.** 自己拜望自己？你这话怎么讲？

**威特伍德.** 怎么讲！他跟你正说着话呢，一转身就溜出这巧克力屋（19）。你前脚刚走——嗖，他没了；三步并作两步回寓所，兜帽、头巾、面罩往身上一披，噼啪钻进出租马车，眨眼间又折回这大门口；打发人进来请他自己；我说的就是：请自己，候自己，还不止——候不着自己的时候，有时还给自家留封信。

**米兰芒特.** 我承认这桩事确乎非比寻常。我猜他这会儿正候着自己大驾光临，要不怎么迟迟不来；哦，失敬，失敬。

===Original===
*Enter **Petulant** and **Betty**.*
Betty
Sir, the coach stays.
Petulant
Well, well, I come. ’Sbud, a man had as good be a professed midwife as a professed whoremaster, at this rate! To be knocked up and raised at all hours, and in all places. Pox on ’em, I won’t come.﻿—D’ye hear, tell ’em I won’t come.﻿—Let ’em snivel and cry their hearts out.
Fainall
You are very cruel, Petulant.
Petulant
All’s one, let it pass. I have a humour to be cruel.
Mirabell
I hope they are not persons of condition that you use at this rate.
Petulant
Condition? Condition’s a dried fig, if I am not in humour. By this hand, if they were your﻿—a﻿—a﻿—your what-d’ye-call-’ems themselves, they must wait or rub off, if I want appetite.
Mirabell
What-d’ye-call-’ems! What are they, Witwoud?
Witwoud
Empresses, my dear. By your what-d’ye-call-’ems he means sultana queens.
Petulant
Aye, Roxolanas.
Mirabell
Cry you mercy.

===Chinese===
*佩特兰特与贝蒂上。*

**贝蒂.** 先生，马车还候着。

**佩特兰特.** 就来，就来。老天爷，照这么个折腾法，做挂牌的嫖客还不如做挂牌的产婆！不分昼夜，不论地场，随叫随到。去她们的，我不去了。——听见没有，就说我不去了。——让她们抹眼泪去，哭穿了心才好。

**费诺.** 你也太狠了，佩特兰特。

**佩特兰特.** 无所谓，由它去。我这会儿正兴起要狠一狠。

**米兰芒特.** 但愿你这么打发的，不是有身份的人物。

**佩特兰特.** 身份？我兴致不来，身份就是颗干瘪无花果。凭这只手，她们就是你那位——那个——你的那个什么玩意儿御驾亲征，我胃口不到，她们也只有干候着，或者走人。

**米兰芒特.** 那个什么玩意儿！她们到底是什么人，威特伍德？

**威特伍德.** 女皇啊，亲爱的。他说的那个什么玩意儿，指的是苏丹后妃。

**佩特兰特.** 对，罗克塞拉娜一流。

**米兰芒特.** 失敬，失敬。

===Original===
Fainall
Witwoud says they are﻿—
Petulant
What does he say th’are?
Witwoud
I? Fine ladies, I say.
Petulant
Pass on, Witwoud. Harkee, by this light, his relations: two co-heiresses his cousins, and an old aunt, who loves caterwauling better than a conventicle.
Witwoud
Ha, ha, ha! I had a mind to see how the rogue would come off. Ha, ha, ha! Gad, I can’t be angry with him, if he had said they were my mother and my sisters.
Mirabell
No?
Witwoud
No; the rogue’s wit and readiness of invention charm me, dear Petulant.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 威特伍德说她们是——

**佩特兰特.** 他说她们是什么？

**威特伍德.** 我？我说的是娇滴滴的名媛。

**佩特兰特.** 少来这套，威特伍德。听着，指着这盏灯起誓：是他的亲戚——他两个表妹，共同继承人，还有一位老姨妈，爱夜猫子叫春胜过爱秘密布道会。

**威特伍德.** 哈，哈，哈！我原想瞧瞧这流氓怎么下台。哈，哈，哈！老天，哪怕他说她们是我娘和我姐妹，我也恼不起来。

**米兰芒特.** 恼不起来？

**威特伍德.** 恼不起来；这流氓的急智和现编现造的本事把我迷住了，亲爱的佩特兰特。

===Original===
Betty
They are gone, sir, in great anger.
Petulant
Enough, let ’em trundle. Anger helps complexion, saves paint.
Fainall
This continence is all dissembled; this is in order to have something to brag of the next time he makes court to Millamant, and swear he has abandoned the whole sex for her sake.
Mirabell
Have you not left off your impudent pretensions there yet? I shall cut your throat, sometime or other, Petulant, about that business.
Petulant
Aye, aye, let that pass. There are other throats to be cut.
Mirabell
Meaning mine, sir?
Petulant
Not I﻿—I mean nobody﻿—I know nothing. But there are uncles and nephews in the world﻿—and they may be rivals. What then? All’s one for that.

===Chinese===
**贝蒂.** 她们走了，先生，气得不轻。

**佩特兰特.** 很好，让她们滚吧。动气润气色，省胭脂。

**费诺.** 他这番节欲全是装的：为的是下回向米勒曼特献殷勤时，好有得吹嘘，赌咒说自己为了她，把整个女性都戒绝了。

**米兰芒特.** 你那副不要脸的非分之想，到她那儿还没收摊？早晚有那么一天，为着这桩事我要割你的喉咙，佩特兰特。

**佩特兰特.** 是，是，这事不提。要割的喉咙还有别的。

**米兰芒特.** 说的是我的喉咙吗，先生？

**佩特兰特.** 我没说——我没说谁——我什么都不知道。不过世上么，叔叔有的是，侄儿也有的是——他们兴许还是情敌。那又怎样？横竖一回事。

===Original===
Mirabell
How? Harkee, Petulant, come hither﻿—explain, or I shall call your interpreter.
Petulant
Explain? I know nothing. Why, you have an uncle, have you not, lately come to town, and lodges by my Lady Wishfort’s?
Mirabell
True.
Petulant
Why, that’s enough﻿—you and he are not friends; and if he should marry and have a child, yon may be disinherited, ha?
Mirabell
Where hast thou stumbled upon all this truth?
Petulant
All’s one for that; why, then, say I know something.
Mirabell
Come, thou art an honest fellow, Petulant, and shalt make love to my mistress, thou shalt, faith. What hast thou heard of my uncle?
Petulant
I? Nothing, I. If throats are to be cut, let swords clash. Snug’s the word; I shrug and am silent.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 什么？听着，佩特兰特，过来——讲明白，不然我可要给你请位通译。

**佩特兰特.** 讲明白？我什么都不知道。嗐，你有位叔叔，不是吗？新近进的城，下榻在威什福特夫人家左近？

**米兰芒特.** 不假。

**佩特兰特.** 这不就齐了——你们叔侄不睦；他若是娶妻生子，你那份继承权可就悬了，嗯？

**米兰芒特.** 这一大堆实情，你是打哪儿绊着的？

**佩特兰特.** 那也无关紧要；那么，就算我知道些风声吧。

===Original===
Mirabell
Oh, raillery, raillery! Come, I know thou art in the women’s secrets.﻿—What, you’re a cabalist; I know you stayed at Millamant’s last night after I went. Was there any mention made of my uncle or me? Tell me; if thou hadst but good nature equal to thy wit, Petulant, Tony Witwoud, who is now thy competitor in fame, would show as dim by thee as a dead whiting’s eye by a pearl of orient; he would no more be seen by thee than Mercury is by the sun: come, I’m sure thou wo’t tell me.20
Petulant
If I do, will you grant me common sense, then, for the future?
Mirabell
Faith, I’ll do what I can for thee, and I’ll pray that Heavan may grant it thee in the meantime.
Petulant
Well, hark’ee.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 得，你是个好汉子，佩特兰特，准你去追我的心上人，真格的。关于我叔叔，你都听了些什么？

**佩特兰特.** 我？什么也没听，我。真要割喉咙，让刀剑去碰头。稳妥第一；我一耸肩，缄口不言。

**米兰芒特.** 哦，打趣，打趣！得，我知道那些太太们的秘密里少不了你。——怎么，你是密社的一份子；我知道昨晚我走后你留在了米勒曼特那儿。可曾有人提起我叔叔，或者提起我？说吧；佩特兰特，你的好心肠若赶得上你的才情一半，如今同你齐名的托尼·威特伍德，摆在你旁边就要像上好明珠旁边一颗死牙鳕的眼睛（20），黯然失色；他之不能与你并观，一如水星之不能与太阳并观：来，我保准你肯告诉我。

**佩特兰特.** 我若说了，往后你可肯承认我有起码的常识？

**米兰芒特.** 实话实说，我尽力替你周旋；这期间，我且祷告上天，求他老人家先赏给你。

**佩特兰特.** 好吧，听着。

*米兰芒特与佩特兰特一旁密谈。*

===Original===
***Mirabell** and **Petulant** talk apart.*
Fainall
Petulant and you both will find Mirabell as warm a rival as a lover.
Witwoud
Pshaw, pshaw, that she laughs at Petulant is plain. And for my part, but that it is almost a fashion to admire her, I should﻿—hark’ee﻿—to tell you a secret, but let it go no further between friends, I shall never break my heart for her.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 你和佩特兰特都会发现，米兰芒特做起情敌来，同做起情人一样火辣。

**威特伍德.** 呸，呸，她拿佩特兰特打趣，这是明摆的。至于我么，若不是倾慕她几乎成了风气，我本该——听着——告诉你一桩秘密，朋友之间到此为止——我断不至于为她心碎。

**费诺.** 什么！

**威特伍德.** 她是标致；可她是个有些没准性的女人。

===Original===
Fainall
How!
Witwoud
She’s handsome; but she’s a sort of an uncertain woman.
Fainall
I thought you had died for her.
Witwoud
Umh﻿—no﻿—
Fainall
She has wit.
Witwoud
’Tis what she will hardly allow anybody else. Now, demme, I should hate that, if she were as handsome as Cleopatra. Mirabell is not so sure of her as he thinks for.
Fainall
Why do you think so?
Witwoud
We stayed pretty late there last night, and heard something of an uncle to Mirabell, who is lately come to town, and is between him and the best part of his estate. Mirabell and he are at some distance, as my Lady Wishfort has been told; and you know she hates Mirabell worse than a quaker hates a parrot,21 or than a fishmonger hates a hard frost.22 Whether this uncle has seen Mrs. Millamant or not, I cannot say; but there were items of such a treaty being in embryo; and if it should come to life, poor Mirabell would be in some sort unfortunately fobbed, i’faith.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 我还当你是要为她死的。

**威特伍德.** 唔——倒也不——

**费诺.** 她有才情。

**威特伍德.** 那是旁人休想从她那儿分去一星半点的。哼，见鬼，哪怕她标致如克娄巴特拉，我也受不得这个。米兰芒特对她的把握，没有他自己想的那么稳。

**费诺.** 何以见得？

**威特伍德.** 昨晚我们在那儿坐到很晚，听说米兰芒特有位叔叔新近进城，横在他同最要紧的产业之间。叔侄俩有些生分，这话已传到威什福特夫人耳朵里；你知道，她恨米兰芒特，赛过教友会信徒恨鹦鹉21，鱼贩子恨大霜冻（22）。这位叔叔见过米勒曼特小姐没有，我说不准；但风闻有这么一桩和议正在胎中；它若真呱呱坠地，可怜的米兰芒特可就要在某种意义上倒足大霉，真的。

===Original===
Fainall
’Tis impossible Millamant should hearken to it.
Witwoud
Faith, my dear, I can’t tell; she’s a woman and a kind of a humorist.
Mirabell
And this is the sum of what you could collect last night?
Petulant
The quintessence. Maybe Witwoud knows more; he stayed longer. Besides, they never mind him; they say anything before him.
Mirabell
I thought you had been the greatest favourite.
Petulant
Aye, tête-à-tête; but not in public, because I make remarks.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 米勒曼特断断听不进这桩事。

**威特伍德.** 说实话，亲爱的，难说；她是个女人，又是个任性使气的主儿。

**米兰芒特.** 这就是你们昨晚搜罗到的全部？

**佩特兰特.** 提炼出的精华。威特伍德兴许知道得多些；他待得久。再者，她们从不把他放在心上；当着他什么话都说。

**米兰芒特.** 我还当你是头一号的红人。

**佩特兰特.** 是啊，私底下；当众就不是了，因为我专出恶评。

===Original===
Mirabell
You do?
Petulant
Aye, aye, pox, I’m malicious, man. Now he’s soft, you know, they are not in awe of him﻿—the fellow’s well bred, he’s what you call a﻿—what d’ye-call-’em﻿—a fine gentleman, but he’s silly withal.
Mirabell
I thank you, I know as much as my curiosity requires. Fainall, are you for the Mall?23
Fainall
Aye, I’ll take a turn before dinner.
Witwoud
Aye, we’ll all walk in the park; the ladies talked of being there.
Mirabell
I thought you were obliged to watch for your brother Sir Wilfull’s arrival.
Witwoud
No, no, he comes to his aunt’s, my Lady Wishfort; pox on him, I shall be troubled with him too; what shall I do with the fool?

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 是吗？

**佩特兰特.** 是啊是啊，见鬼，我这人心地不善，伙计。他呢，性子绵软，你知道，她们不怕他——这家伙教养是好，是你们所谓的——那个什么来着——风雅绅士，可傻气又随身带着。

**米兰芒特.** 承情了，我的好奇心已经餍足。费诺，去林荫道（23）走走？

**费诺.** 好，饭前遛一圈。

**威特伍德.** 好，我们都上公园溜达去；太太们说好要去的。

**米兰芒特.** 我记得你还欠着差事，要守候你兄弟威尔福爵士到埠。

**威特伍德.** 不用，不用，他直奔他姨妈威什福特夫人家；去他的，回头少不得也来缠我；这个傻瓜可怎么打发？

===Original===
Petulant
Beg him for his estate, that I may beg you afterwards, and so have but one trouble with you both.
Witwoud
O rare Petulant! Thou art as quick as fire in a frosty morning; thou shalt to the Mall with us, and we’ll be very severe.
Petulant
Enough; I’m in a humour to be severe.
Mirabell
Are you? Pray then walk by yourselves. Let not us be accessory to your putting the ladies out of countenance with your senseless ribaldry, which you roar out aloud as often as they pass by you, and when you have made a handsome woman blush, then you think you have been severe.
Petulant
What, what! Then let ’em either show their innocence by not understanding what they hear, or else show their discretion by not hearing what they would not be thought to understand.
Mirabell
But hast not thou then sense enough to know that thou ought’st to be most ashamed thyself when thou hast put another out of countenance?
Petulant
Not I, by this hand﻿—I always take blushing either for a sign of guilt or ill-breeding.

===Chinese===
**佩特兰特.** 把他连人带产业一并讨了过来，回头我再把你讨过去，你们两个一总，我只消麻烦这一回。

**威特伍德.** 好个佩特兰特！你这嘴皮子，赛过霜晨的火，一点就着；跟我们去林荫道，咱们好好刻薄一场。

**佩特兰特.** 说定了；我这会儿正有刻薄的兴致。

**米兰芒特.** 是吗？那就请二位自便，单独走。休想拉我们同谋，看你们拿那些不知所云的浑话把太太们窘得下不来台——她们每打你们跟前过，你们就扯开嗓子吼上一句，吼得哪位标致太太红了脸，你们便自以为刻薄到家了。

**佩特兰特.** 什么话，什么话！那就请她们要么装听不懂，以示清白；要么压根不去听，好显乖觉，省得叫人当作听得懂。

**米兰芒特.** 可你自己就没长那个脑子，想一想：把别人窘得下不来台的时候，最该臊的是你自己？

**佩特兰特.** 我才不臊，凭这只手——脸红这东西，我素来不是看作心虚的凭证，就是看作没家教的凭证。

===Original===
Mirabell
I confess you ought to think so. You are in the right, that you may plead the error of your judgment in defence of your practice.
Where modesty’s ill manners, ’tis but fit
That impudence and malice pass for wit.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 我承认你有权这么想。你对就对在：往后大可以拿判断失误，替你的行径辩护——
既然羞怯算作失礼，  
合该放肆与恶毒冒充才情。

*同下。*

## Act II / 第二幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
St. James’s Park.

***Mrs. Fainall** and **Mrs. Marwood**.*

===Chinese===
圣詹姆斯公园（St. James’s Park）。

*（**费诺夫人（Mrs. Fainall）**与**玛伍德夫人**上。）*

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Aye, aye, dear Marwood, if we will be happy, we must find the means in ourselves, and among ourselves. Men are ever in extremes; either doting or averse. While they are lovers, if they have fire and sense, their jealousies are insupportable: and when they cease to love (we ought to think at least) they loathe, they look upon us with horror and distaste, they meet us like the ghosts of what we were, and as from such, fly from us.
Mrs. Marwood
True, ’tis an unhappy circumstance of life, that love should ever die before us; and that the man so often should outlive the lover. But say what you will, ’tis better to be left than never to have been loved. To pass our youth in dull indifference, to refuse the sweets of life because they once must leave us, is as preposterous as to wish to have been born old, because we one day must be old. For my part, my youth may wear and waste, but it shall never rust in my possession.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 是啊，是啊，亲爱的玛伍德，我们女人若要幸福，法门只得在自身之中、在彼此之间去找。男人行事从来趋于极端：不痴迷，便厌弃。做情人那阵，他们但凡有火气、有头脑，那股醋劲便叫人消受不了；而一旦情爱消歇（至少我们该作此想），他们便转生嫌恶，望向我们时满眼的惊骇与腻烦，同我们打照面，就像撞见了自己从前的亡魂——既然撞的是鬼，见我们便逃。

**玛伍德夫人.** 不错，人生有一桩不幸的境遇：爱情总要死在我们前头，而做男人的，又常常比做情人的活得长久。可随你怎么说，被人撇下，总强过从未被人爱过。为了甜头终有离时，便拒绝人生的甜、把青春消磨在死水般的漠然里，就同因为迟早要老、便巴望一落生就是老头一样荒悖。至于我，我的青春尽可以磨损、耗损，却断不肯搁在我手里生锈。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Then it seems you dissemble an aversion to mankind only in compliance to my mother’s humour.
Mrs. Marwood
Certainly. To be free, I have no taste of those insipid dry discourses with which our sex of force must entertain themselves apart from men. We may affect endearments to each other, profess eternal friendships, and seem to dote like lovers; but ’tis not in our natures long to persevere. Love will resume his empire in our breasts, and every heart, or soon or late, receive and readmit him as its lawful tyrant.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 这么说来，你那份厌弃男人的名头，不过是顺着我母亲的脾气装出来的。

**玛伍德夫人.** 正是。说句体己话，撇开男人，我们女人家凑作一堆，只好拿那些寡淡干燥的谈话自遣，这口味我素来没有。我们尽可以彼此扮出亲热、宣言地久天长的交情、装出情人的痴态；可要坚持长久，原非我们的天性。爱神总要重登我们胸中的帝位，每颗心或早或迟，都得把他当名正言顺的暴君接回来、再接回来。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Bless me, how have I been deceived! Why, you profess a libertine.
Mrs. Marwood
You see my friendship by my freedom. Come, be as sincere, acknowledge that your sentiments agree with mine.
Mrs. Fainall
Never!
Mrs. Marwood
You hate mankind?
Mrs. Fainall
Heartily, inveterately.
Mrs. Marwood
Your husband?
Mrs. Fainall
Most transcendently;24 aye, though I say it, meritoriously.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 天哪，我叫人瞒得好苦！嗬，你这可是自认浪荡子了。

**玛伍德夫人.** 我肯对你放言无忌，你便见出我的交情。来，你也爽快些，认了吧——你的心迹同我一致。

**费诺夫人.** 休想！

**玛伍德夫人.** 你恨男人？

**费诺夫人.** 恨之入骨，切齿不休。

**玛伍德夫人.** 恨你丈夫？

**费诺夫人.** 恨得超凡入圣（24）；嗬，这话出自我口——还恨得功德无量。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
Give me your hand upon it.
Mrs. Fainall
There.
Mrs. Marwood
I join with you; what I have said has been to try you.
Mrs. Fainall
Is it possible? Dost thou hate those vipers, men?
Mrs. Marwood
I have done hating ’em, and am now come to despise ’em; the next thing I have to do is eternally to forget ’em.
Mrs. Fainall
There spoke the spirit of an Amazon, a Penthesilea.25

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 击掌为凭。

**费诺夫人.** 给你。

**玛伍德夫人.** 我与你同心；方才那些话，不过试你一试。

**费诺夫人.** 竟有此事？你当真恨那群毒蛇——男人？

**玛伍德夫人.** 恨，我已经恨完了，如今进到鄙夷这一层；再往下一件，是把他们永远忘个干净。

**费诺夫人.** 这才是亚马逊女杰的魂灵、一位彭忒西勒亚（25）在开口。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
And yet I am thinking sometimes to carry my aversion further.
Mrs. Fainall
How?
Mrs. Marwood
Faith, by marrying; if I could but find one that loved me very well, and would be throughly sensible of ill usage, I think I should do myself the violence of undergoing the ceremony.
Mrs. Fainall
You would not make him a cuckold?
Mrs. Marwood
No; but I’d make him believe I did, and that’s as bad.

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 不过我有时还在想，要把这份厌弃再往前推一步。

**费诺夫人.** 怎么个推法？

**玛伍德夫人.** 说实话，靠嫁人。只消寻着一个爱我至深、又极能领会虐待滋味的人，我想我就舍得委屈自己，受一回那场仪式。

**费诺夫人.** 你不至于给他戴绿帽罢？

**玛伍德夫人.** 不至于；可我要叫他信我戴了——这同真戴一样狠。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Why had not you as good do it?
Mrs. Marwood
Oh, if he should ever discover it, he would then know the worst, and be out of his pain; but I would have him ever to continue upon the rack of fear and jealousy.
Mrs. Fainall
Ingenious mischief! Would thou wert married to Mirabell.
Mrs. Marwood
Would I were.
Mrs. Fainall
You change colour.
Mrs. Marwood
Because I hate him.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 那何不索性真做了？

**玛伍德夫人.** 嗬，他万一识破，倒摸着了底，从此不受煎熬；我偏要叫他永远吊在疑惧吃醋的刑架上受用。

**费诺夫人.** 好精巧的祸心！恨不得把你嫁的是米兰芒特。

**玛伍德夫人.** 恨不得呢。

**费诺夫人.** 你变了脸色。

**玛伍德夫人.** 因为我恨他。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
So do I; but I can hear him named. But what reason have you to hate him in particular?
Mrs. Marwood
I never loved him; he is, and always was, insufferably proud.
Mrs. Fainall
By the reason you give for your aversion, one would think it dissembled; for you have laid a fault to his charge, of which his enemies must acquit him.
Mrs. Marwood
Oh, then it seems you are one of his favourable enemies! Methinks you look a little pale, and now you flush again.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 我也恨；可他的名字我还听得进。你恨他却恨得格外，有什么缘故？

**玛伍德夫人.** 我从没爱过他；这人从前、如今，都骄傲得叫人受不住。

**费诺夫人.** 照你供出的这条恨由，倒叫人疑心这恨是装的；你安在他头上的这条罪名，连他的仇家也得替他开脱。

**玛伍德夫人.** 哦，这么说，你竟是他那些「手下留情的仇敌」里的一位了！我看你白了一白，这会儿又红上来。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Do I? I think I am a little sick o’ the sudden.
Mrs. Marwood
What ails you?
Mrs. Fainall
My husband. Don’t you see him? He turned short upon me unawares, and has almost overcome me.
*Enter **Fainall** and **Mirabell**.*
Mrs. Marwood
Ha, ha, ha! He comes opportunely for you.
Mrs. Fainall
For you, for he has brought Mirabell with him.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 有么？我想我是突然犯了点不适。

**玛伍德夫人.** 怎么了？

**费诺夫人.** 我丈夫来了。你没瞧见？他冷不丁转到我眼前，险些把我当场撂倒。

*（**费诺**与**米兰芒特**上。）*

**玛伍德夫人.** 哈，哈，哈！他来得正好——正冲着您来。

**费诺夫人.** 冲着您来——他可是把米兰芒特也一并领来了。

===Original===
Fainall
My dear!
Mrs. Fainall
My soul!
Fainall
You don’t look well today, child.
Mrs. Fainall
D’ye think so?
Mirabell
He is the only man that does, madam.
Mrs. Fainall
The only man that would tell me so at least, and the only man from whom I could hear it without mortification.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 我的心肝！

**费诺夫人.** 我的灵魂！

**费诺.** 你今天气色不佳，孩子。

**费诺夫人.** 你这样想？

**米兰芒特.** 这样想的男人，天下也就他一个，夫人。

**费诺夫人.** 至少，肯这么直说的只有他一个；而听着不伤体面的，也只有他一个。

===Original===
Fainall
Oh, my dear, I am satisfied of your tenderness; I know you cannot resent anything from me; especially what is an effect of my concern.
Mrs. Fainall
Mr. Mirabell, my mother interrupted you in a pleasant relation last night: I would fain hear it out.
Mirabell
The persons concerned in that affair have yet a tolerable reputation.—I am afraid Mr. Fainall will be censorious.
Mrs. Fainall
He has a humour more prevailing than his curiosity, and will willingly dispense with the hearing of one scandalous story, to avoid giving an occasion to make another by being seen to walk with his wife. This way, Mr. Mirabell, and I dare promise you will oblige us both.
*Exeunt **Mrs. Fainall** and **Mirabell**.*

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 哦，亲爱的，你的温存我心领了；我知道你断不会怪罪我什么，尤其怪不到我这一片关切上头。

**费诺夫人.** 米兰芒特先生，昨晚我母亲打断了您一段有趣的叙述：我倒很想听个下回分解。

**米兰芒特.** 那桩事里牵涉的几位，眼下名声还过得去。——只怕费诺先生要摆出苛察的面孔。

**费诺夫人.** 他有一样脾性，比好奇心更有势力：一桩风流丑闻，他宁可豁免了不听，也免得叫人瞧见他陪太太散步，又造出另一桩来。这边走，米兰芒特先生；我敢保您这一走，我们夫妻两个都承情。

*（**费诺夫人**与**米兰芒特**下。）*

===Original===
Fainall
Excellent creature! Well, sure, if I should live to be rid of my wife, I should be a miserable man.
Mrs. Marwood
Aye?
Fainall
For having only that one hope, the accomplishment of it of consequence must put an end to all my hopes, and what a wretch is he who must survive his hopes! Nothing remains when that day comes but to sit down and weep like Alexander when he wanted other worlds to conquer.
Mrs. Marwood
Will you not follow ’em?

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 好一个尤物！唉，说真的，我若熬到拔除了老婆那一天，只怕反倒要沦为悲惨之人。

**玛伍德夫人.** 哦？

**费诺.** 因为我统共只剩那一根指望；指望一偿，我全部的指望便随之出清。指望之后还活着的人，是何等可怜虫！到得那日，别无余事，只好坐下来嚎啕，像亚历山大大帝一般——再没有别的世界好征服了。

**玛伍德夫人.** 不跟了去？

===Original===
Fainall
Faith, I think not,
Mrs. Marwood
Pray let us; I have a reason.
Fainall
You are not jealous?
Mrs. Marwood
Of whom?
Fainall
Of Mirabell.
Mrs. Marwood
If I am, is it inconsistent with my love to you that I am tender of your honour?

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 实说，不想。

**玛伍德夫人.** 求你同去；我有缘故。

**费诺.** 你吃醋了？

**玛伍德夫人.** 吃谁的醋？

**费诺.** 吃米兰芒特的醋。

**玛伍德夫人.** 就算吃了，我爱你而不忍你的体面受损，这两件事难道相抵触？

===Original===
Fainall
You would intimate then, as if there were a fellow-feeling between my wife and him?
Mrs. Marwood
I think she does not hate him to that degree she would be thought.
Fainall
But he, I fear, is too insensible.
Mrs. Marwood
It may be you are deceived.
Fainall
It may be so. I do not now begin to apprehend it.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 这么说，你是在点我：我太太同他之间，有些灵犀暗通？

**玛伍德夫人.** 依我看，她恨他，恨不到她巴望人相信的那个份上。

**费诺.** 可他呢，只怕木然无觉。

**玛伍德夫人.** 说不定，是你受了蒙蔽。

**费诺.** 说不定。我如今可算开始省悟了。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
What?
Fainall
That I have been deceived, madam, and you are false.
Mrs. Marwood
That I am false? What mean you?
Fainall
To let you know I see through all your little arts.—Come, you both love him, and both have equally dissembled your aversion. Your mutual jealousies of one another have made you clash till you have both struck fire. I have seen the warm confession reddening on your cheeks, and sparkling from your eyes.

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 省悟什么？

**费诺.** 省悟我受了骗，夫人——而你，是假的。

**玛伍德夫人.** 我是假的？什么意思？

**费诺.** 意思是叫你知道：你那些小巧的伎俩，我都看穿了。——招了吧，你们两个都爱他，也都把厌弃装了个旗鼓相当。你们彼此斗醋，磕磕撞撞，竟撞出了火。那滚热的招供，我眼见它烧红你们的腮颊、从你们眼里迸出火星来。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
You do me wrong.
Fainall
I do not. ’Twas for my ease to oversee and wilfully neglect the gross advances made him by my wife, that by permitting her to be engaged, I might continue unsuspected in my pleasures, and take you oftener to my arms in full security. But could you think, because the nodding husband would not wake, that e’er the watchful lover slept?

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 你冤枉我。

**费诺.** 一点不冤。我太太对他抛去那份赤裸裸的殷勤，我乐得睁一只眼闭一只眼、故意不瞧——容她拴在那头，我在我的销魂账上便可永无嫌疑，好安安稳稳把你搂得更勤。只是你怎么想得到：打盹的丈夫不肯醒，警醒的情人倒睡得着了？

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
And wherewithal can you reproach me?
Fainall
With infidelity, with loving another, with love of Mirabell.
Mrs. Marwood
’Tis false. I challenge you to show an instance that can confirm your groundless accusation. I hate him.
Fainall
And wherefore do you hate him? He is insensible, and your resentment follows his neglect. An instance? The injuries you have done him are a proof: your interposing in his love. What cause had you to make discoveries of his pretended passion? To undeceive the credulous aunt, and be the officious obstacle of his match with Millamant?

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 那你又拿什么责难我？

**费诺.** 拿你的不忠，拿你另有所爱，拿你对米兰芒特的爱。

**玛伍德夫人.** 一派胡言。我倒要请你拿出实据，坐实这无根的指控。我恨他。

**费诺.** 那你又为何恨他？他木然无觉，你的怨望不过随着他的怠慢而来。要实据？你施与他的种种伤害便是明证：你在他的爱情里横插一手。你有什么来由，去戳穿他那份佯装的热情？去点破那位轻信的姨妈，甘当他和米勒曼特好事的殷勤绊脚石？

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
My obligations to my lady urged me: I had professed a friendship to her, and could not see her easy nature so abused by that dissembler.
Fainall
What, was it conscience then? Professed a friendship! Oh, the pious friendships of the female sex!
Mrs. Marwood
More tender, more sincere, and more enduring, than all the vain and empty vows of men, whether professing love to us or mutual faith to one another.
Fainall
Ha, ha, ha! yyu are my wife’s friend too.

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 是我对夫人的情分逼的：我向她宣言过交情，眼见她那副好性儿叫那骗子作践，不能不管。

**费诺.** 什么，原来出自良心？宣言过的交情！哦，女人家这份虔敬的友谊！

**玛伍德夫人.** 比男人一切虚空浮泛的誓言——无论向我们许的爱情，还是彼此许的信义——都更温存、更真诚、也更长久。

**费诺.** 哈，哈，哈！你也是我太太的朋友。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
Shame and ingratitude! Do you reproach me? You, you upbraid me? Have I been false to her, through strict fidelity to you, and sacrificed my friendship to keep my love inviolate? And have you the baseness to charge me with the guilt, unmindful of the merit? To you it should be meritorious that I have been vicious. And do you reflect that guilt upon me which should lie buried in your bosom?

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 无耻，负义！你竟责难我？你，你倒来数落我？我为了对你守死忠，才对她行了不义；我牺牲了友谊，为的是保住爱情的完璧——你竟下作到拿罪名扣我，全不想这一层功德？我在恶行里挣下的，于你原该记作功劳。那桩罪，你本该揣在自家怀里焐烂，如今倒反照到我头上？

===Original===
Fainall
You misinterpret my reproof. I meant but to remind you of the slight account you once could make of strictest ties when set in competition with your love to me.
Mrs. Marwood
’Tis false, you urged it with deliberate malice. ’Twas spoke in scorn, and I never will forgive it.
Fainall
Your guilt, not your resentment, begets your rage. If yet you loved, you could forgive a jealousy: but you are stung to find you are discovered.
Mrs. Marwood
It shall be all discovered. You too shall be discovered; be sure you shall. I can but be exposed. If I do it myself I shall prevent your baseness.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 你错解了我的责备。我不过提醒你一句：当初最紧的系绊，一旦同你对我的爱放在一处掂量，你是看得很轻的。

**玛伍德夫人.** 胡说，你是存着恶意、算准了戳出来的。那是讥诮之词，我到死也不饶。

**费诺.** 是你的罪，不是你的怨，养出你这团火。你若还爱，这点醋也担得起；你是叫「已然败露」蜇着了。

**玛伍德夫人.** 那就索性统统揭穿。你也逃不掉被揭穿；你只管等着。我横竖不过出丑，自家先抖出来，倒抢在你那下作前头。

===Original===
Fainall
Why, what will you do?
Mrs. Marwood
Disclose it to your wife; own what has past between us.
Fainall
Frenzy!
Mrs. Marwood
By all my wrongs I’ll do’t. I’ll publish to the world the injuries you have done me, both in my fame and fortune: with both I trusted you, you bankrupt in honour, as indigent of wealth.
Fainall
Your fame I have preserved. Your fortune has been bestowed as the prodigality of your love would have it, in pleasures which we both have shared. Yet, had not you been false I had e’er this repaid it.—’tis true—had you permitted Mirabell with Millamant to have stolen their marriage, my lady had been incensed beyond all means of reconcilement: Millamant had forfeited the moiety of her fortune, which then would have descended to my wife. And wherefore did I marry but to make lawful prize of a rich widow’s wealth, and squander it on love and you?
Mrs. Marwood
Deceit and frivolous pretence!
Fainall
Death, am I not married? What’s pretence? Am I not imprisoned, fettered? Have I not a wife? Nay, a wife that was a widow, a young widow, a handsome widow, and would be again a widow, but that I have a heart of proof, and something of a constitution to bustle through the ways of wedlock and this world. Will you yet be reconciled to truth and me?

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 哦，你要怎样？

**玛伍德夫人.** 抖给你太太；你我之间的旧账，一总认下。

**费诺.** 疯了！

**玛伍德夫人.** 凭我受的诸般冤屈，我非做不可。我要昭告天下，你在我的名誉和财产上欠下的伤：两样我都托付了你——你这么个荣誉上破了产的，同钱财上一样的一贫如洗。

**费诺.** 你的名誉，我保全完好。你的财产，是照你爱情那副挥霍性子花出去的，花在你我共享的那些快活上。可你若不曾变心，我原早偿还了。——不假——你若由着米兰芒特同米勒曼特把婚礼偷成了，老太太必定怒到再无转圜：米勒曼特就得被削夺一半身家，那一半届时便落到我太太名下。再问一句：我结婚图什么？不就为正大光明掳下一位阔寡妇的家财，再挥霍到爱情和你身上？

**玛伍德夫人.** 欺瞒，加空洞的托词！

**费诺.** 该死，我难道没结过婚？托的什么词？我难道不是身陷囹圄、戴着镣铐？我难道没有太太？非但有，还是寡妇改嫁的、年轻的寡妇、标致的寡妇——若非我这颗心是淬过火的，这条身板还经得起在婚姻和尘世两道上扑腾，她早又做寡妇了。这回，你可肯同真话、同我言归于好？

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
Impossible. Truth and you are inconsistent.—I hate you, and shall for ever.
Fainall
For loving you?
Mrs. Marwood
I loathe the name of love after such usage; and next to the guilt with which you would asperse me, I scorn you most. Farewell.
Fainall
Nay, we must not part thus.
Mrs. Marwood
Let me go.
Fainall
Come, I’m sorry.
Mrs. Marwood
I care not. Let me go. Break my hands, do—I’d leave ’em to get loose.

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 断无可能。真话同你势不两立。——我恨你，永生永世。

**费诺.** 就为了爱你？

**玛伍德夫人.** 受过这番作践，连「爱」这个字眼我都嫌恶；除了你要泼我的那身罪污，天下我最鄙夷的就是你。去了。

**费诺.** 嗳，咱们不能就这样散。

**玛伍德夫人.** 放手。

**费诺.** 来，我知错了。

**玛伍德夫人.** 我不管。放手。索性拗断我的手——断了手我也要挣脱。

===Original===
Fainall
I would not hurt you for the world. Have I no other hold to keep you here?
Mrs. Marwood
Well, I have deserved it all.
Fainall
You know I love you.
Mrs. Marwood
Poor dissembling! Oh, that—well, it is not yet—
Fainall
What? What is it not? What is it not yet? It is not yet too late—
Mrs. Marwood
No, it is not yet too late—I have that comfort.
Fainall
It is, to love another.
Mrs. Marwood
But not to loathe, detest, abhor mankind, myself, and the whole treacherous world.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 天塌下来我也不肯伤你。除了这双手，我就没别的法子留你么？

**玛伍德夫人.** 也罢，这都是我应得的。

**费诺.** 你知道我爱你。

**玛伍德夫人.** 可怜的虚情！哦，那个——罢了，如今还没到——

**费诺.** 什么？什么还没到？什么还没到？如今还没到太迟——

**玛伍德夫人.** 是，如今还没到太迟——这总算个安慰。

**费诺.** 迟的是，去另爱一个。

**玛伍德夫人.** 可去厌弃、憎恶、痛绝人类、痛绝我自己、痛绝这整个奸诈的世界，永不嫌迟。

===Original===
Fainall
Nay, this is extravagance. Come, I ask your pardon. No tears—I was to blame, I could not love you and be easy in my doubts. Pray forbear—I believe you; I’m convinced I’ve done you wrong; and any way, every way will make amends: I’ll hate my wife yet more, damn her, I’ll part with her, rob her of all she’s worth, and we’ll retire somewhere, anywhere, to another world; I’ll marry thee—be pacified.—’Sdeath, they come: hide your face, your tears. You have a mask,26 wear it a moment. This way, this way: be persuaded.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 嗳，这又走极端了。来，我赔个不是。别哭——是我不好；我爱你，便做不到揣着疑心高枕。求你忍一忍——我信你；我认了，是我冤了你；怎么都行，样样都依，我补过就是：我恨我太太，往后恨得更狠，该死的，我要甩了她，把她值钱的刮个精光，咱们寻个地方退隐——哪儿都成，另换一个世界；我娶你——消消气。——哎呀，他们来了：遮住你的脸、你的泪。你带着面罩（26）呢，且戴这一会儿。这边，这边：听话。

*（同下。）*

### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
The same.

***Mirabell** and **Mrs. Fainall**.*
Mrs. Fainall
They are here yet.
Mirabell
They are turning into the other walk.
Mrs. Fainall
While I only hated my husband, I could bear to see him; but since I have despised him, he’s too offensive.
Mirabell
Oh, you should hate with prudence.
Mrs. Fainall
Yes, for I have loved with indiscretion.

===Chinese===
同前。

*（**米兰芒特**与**费诺夫人**上。）*

**费诺夫人.** 他们还没走远。

**米兰芒特.** 他们拐进另一条小径了。

**费诺夫人.** 起初我只恨我丈夫，还见得下他这张脸；如今我鄙夷了他，他可就臭不可当。

**米兰芒特.** 哦，恨也要恨得有章法。

**费诺夫人.** 章法是该有——谁叫我从前爱得没了章法。

===Original===
Mirabell
You should have just so much disgust for your husband as may be sufficient to make you relish your lover.
Mrs. Fainall
You have been the cause that I have loved without bounds, and would you set limits to that aversion of which you have been the occasion? Why did you make me marry this man?
Mirabell
Why do we daily commit disagreeable and dangerous actions? To save that idol, reputation. If the familiarities of our loves had produced that consequence of which you were apprehensive, where could you have fixed a father’s name with credit but on a husband? I knew Fainall to be a man lavish of his morals, an interested and professing friend, a false and a designing lover, yet one whose wit and outward fair behaviour have gained a reputation with the town, enough to make that woman stand excused who has suffered herself to be won by his addresses. A better man ought not to have been sacrificed to the occasion; a worse had not answered to the purpose. When you are weary of him you know your remedy.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 对丈夫，你该存的嫌恶，够给情人提味，便恰到好处。

**费诺夫人.** 害我爱得没了边的原是你；如今这恨也由你而起，你倒要来给它划界？当初你为什么叫我嫁这个男人？

**米兰芒特.** 我们天天干些又败兴又危险的事，图的是什么？图的是保住名誉那尊偶像。你我情爱的亲昵，若真结出你当初忧惧的那个果子，除了一顶丈夫的名义，还有什么地方安得下一个父亲的名分而不失体面？费诺这人，我早摸透了：德行上挥霍，交情里算计，满口应承的朋友，假意设谋的情人；偏偏他的才情和一表的好举止，在城里挣下了一份名声，足够叫一个被他追求到手的女人脸面无伤。再好些的男人，犯不着为这场合牺牲；再差些的，又顶不了这个用场。哪天你厌了他，药方你是现成的。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
I ought to stand in some degree of credit with you, Mirabell.
Mirabell
In justice to you, I have made you privy to my whole design, and put it in your power to ruin or advance my fortune.
Mrs. Fainall
Whom have you instructed to represent your pretended uncle?
Mirabell
Waitwell, my servant.
Mrs. Fainall
He is an humble servant to Foible, my mother’s woman, and may win her to your interest.
Mirabell
Care is taken for that—she is won and worn by this time. They were married this morning.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 在你这里，我总该有几分信用罢，米兰芒特。

**米兰芒特.** 正为对得起你，我把全盘设计都让你与闻，把毁掉或成全我前程的权柄，也交到了你手里。

**费诺夫人.** 你派了谁来扮你那位凭空捏造的叔叔？

**米兰芒特.** 我的仆人韦特韦尔。

**费诺夫人.** 他正巴结着我母亲的贴身女仆福伊布尔（Foible），说不定能把她拉进你的阵营。

**米兰芒特.** 这一层早有了着落——到这会儿，人既赢得手，衣也穿上身了。今儿早上，他们成了亲。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Who?
Mirabell
Waitwell and Foible. I would not tempt my servant to betray me by trusting him too far. If your mother, in hopes to ruin me, should consent to marry my pretended uncle, he might, like Mosca in *The Fox*, stand upon terms;27 so I made him sure beforehand.
Mrs. Fainall
So, if my poor mother is caught in a contract, you will discover the imposture betimes, and release her by producing a certificate of her gallant’s former marriage.
Mirabell
Yes, upon condition that she consent to my marriage with her niece, and surrender the moiety of her fortune in her possession.
Mrs. Fainall
She talked last night of endeavouring at a match between Millamant and your uncle.
Mirabell
That was by Foible’s direction and my instruction, that she might seem to carry it more privately.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 谁？

**米兰芒特.** 韦特韦尔和福伊布尔。我不肯把仆人信托过了头，反倒引诱他背叛我。你母亲若存心毁我，当真许婚给我那位假叔叔，他保不齐学《狐狸》（The Fox）里莫斯卡（Mosca）的样，待价而沽、讲起条件来（27）；所以我先下手，把他钉牢在先。

**费诺夫人.** 这么着，我那可怜的母亲一旦套进婚约，你便及时揭穿这场骗局，亮出她那位情郎先前已成亲的婚书，好放她一条生路。

**米兰芒特.** 正是——条件是她点头应下我同她外甥女的婚事，并把她攥在手里的那一半财产拱手交出。

**费诺夫人.** 昨晚她还念叨，要张罗着撮合米勒曼特同你那位叔叔。

**米兰芒特.** 那是福伊布尔引的头、我授的意，好教她办起来，像是自家的悄悄主张。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Well, I have an opinion of your success, for I believe my lady will do anything to get an husband; and when she has this, which you have provided for her, I suppose she will submit to anything to get rid of him.
Mirabell
Yes, I think the good lady would marry anything that resembled a man, though ’twere no more than what a butler could pinch out of a napkin.
Mrs. Fainall
Female frailty! We must all come to it, if we live to be old, and feel the craving of a false appetite when the true is decayed.
Mirabell
An old woman’s appetite is depraved like that of a girl—’tis the greensickness of a second childhood, and, like the faint offer of a latter spring, serves but to usher in the fall, and withers in an affected bloom.
Mrs. Fainall
Here’s your mistress.
*Enter **Mrs. Millamant**, **Witwoud** and **Mincing**.*

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 嗯，我看你必成：我信老太太为了弄到一个丈夫，什么都肯干；等你替她备下的这一位到手，我料她为了脱手，也会什么都肯干。

**米兰芒特.** 正是。这位好夫人哪，只要有个男人的影儿就肯嫁——哪怕那影儿，不过是管家用餐巾掐出来的一个。

**费诺夫人.** 女人的软弱！我们迟早都要落到这一步，只要活得够老：正胃口衰退了，偏又犯起假胃口的馋。

**米兰芒特.** 老妇人的胃口，败坏得同少女的一个样——那是第二童年里的萎黄病；又像一季迟来的春天，只为把秋日迎进场，在一身强撑的艳色里枯萎。

**费诺夫人.** 你的意中人来了。

*（**米勒曼特**、**威特伍德**与**明辛（Mincing）**上。）*

===Original===
Mirabell
Here she comes, i’faith, full sail, with her fan spread and streamers out, and a shoal of fools for tenders.—Ha, no, I cry her mercy.
Mrs. Fainall
I see but one poor empty sculler, and he tows her woman after him.
Mirabell
*To **Mrs. Millamant**.* You seem to be unattended, madam. You used to have the *beau monde*28 throng after you, and a flock of gay fine perukes hovering round you.
Witwoud
Like moths about a candle. I had like to have lost my comparison for want of breath.
Mrs. Millamant
Oh, I have denied myself airs today. I have walked as fast through the crowd.
Witwoud
As a favourite just disgraced, and with as few followers.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 可不是她，千真万确，满帆驾到：扇子张开，彩旗全挂，还有一整群傻瓜充作随航的小艇。——哈，不，我向她告罪。

**费诺夫人.** 我只瞧见一条寒酸的空舢板，船尾还拖着她的女仆。

**米兰芒特.**（向**米勒曼特**）夫人今日，似乎无人随侍。往日您身后总挤满了时髦上流圈（beau monde）（28）的众生，一圈花花绿绿的漂亮假发绕着您盘旋。

**威特伍德.** 像扑蜡烛的飞蛾。我这条妙喻险些儿丢了——全怪赶路赶得上气不接下气。

**米勒曼特.** 哦，我今儿克扣了自己的排场，打人堆里穿过来，走得飞快。

**威特伍德.** 快得像刚失宠的佞臣，随从也一样寥寥。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Dear Mr. Witwoud, truce with your similitudes, for I am as sick of ’em—
Witwoud
As a physician of a good air. I cannot help it, madam, though ’tis against myself.
Mrs. Millamant
Yet again! Mincing, stand between me and his wit.
Witwoud
Do, Mrs. Mincing, like a screen before a great fire. I confess I do blaze today; I am too bright.
Mrs. Fainall
But, dear Millamant, why were you so long?

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 亲爱的威特伍德先生，你那些比方请休兵罢，我腻它们，腻得就像——

**威特伍德.** 像好天气里的郎中腻病人。没法子，夫人，虽说这一比是搬石头砸自己。

**米勒曼特.** 又来了！明辛，站到我前头，替我挡一挡他的才情。

**威特伍德.** 明辛尽管站，像大火炉前一扇屏风。我认了，我今儿确实熊熊放光；亮得扎眼。

**费诺夫人.** 可是，亲爱的米勒曼特，你怎么耽搁这么久？

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Long! Lord, have I not made violent haste? I have asked every living thing I met for you; I have enquired after you, as after a new fashion.
Witwoud
Madam, truce with your similitudes.—No, you met her husband, and did not ask him for her.
Mirabell
By your leave, Witwoud, that were like enquiring after an old fashion to ask a husband for his wife.
Witwoud
Hum, a hit, a hit, a palpable hit! I confess it.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 久！天哪，我何尝不是拼命赶来的？一路逢着活物，便向它打听你；我寻你，寻得像打听一款新时样。

**威特伍德.** 夫人，您的那些比方也请休兵。——不对：您方才迎面遇上她的丈夫，倒没向他打听她。

**米兰芒特.** 恕我插一句，威特伍德，向做丈夫的打听太太，那就好比打听一款过了气的旧时样。

**威特伍德.** 唔，中了一箭，中了一箭，明明白白中了一箭！我认账。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
You were dressed before I came abroad.
Mrs. Millamant
Aye, that’s true. Oh, but then I had—Mincing, what had I? Why was I so long?
Mincing
O mem, your la’ship stayed to peruse a packet of letters.
Mrs. Millamant
Oh, aye, letters—I had letters—I am persecuted with letters—I hate letters. Nobody knows how to write letters; and yet one has ’em, one does not know why. They serve one to pin up one’s hair.
Witwoud
Is that the way? Pray, madam, do you pin up your hair with all your letters? I find I must keep copies.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 我出门之前，你就装扮停当了。

**米勒曼特.** 嗯，这倒不假。哦，可后来我又——明辛，我又怎么了？我为什么耽搁那么久？

**明辛.** 哎哟小姐，您停下来，捧读了一包信来着。

**米勒曼特.** 哦，对了，信——我收了一堆信——我叫信缠磨死了——我恨信。天底下没人会写信；可偏有人给你写，不知图什么。这东西的用处，是拿来别头发。

**威特伍德.** 原来是这个用法？请问夫人，您是拿所有的信，往头发上别么？看来我得留底稿了。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Only with those in verse, Mr. Witwoud. I never pin up my hair with prose—I think I tried once, Mincing.
Mincing
O mem, I shall never forget it.
Mrs. Millamant
Aye, poor Mincing tift and tift29 all the morning.
Mincing
Till I had the cramp in my fingers, I’ll vow, mem. And all to no purpose. But when your la’ship pins it up with poetry, it fits so pleasant the next day as anything, and is so pure and so crips.
Witwoud
Indeed, so crips?
Mincing
You’re such a critic, Mr. Witwoud.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 光拿韵文的，威特伍德先生。散文的，我从来不拿来别头发——我记得试过一回，明辛。

**明辛.** 哎哟小姐，我一辈子忘不了。

**米勒曼特.** 是啊，可怜的明辛，一早上嘟着嘴直咕哝（29）。

**明辛.** 直到我手指头都抽了筋，真的，小姐。可全不顶事。您哪，可您拿诗把头发别起来，第二天那头发伏帖得就跟什么似的，又那么爽净，那么卷翘。

**威特伍德.** 哦，还卷翘？

**明辛.** 您可真会挑字眼儿，威特伍德先生。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Mirabell, did you take exceptions last night? Oh, aye, and went away.—Now I think on’t I’m angry—no, now I think on’t I’m pleased—for I believe I gave you some pain.
Mirabell
Does that please you?
Mrs. Millamant
Infinitely; I love to give pain.
Mirabell
You would affect a cruelty which is not in your nature; your true vanity is in the power of pleasing.
Mrs. Millamant
Oh, I ask your pardon for that. One’s cruelty is one’s power, and when one parts with one’s cruelty one parts with one’s power, and when one has parted with that, I fancy one’s old and ugly.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 米兰芒特，昨晚是你见怪了？哦，对了，还拔脚走了。——这会儿想起来，我生气了——不，这会儿想起来，我又高兴了——因为我想，我让你吃了点苦头。

**米兰芒特.** 那也值得高兴？

**米勒曼特.** 高兴到天上去；我就爱叫人吃苦头。

**米兰芒特.** 你这是装出一份天性里没有的狠心；你真正的虚荣，在讨人欢喜的本事。

**米勒曼特.** 哦，这一层恕我不领教。狠心就是权柄；狠心一撒手，权柄就撒手；撒了手之后，依我想，人就老了、丑了。

===Original===
Mirabell
Aye, aye; suffer your cruelty to ruin the object of your power, to destroy your lover—and then how vain, how lost a thing you’ll be! Nay, ’tis true; you are no longer handsome when you’ve lost your lover: your beauty dies upon the instant. For beauty is the lover’s gift; ’tis he bestows your charms—your glass is all a cheat. The ugly and the old, whom the looking-glass mortifies, yet after commendation can be flattered by it, and discover beauties in it: for that reflects our praises rather than your face.
Mrs. Millamant
Oh, the vanity of these men!—Fainall, d’ye hear him? If they did not commend us, we were not handsome! Now you must know they could not commend one if one was not handsome. Beauty the lover’s gift! Lord, what is a lover, that it can give? Why, one makes lovers as fast as one pleases, and they live as long as one pleases, and they die as soon as one pleases; and then, if one pleases, one makes more.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 是啊，是啊；由着你的狠心，毁掉你权柄的靶子，毁掉你的情人——到那时，你是个多么虚、多么完了的东西！不，这是实话：失了情人，你便再不标致：你的美当场就死。因为美原是情人的馈赠；你的妩媚，是他封赏的——你那面镜子，是个彻头彻尾的骗局。丑的和老的，照镜子本来自惭，受了夸赞之后再照，倒能从镜子里受用，还照出美人来：因为镜子照出的与其说是你的脸，不如说是我们的颂词。

**米勒曼特.** 哦，这些男人的自负！——费诺，你听见没有？他们不夸咱们，咱们就不标致了！可你须知，咱们不标致，他们也没法夸。美是情人的馈赠！天哪，情人算个什么东西，也配馈赠？嗬，情人想要多少就造多少，想叫活多久就活多久，想叫几时死就几时死；死绝之后，高兴了再造一批。

===Original===
Witwoud
Very pretty. Why, you make no more of making of lovers, madam, than of making so many card-matches.
Mrs. Millamant
One no more owes one’s beauty to a lover than one’s wit to an echo. They can but reflect what we look and say; vain empty things if we are silent or unseen, and want a being.
Mirabell
Yet, to those two vain empty things, you owe two the greatest pleasures of your life.
Mrs. Millamant
How so?
Mirabell
To your lover you owe the pleasure of hearing yourselves praised, and to an echo the pleasure of hearing yourselves talk.
Witwoud
But I know a lady that loves talking so incessantly, she won’t give an echo fair play; she has that everlasting rotation of tongue that an echo must wait till she dies before it can catch her last words.

===Chinese===
**威特伍德.** 妙极了。嗬，夫人您造起情人来，竟同搓那么几根纸火媒一样，不当回事。

**米勒曼特.** 美貌何曾亏欠情人，正如才思何曾亏欠回声。它们不过回照我们的顾盼与言谈；我们一缄口、一藏形，它们便是虚空的废物，连个存在都够不上。

**米兰芒特.** 可你生平两桩顶大的欢愉，偏偏就欠着这两样虚空废物。

**米勒曼特.** 此话怎讲？

**米兰芒特.** 欠情人的，是听人夸你的乐子；欠回声的，是听你自己说话的乐子。

**威特伍德.** 可我认得一位太太，爱说话爱得没个停，连回声的公平机会都不给；她那条舌头转个不了，回声要接上她的临终遗言，非得先等她咽气不可。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Oh, fiction—Fainall, let us leave these men.
Mirabell
*Aside to **Mrs. Fainall**.* Draw off Witwoud.
Mrs. Fainall
Immediately;—I have a word or two for Mr. Witwoud.
*Exeunt **Mrs. Fainall** and **Witwoud**.*
Mirabell
I would beg a little private audience too.—You had the tyranny to deny me last night, though you knew I came to impart a secret to you that concerned my love.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 哦，瞎编的——费诺，咱们撇下这些男人走。

**米兰芒特.**（向**费诺夫人**旁白）把威特伍德引开。

**费诺夫人.** 这就去；——我正有两句话，要同威特伍德先生讲。

*（**费诺夫人**与**威特伍德**下。）*

**米兰芒特.** 我也求一次小小的私晤。——昨晚你竟忍心拒了我，明知我来，是要向你吐一桩关系着我爱情的机密。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
You saw I was engaged.
Mirabell
Unkind! You had the leisure to entertain a herd of fools: things who visit you from their excessive idleness, bestowing on your easiness that time which is the incumbrance of their lives. How can you find delight in such society? It is impossible they should admire you; they are not capable; or, if they were, it should be to you as a mortification: for, sure, to please a fool is some degree of folly.
Mrs. Millamant
I please myself: besides, sometimes to converse with fools is for my health.
Mirabell
Your health! Is there a worse disease than the conversation of fools?
Mrs. Millamant
Yes, the vapours; fools are physic for it, next to asafoetida.
Mirabell
You are not in a course of fools?30

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 你也瞧见，我脱不开身。

**米兰芒特.** 狠心！你倒有闲工夫应酬一群傻瓜：这路人闲得发慌才来拜访你，把他们人生里砸在手里的光阴，转手扔给你的好说话。这样的交际里有什么可乐？他们断不可能赏鉴你；没这份本事；就算有，于你也该是桩苦修：讨傻瓜的欢心，怎么说也有几分傻气。

**米勒曼特.** 我自讨欢喜罢了：再说，间或同傻瓜谈谈天，于我的健康有益。

**米兰芒特.** 健康！天下还有比傻瓜的谈话更凶的病么？

**米勒曼特.** 有，忧郁气；傻瓜便是它的药，药力仅次于阿魏胶。

**米兰芒特.** 你总不至于在按疗程服用傻瓜罢（30）？

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Mirabell, if you persist in this offensive freedom you’ll displease me. I think I must resolve after all not to have you; we shan’t agree.
Mirabell
Not in our physic, it may be.
Mrs. Millamant
And yet our distemper in all likelihood will be the same; for we shall be sick of one another. I shan’t endure to be reprimanded nor instructed; ’tis so dull to act always by advice, and so tedious to be told of one’s faults—I can’t bear it. Well, I won’t have you, Mirabell—I’m resolved—I think—you may go—ha, ha, ha! What would you give that you could help loving me?
Mirabell
I would give something that you did not know I could not help it.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 米兰芒特，你再这样放肆得犯嫌，我可要不痛快了。我想我到底还是得打定主意，不要你；咱们合不来。

**米兰芒特.** 药方上，许是合不来。

**米勒曼特.** 可咱们的病症，多半倒是同一个；因为咱们迟早要彼此生厌。我可受不了被人规训、被人开导；事事听嘱咐行事，多么无趣；被人数落毛病，多么腻烦——我受不住。得，我不要你，米兰芒特——说定了——我想是的——你可以走了——哈，哈，哈！你说，你肯出什么价，换得个「能不爱你」？

**米兰芒特.** 我倒肯出点价，买你不知道：我身不由己。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Come, don’t look grave then. Well, what do you say to me?
Mirabell
I say that a man may as soon make a friend by his wit, or a fortune by his honesty, as win a woman with plain-dealing and sincerity.
Mrs. Millamant
Sententious Mirabell!—Prithee don’t look with that violent and inflexible wise face, like Solomon at the dividing of the child in an old tapestry hanging!31
Mirabell
You are merry, madam, but I would persuade you for a moment to be serious.
Mrs. Millamant
What, with that face? No, if you keep your countenance, ’tis impossible I should hold mine. Well, after all, there is something very moving in a lovesick face. Ha, ha, ha! Well I won’t laugh; don’t be peevish. Heigho! Now I’ll be melancholy, as melancholy as a watch-light. Well, Mirabell, if ever you will win me, woo me now.—Nay, if you are so tedious, fare you well—I see they are walking away.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 来，那就别拉长脸。说罢，你对我怎么说？

**米兰芒特.** 我说：男人靠才思交到朋友、靠诚实挣得家业，同靠直来直去和一片真心赢得女人，是一样地难。

**米勒曼特.** 好一位满口格言的米兰芒特！——求你别摆出那副凶巴巴、僵死死的智者脸，活像旧挂毯上分婴孩的所罗门（31）！

**米兰芒特.** 您尽顾取乐，夫人；可我想劝您，正经那么一小会儿。

**米勒曼特.** 什么，就冲那张脸？不，你若绷得住，我断断绷不住我的。也罢，说到底，一张害相思的脸，总有几分动人处。哈，哈，哈！好了，我不笑了；别闹脾气。唉！这会儿我要忧郁了，忧郁得像一盏守夜灯。好了，米兰芒特，你若有朝一日要我，趁现在求我。——嗳，你要还这样磨蹭，就再会了——我瞧他们走远了。

===Original===
Mirabell
Can you not find in the variety of your disposition one moment—
Mrs. Millamant
To hear you tell me Foible’s married, and your plot like to speed—no.
Mirabell
But how you came to know it?
Mrs. Millamant
Without the help of the devil, you can’t imagine; unless she should tell me herself. Which of the two it may have been, I will leave you to consider; and when you have done thinking of that, think of me.
*Exit.*
Mirabell
I have something more.—Gone!—Think of you? To think of a whirlwind, though ’twere in a whirlwind, were a case of more steady contemplation, a very tranquillity of mind and mansion. A fellow that lives in a windmill has not a more whimsical dwelling than the heart of a man that is lodged in a woman. There is no point of the compass to which they cannot turn, and by which they are not turned, and by one as well as another; for motion, not method, is their occupation. To know this, and yet continue to be in love, is to be made wise from the dictates of reason, and yet persevere to play the fool by the force of instinct.—Oh, here come my pair of turtles. What, billing so sweetly? Is not Valentine’s day over with you yet?
*To him **Waitwell** and **Foible**.*

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 你那百变的性情里，就匀不出片刻——

**米勒曼特.** 匀出来听你讲「福伊布尔嫁了人、你的计策眼看要得手」么——匀不出。

**米兰芒特.** 可你是怎么知道的？

**米勒曼特.** 不借魔鬼的力，你自己想去；除非是她亲口告诉我。两样里是哪一样，留给你自个儿斟酌；斟酌完了，再想想我。

*（下。）*

**米兰芒特.** 我还有话。——走了！——想她？去想一场旋风，哪怕人就站在旋风当中，也比这情形更经得起凝思，简直是心神与居所的双重安谧。住在风磨里的家伙，那住处也赛不过安顿在女人心里的那颗男人心来得离奇。罗盘上没有一个方位，她们转不过去；也没有一个方位，不叫她们牵着转；而且转东转西，全凭一样容易——因为她们的营生是动，不是章法。明知如此却还要爱，等于被理性的敕令封作智者，又凭本能的蛮力，继续扮那傻子。——哦，我这对斑鸠来了。怎么，亲热得这样甜？你们俩的情人节还没过完么？

*（**韦特韦尔**与**福伊布尔**到他跟前。）*

===Original===
Mirabell
Sirrah, Waitwell, why, sure, you think you were married for your own recreation and not for my conveniency.
Waitwell
Your pardon, sir. With submission, we have indeed been solacing in lawful delights; but still with an eye to business, sir. I have instructed her as well as I could. If she can take your directions as readily as my instructions, sir, your affairs are in a prosperous way.
Mirabell
Give you joy, Mrs. Foible.
Foible
Oh ’las, sir, I’m so ashamed.—I’m afraid my lady has been in a thousand inquietudes for me. But I protest, sir, I made as much haste as I could.
Waitwell
That she did indeed, sir. It was my fault that she did not make more.
Mirabell
That I believe.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 小子，韦特韦尔，怎么，你八成以为成亲是为了你自个儿取乐，不是为我办事方便罢。

**韦特韦尔.** 您恕罪，先生。不敢欺瞒，我们确乎在合法的欢娱里温存了一番；可眼睛始终盯着正事，先生。我已尽我所能，把她调教了一遍。先生，她若能接您的指令，接得同接我的教导一样利索，您的事务便在兴旺道上了。

**米兰芒特.** 贺喜你，福伊布尔太太。

**福伊布尔.** 哎呀，先生，臊死我了。——只怕夫人为我，已经悬了一千回心。可我敢说，先生，我是掐着最快的脚程赶来的。

**韦特韦尔.** 千真万确，先生。没能更快，是我的不是。

**米兰芒特.** 这个我信。

===Original===
Foible
But I told my lady as you instructed me, sir, that I had a prospect of seeing Sir Rowland, your uncle, and that I would put her ladyship’s picture in my pocket to show him, which I’ll be sure to say has made him so enamoured of her beauty, that he burns with impatience to lie at her ladyship’s feet and worship the original.
Mirabell
Excellent Foible! Matrimony has made you eloquent in love.
Waitwell
I think she has profited, sir. I think so.
Foible
You have seen Madam Millamant, sir?
Mirabell
Yes.
Foible
I told her, sir, because I did not know that you might find an opportunity; she had so much company last night.
Mirabell
Your diligence will merit more. In the meantime—*Gives money.*
Foible
O dear sir, your humble servant!
Waitwell
Spouse—
Mirabell
Stand off, sir, not a penny. Go on and prosper, Foible. The lease shall be made good and the farm stocked, if we succeed.

===Chinese===
**福伊布尔.** 可我照您的吩咐回了夫人，先生：说我有望见到您的叔叔罗兰爵士（Sir Rowland），说我要把夫人的小像揣在兜里给他看；我保管还要添上一句——那小像已然叫他迷上了夫人的姿色，烧得他急不可耐，恨不能伏到夫人脚边，礼拜那幅原本。

**米兰芒特.** 好个伶俐的福伊布尔！婚姻叫你说起情话来，口舌生莲。

**韦特韦尔.** 我看她大有进益，先生。我看是。

**福伊布尔.** 您见过米勒曼特小姐了，先生？

**米兰芒特.** 见过了。

**福伊布尔.** 是我告诉她的，先生——昨儿晚上她那儿宾客太多，我怕您寻不着开口的机会。

**米兰芒特.** 你这份勤谨，日后另有重赏。且先——（给钱。）

**福伊布尔.** 哎呀，多谢先生，您太抬举了！

**韦特韦尔.** 娘子——

**米兰芒特.** 站开，先生，一个子儿也没有。接着办，办到底，福伊布尔。大事若成，地契照给，庄上也与你们置上牲口。

===Original===
Foible
I don’t question your generosity, sir, and you need not doubt of success. If you have no more commands, sir, I’ll be gone; I’m sure my lady is at her toilet, and can’t dress till I come. Oh dear, I’m sure that *Looking out.* was Mrs. Marwood that went by in a mask; if she has seen me with you I’m sure she’ll tell my lady. I’ll make haste home and prevent her. Your servant, Sir.—B’w’y,32 Waitwell.
*Exit.*
Waitwell
Sir Rowland, if you please. The jade’s so pert upon her preferment she forgets herself.
Mirabell
Come, sir, will you endeavour to forget yourself—and transform into Sir Rowland?
Waitwell
Why, sir, it will be impossible I should remember myself. Married, knighted, and attended all in one day! ’Tis enough to make any man forget himself. The difficulty will be how to recover my acquaintance and familiarity with my former self, and fall from my transformation to a reformation into Waitwell. Nay, I shan’t be quite the same Waitwell neither—for now I remember me, I’m married, and can’t be my own man again.
Aye, there’s my grief; that’s the sad change of life:
To lose my title, and yet keep my wife.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**福伊布尔.** 您的慷慨，我信得过，先生；您的成功，也错不了。您若没有别的吩咐，我就去了；夫人这会儿准在梳妆，不等我回去，穿戴不成。哎呀——（张望。）——方才蒙着面罩打过去的那位，准是玛伍德夫人；她若瞧见我同您在一处，保管要去回夫人。我得赶紧到家，抢到她前头。先生，告退了。——再会（32），韦特韦尔。

*（下。）*

**韦特韦尔.** 该称罗兰爵士，劳驾。这丫头得了提拔就轻狂，忘了自己的身份。

**米兰芒特.** 来吧，先生，你能不能也想法忘了你自己——变身成罗兰爵士？

**韦特韦尔.** 嗬，先生，再要记得我自己，那是万万不能了。成亲、封爵、外加仆从伺候，一天之内全齐！够叫随便哪个男人忘了自己。难的是往后：怎么同先前的自己重续旧交、恢复熟络，从这番化身跌落，改邪归正回韦特韦尔。不，回头我也做不回原先那个韦特韦尔了——这会儿我想起来了：我成了亲，做不回光棍儿了。

唉，我的伤心处正在此；这正是人生悲伤的转变：
爵位丢了，太太还在身边。

*（同下。）*

## Act III / 第三幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
A room in **Lady Wishfort’s** house.

***Lady Wishfort** at her toilet, **Peg** waiting.*

===Chinese===
威什福特夫人家中一室。

*（**威什福特夫人**正在梳妆，**佩格（Peg）**在旁伺候。）*

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Merciful! No news of Foible yet?
Peg
No, madam.
Lady Wishfort
I have no more patience.—If I have not fretted myself till I am pale again, there’s no veracity in me. Fetch me the red—the red, do you hear, sweetheart?—An errant ash colour, as I’m a person. Look you how this wench stirs! Why dost thou not fetch me a little red? Didst thou not hear me, Mopus?33

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 老天慈悲！福伊布尔还没个音信？

**佩格.** 没有，夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 我一点耐性也不剩了。——我若不是把自己烦得又白了脸，就算我这人没有一句实话。给我拿红——拿红的，听见没有，心肝？——瞧这一脸死灰，我以人格担保。瞧这丫头磨蹭的！怎么还不去给我拿点红来？没听见我说话么，木头脑袋（33）？

===Original===
Peg
The red ratafia, does your ladyship mean, or the cherry brandy?
Lady Wishfort
Ratafia, fool? No, fool. Not the ratafia, fool—grant me patience!—I mean the Spanish paper,34 idiot; complexion, darling. Paint, paint, paint, dost thou understand that, changeling, dangling thy hands like bobbins before thee? Why dost thou not stir, puppet? Thou wooden thing upon wires!
Peg
Lord, madam, your ladyship is so impatient!—I cannot come at the paint, madam: Mrs. Foible has locked it up, and carried the key with her.
Lady Wishfort
A pox take you both.—Fetch me the cherry brandy then.*Exit **Peg**.* I’m as pale and as faint, I look like Mrs. Qualmsick, the curate’s wife, that’s always breeding. Wench, come, come, wench, what art thou doing? Sipping? Tasting?—Save thee, dost thou not know the bottle?

===Chinese===
**佩格.** 夫人是说红的拉塔菲亚甜酒，还是樱桃白兰地？

**威什福特夫人.** 拉塔菲亚，傻瓜？不，傻瓜。不是拉塔菲亚，傻瓜——老天赐我耐心！——我说的是西班牙纸（34），蠢货；是脸色，宝贝。胭脂！胭脂！胭脂！这你懂不懂，个傻丫头，两只手吊在身前，活像两轴线？还不动弹，木偶？你这牵线的木头东西！

**佩格.** 天哪，夫人，您也太急了！——胭脂我够不着，夫人：福伊布尔把它锁起来了，钥匙随身带走了。

**威什福特夫人.** 叫瘟病把你们两个都拿了去。——那就给我拿樱桃白兰地来。（**佩格**下。）我白得发虚，活像副牧师太太阔姆赛克太太，那位常年有孕的。丫头，来呀，来呀，丫头，你磨蹭什么？咂一口？尝一口？——老天保佑你，你认不认得那只瓶子？

===Original===
*Reenter **Peg** with a bottle and china cup.*
Peg
Madam, I was looking for a cup.
Lady Wishfort
A cup, save thee, and what a cup hast thou brought! Dost thou take me for a fairy, to drink out of an acorn? Why didst thou not bring thy thimble? Hast thou ne’er a brass thimble clinking in thy pocket with a bit of nutmeg?35—I warrant thee. Come, fill, fill!—So—again.—*Knocking at the door.* See who that is.—Set down the bottle first!—here, here, under the table.—What, wouldst thou go with the bottle in thy hand like a tapster? As I’m a person, this wench has lived in an inn upon the road, before she came to me, like Maritornes the Asturian in Don Quixote.36—No Foible yet?
Peg
No, madam; Mrs. Marwood.
Lady Wishfort
Oh, Marwood: let her come in.—Come in, good Marwood.

===Chinese===
*（**佩格**持一瓶一盏瓷杯又上。）*

**佩格.** 夫人，我方才在寻一只杯子。

**威什福特夫人.** 杯子！老天保佑你，瞧你端来的是盏什么杯子！你当我是仙女，能就着橡子壳喝酒么？怎么不把你顶针捎来？你兜里就没有一只铜顶针，里头搁一小块肉豆蔻（35），叮叮当当？——我算准了你有。来，斟上，斟上！——好——再斟。——（叩门声。）瞧瞧是谁。——先把瓶子搁下！——这儿，这儿，桌子底下。——怎么，还要攥着瓶子去开门，像个酒馆跑堂的？我以人格担保，这丫头来我这里之前，准在大路上的客栈里待过，活脱《堂吉诃德》（36）里那位阿斯图里亚斯姑娘马利托内斯。——福伊布尔还没信儿？

**佩格.** 没有，夫人；是玛伍德夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 哦，玛伍德：请她进来。——进来吧，好玛伍德。

===Original===
*Enter **Mrs. Marwood**.*
Mrs. Marwood
I’m surprised to find your ladyship in deshabille at this time of day.
Lady Wishfort
Foible’s a lost thing; has been abroad since morning, and never heard of since.
Mrs. Marwood
I saw her but now, as I came masked through the park, in conference with Mirabell.
Lady Wishfort
With Mirabell!—You call my blood into my face with mentioning that traitor. She durst not have the confidence. I sent her to negotiate an affair, in which if I’m detected I’m undone. If that wheedling villain has wrought upon Foible to detect me, I’m ruined. O my dear friend, I’m a wretch of wretches if I’m detected.

===Chinese===
*（**玛伍德夫人**上。）*

**玛伍德夫人.** 这时辰见夫人还没穿戴齐整，好生意外。

**威什福特夫人.** 福伊布尔成了没影儿的东西：一早出去，至今音信全无。

**玛伍德夫人.** 方才我蒙着面罩穿过公园，还瞧见她，正同米兰芒特密谈。

**威什福特夫人.** 同米兰芒特！——你一提这奸贼，我的血就往脸上撞。她断不敢有这样的胆量。我派她去交涉一桩事，这桩事一旦叫人识破，我就完了。倘若那个油嘴的恶棍说动了福伊布尔来出首我，我就毁了。哦，亲爱的朋友，事情一败露，我就是可怜虫里头顶可怜的那一个。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
O madam, you cannot suspect Mrs. Foible’s integrity!
Lady Wishfort
Oh, he carries poison in his tongue that would corrupt integrity itself! If she has given him an opportunity, she has as good as put her integrity into his hands. Ah, dear Marwood, what’s integrity to an opportunity?—Hark! I hear her!—dear friend, retire into my closet, that I may examine her with more freedom—you’ll pardon me, dear friend, I can make bold with you—there are books over the chimney—Quarles and Prynne,37 and the *Short View of the Stage*,38 with Bunyan’s works to entertain you.—*To **Peg**.*—Go, you thing, and send her in.
*Exeunt **Mrs. Marwood** and **Peg**.*
*Enter **Foible**.*

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 哦夫人，您总不至于疑心福伊布尔的忠心！

**威什福特夫人.** 哦，他那条舌头上驮着毒，忠心本身也能叫他蚀化！她只要给了他一个机会，就等于把忠心捧进了他的手心。啊，亲爱的玛伍德，忠心算得了什么，敌得过机会么？——听！她回来了！——亲爱的朋友，先到我内室避一避，我好放开了盘问她——您别见怪，亲爱的朋友，我在您跟前放肆惯了——壁炉上头有书——夸尔斯和普林（37），《剧坛短评》（38），连班扬的著作，够您解闷的。——（对**佩格**。）——去，你这个东西，叫她进来。

*（**玛伍德夫人**与**佩格**下。）*

*（**福伊布尔**上。）*

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
O Foible, where hast thou been? What hast thou been doing?
Foible
Madam, I have seen the party.
Lady Wishfort
But what hast thou done?
Foible
Nay, ’tis your ladyship has done, and are to do; I have only promised. But a man so enamoured—so transported! Well, if worshipping of pictures be a sin—poor Sir Rowland, I say.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 哦，福伊布尔，你上哪儿去了？干什么去了？

**福伊布尔.** 夫人，那一位，我见着了。

**威什福特夫人.** 可你办成了什么？

**福伊布尔.** 嗳，办成的、要办的，全在夫人您身上；我不过替人应承了一桩。可那一位爱得那个样儿——神魂颠倒！唉，若说拜画像也算罪过——真难为了罗兰爵士，我是说，可怜他一片痴心。

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
