The miniature has been counted like. But hast thou not betrayed me, Foible? Hast thou not detected me to that faithless Mirabell? What hast thou to do with him in the park? Answer me, has he got nothing out of thee?
Foible
*Aside.* So, the devil has been beforehand with me; what shall I say?—*Aloud.*—Alas, madam, could I help it, if I met that confident thing? Was I in fault? If you had heard how he used me, and all upon your ladyship’s account, I’m sure you would not suspect my fidelity. Nay, if that had been the worst I could have borne: but he had a fling at your ladyship too, and then I could not hold; but, i’faith I gave him his own.
Lady Wishfort
Me? What did the filthy fellow say?
Foible
O madam, ’tis a shame to say what he said—with his taunts and his fleers, tossing up his nose. Humph! (says he) what, you are a hatching some plot (says he), you are so early abroad, or catering (says he), ferreting for some disbanded officer, I warrant.—Half pay is but thin subsistence (says he), well, what pension does your lady propose? Let me see, (says he), what, she must come down pretty deep now, she’s superannuated (says he) and—

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 那张袖珍小像，人家原也说画得像。可你没出卖我罢，福伊布尔？你没把我泄露给那个没良心的米兰芒特罢？你在公园里同他有什么好谈的？回话，他可从你嘴里套出什么去没有？

**福伊布尔.**（旁白）糟了，魔鬼抢在我前头了；这可怎么回话？——（高声）——唉呀夫人，我撞上那个放肆的东西，我有什么法子？难道是我的错？您若听见他怎么作践我——桩桩件件，还全冲着夫人您——我保准您就不会疑心我的忠心。嗳，若只冲着我来，我倒担得住；偏偏他又拿夫人您挖苦，那我就忍无可忍了；可说真的，我把他抢白了个够。

**威什福特夫人.** 冲我？那脏嘴里说了什么？

**福伊布尔.** 哦夫人，他那些话我说不出口——又讥又诮，鼻孔朝天。哼！（他说）怎么，你们在孵什么阴谋（他说），起这么个大早，要么就是办酒席（他说），像雪貂似的嗅退役军官，保管没错。——半饷可养不活人（他说），好，你们家太太打算出多少年俸？我瞧瞧，（他说），怎么，如今可得狠狠放血了，她都超龄了（他说）还有——

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Ods my life, I’ll have him—I’ll have him murdered. I’ll have him poisoned. Where does he eat?—I’ll marry a drawer to have him poisoned in his wine. I’ll send for Robin from Locket’s39 immediately.
Foible
Poison him? Poisoning’s too good for him. Starve him, madam, starve him; marry Sir Rowland, and get him disinherited. Oh, you would bless yourself to hear what he said!
Lady Wishfort
A villain! Superannuated!
Foible
Humph (says he), I hear you are laying designs against me too (says he) and Mrs. Millamant is to marry my uncle (he does not suspect a word of your ladyship); but (says he) I’ll fit you for that, I warrant you (says he) I’ll hamper you for that (says he); you and your old frippery too (says he); I’ll handle you—

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 我的命！我非——非叫人宰了他不可。我要叫他吃毒药。他在哪儿用饭？——我索性嫁个跑堂的，好往他酒里下毒。我这就打发人去洛克特馆（39），即刻把罗宾叫来。

**福伊布尔.** 毒死他？毒死他还便宜了他。饿死他，夫人，饿死他；嫁给罗兰爵士，叫他丢了继承权。哦，他那些话您要是听见了，保准念佛！

**威什福特夫人.** 恶棍！超龄！

**福伊布尔.** 哼（他说），我听说你们也在打我的算盘（他说），米勒曼特小姐要嫁我叔叔（您老这边，他一个字也没疑心）；不过（他说）这回我叫你们好看，保管没错（他说），我叫你们吃不了兜着走（他说）；连你那身老古董旧货也一块儿（他说）；我要好生收拾你——

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Audacious villain! Handle me? Would he durst?—Frippery! Old frippery! Was there ever such a foul-mouthed fellow? I’ll be married tomorrow, I’ll be contracted tonight.
Foible
The sooner the better, madam.
Lady Wishfort
Will Sir Rowland be here, say’st thou? When, Foible?
Foible
Incontinently, madam. No new sheriff’s wife expects the return of her husband after knighthood with that impatience in which Sir Rowland burns for the dear hour of kissing your ladyship’s hand after dinner.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 胆大包天的恶棍！收拾我？他敢！——旧货！老掉牙的旧货！世上可有过这张臭嘴？我明天就成婚，今晚就订婚约！

**福伊布尔.** 越快越好，夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 罗兰爵士会来，你说？几时，福伊布尔？

**福伊布尔.** 须臾便到，夫人。新任郡长太太巴望丈夫受了骑士封号荣归，那份心焦，也比不上罗兰爵士心里烧的那团火——他一心盼着饭后亲吻夫人玉手的那个良辰。

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Frippery! Superannuated frippery! I’ll frippery the villain; I’ll reduce him to frippery and rags, a tatterdemalion! I hope to see him hung with tatters, like a Long Lane penthouse,40 or a gibbet thief. A slander-mouthed railer! I warrant the spendthrift prodigal’s in debt as much as the million lottery,41 or the whole court upon a birthday.42 I’ll spoil his credit with his tailor. Yes, he shall have my niece with her fortune, he shall.
Foible
He! I hope to see him lodge in Ludgate first, and angle into Blackfriars for brass farthings with an old mitten.43
Lady Wishfort
Aye, dear Foible; thank thee for that, dear Foible. He has put me out of all patience. I shall never recompose my features to receive Sir Rowland with any economy of face. This wretch has fretted me that I am absolutely decayed. Look, Foible.
Foible
Your ladyship has frowned a little too rashly, indeed, madam. There are some cracks discernible in the white varnish.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 旧货！超龄的旧货！我定要叫这恶棍「旧货」个够；把他作践成旧货加破烂，一个褴褛鬼！我巴望看他浑身上下挂满破布条，活像朗巷（40）旧衣铺的雨棚，或是上过绞架的贼。一张血口喷人的东西！我拿得准，这挥霍的败家子欠的债，抵得上百万彩票（41），抵得上过生日（42）的整座宫廷。我要去坏他在裁缝跟前的信用。对，他就是要娶我外甥女、连人带财产一并娶去，娶定了。

**福伊布尔.** 他！我巴望先看他搬进路德门（Ludgate）监狱去蹲着，捏着一只旧手套（43），往黑衣修士区（Blackfriars）钓铜子儿去。

**威什福特夫人.** 是啊，亲爱的福伊布尔；难为你这话，亲爱的福伊布尔。他把我一肚子耐性全搅光了。我这副眉眼，再也没法省着使，去迎罗兰爵士了。这混蛋烦得我彻底垮了。瞧，福伊布尔。

**福伊布尔.** 夫人方才皱眉，皱得急了些，真的。那层白瓷漆上，已看得见几道裂纹。

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Let me see the glass.—Cracks, say’st thou? Why, I am arrantly flayed—I look like an old peeled wall. Thou must repair me, Foible, before Sir Rowland comes, or I shall never keep up to my picture.
Foible
I warrant you, madam, a little art once made your picture like you, and now a little of the same art must make you like your picture. Your picture must sit for you, madam.
Lady Wishfort
But art thou sure Sir Rowland will not fail to come? Or will a not fail when he does come? Will he be importunate, Foible, and push? For if he should not be importunate I shall never break decorums—I shall die with confusion if I am forced to advance—oh no, I can never advance—I shall swoon if he should expect advances. No, I hope Sir Rowland is better bred than to put a lady to the necessity of breaking her forms. I won’t be too coy neither—I won’t give him despair—but a little disdain is not amiss; a little scorn is alluring.
Foible
A little scorn becomes your ladyship.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 拿镜子我瞧。——裂纹，你说？嗐，我这算整张皮都剥了——瞧着活像一面剥了皮的老墙。你得把我修补修补，福伊布尔，趁罗兰爵士没来，不然我可撑不到我那张画像的水准了。

**福伊布尔.** 您放心，夫人，当年一星半点的手艺，把画像造得像您；如今同一门手艺稍微一施，就该把您造得像画像了。往后，是画像给夫人当模特儿了，夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 可你拿得稳罗兰爵士不会失约？他来了，又拿得稳不退缩？他会不会穷追猛赶，福伊布尔？他若不追，我可怎么破得了规矩——若逼得我自个儿先递话，我会羞死——哦不，我万万递不得——他若巴望我先递话，我就晕倒给他看。不，我指望罗兰爵士的教养，不至于逼得一位淑女自毁规矩。可我也不过分拿乔——不至于叫他绝望——不过一点薄嗔不为过；三分轻慢最撩人。

**福伊布尔.** 三分轻慢，正衬夫人。

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Yes, but tenderness becomes me best—a sort of a dyingness—you see that picture has a sort of a—ha, Foible! A swimmingness in the eyes—yes, I’ll look so—my niece affects it; but she wants features. Is Sir Rowland handsome? Let my toilet be removed—I’ll dress above. I’ll receive Sir Rowland here. Is he handsome? Don’t answer me. I won’t know; I’ll be surprised. I’ll be taken by surprise.
Foible
By storm, madam. Sir Rowland’s a brisk man.
Lady Wishfort
Is he? Oh, then, he’ll importune, if he’s a brisk man. I shall save decorums if Sir Rowland importunes. I have a mortal terror at the apprehension of offending against decorums. Oh, I’m glad he’s a brisk man. Let my things be removed, good Foible.
*Exit.*
*Enter **Mrs. Fainall**.*

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 是，可最衬我的还是温存——那么一种将晕未晕的娇怯——你瞧那画像就有那么一种——哈，福伊布尔！眼波里汪着水光——对，我就要那个眼神——我外甥女爱学这个；可惜她眉眼本钱不足。罗兰爵士俊不俊？把我这梳妆台搬开——我上楼更衣。我就在这屋里接罗兰爵士。他俊不俊？别答我。我不要知道；我要吃一惊。我要叫人打我个措手不及。

**福伊布尔.** 怕不是措手不及，是干脆攻陷，夫人。罗兰爵士是位麻利人。

**威什福特夫人.** 当真？哦，那他准会穷追了，既是位麻利人。罗兰爵士一穷追，我的规矩就有救了。我一想到触犯规矩，就怕得要死。哦，他是个麻利人，可叫我欢喜。把我的东西搬开，好福伊布尔。

*（下。）*

*（**费诺夫人**上。）*

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
O Foible, I have been in a fright, lest I should come too late. That devil, Marwood, saw you in the park with Mirabell, and I’m afraid will discover it to my lady.
Foible
Discover what, madam?
Mrs. Fainall
Nay, nay, put not on that strange face. I am privy to the whole design, and know that Waitwell, to whom thou wert this morning married, is to personate Mirabell’s uncle, and, as such winning my lady, to involve her in those difficulties from which Mirabell only must release her, by his making his conditions to have my cousin and her fortune left to her own disposal.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 哦，福伊布尔，我一路提着心，只怕来得太迟。那个魔鬼玛伍德，瞧见你们在公园里同米兰芒特在一处，我怕她要去老太太跟前告发。

**福伊布尔.** 告发什么，夫人？

**费诺夫人.** 得了，得了，别装出那副意外面孔。全盘设计我都与闻：今早你嫁的那位韦特韦尔，要扮米兰芒特的叔叔；骗得我母亲上了钩，陷进那一重重的窘境，再非米兰芒特解救不可——解救的条件，便是我表妹连人带财产，都得归她自主。

===Original===
Foible
O dear madam, I beg your pardon. It was not my confidence in your ladyship that was deficient; but I thought the former good correspondence between your ladyship and Mr. Mirabell might have hindered his communicating this secret.
Mrs. Fainall
Dear Foible, forget that.
Foible
O dear madam, Mr. Mirabell is such a sweet winning gentleman—but your ladyship is the pattern of generosity.—Sweet lady, to be so good! Mr. Mirabell cannot choose but be grateful. I find your ladyship has his heart still. Now, madam, I can safely tell your ladyship our success: Mrs. Marwood had told my lady, but I warrant I managed myself. I turned it all for the better. I told my lady that Mr. Mirabell railed at her. I laid horrid things to his charge, I’ll vow; and my lady is so incensed that she’ll be contracted to Sir Rowland tonight, she says; I warrant I worked her up that he may have her for asking for, as they say of a Welsh maidenhead.

===Chinese===
**福伊布尔.** 哎呀夫人，恕罪恕罪。倒不是信不过夫人；只是想，夫人同米兰芒特先生从前那一段交好，说不定反倒碍着他吐露这个秘密。

**费诺夫人.** 亲爱的福伊布尔，那一段不必再提。

**福伊布尔.** 哎呀夫人，米兰芒特先生是那样一位甜嘴蜜舌、逗人喜欢的君子——可夫人才是大方的典范。——好心的夫人，心眼儿这样好！米兰芒特先生不能不感恩。我看夫人的心，如今还攥在他手里。这下，夫人，我可以放心回禀我们的成功了：玛伍德夫人原已回了夫人，可亏得我左右周旋，全给扳成了好事。我告诉夫人，米兰芒特先生如何咒骂她。我往他头上堆的罪状，说出来吓人，真的；夫人气得说，今晚就要同罗兰爵士订婚约；我保管把她烧到火候，叫他开口一讨就到手——正如人家说威尔士人的贞操，问一声就给的。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
O rare Foible!
Foible
Madam, I beg your ladyship to acquaint Mr. Mirabell of his success. I would be seen as little as possible to speak to him—besides, I believe Madam Marwood watches me. She has a month’s mind;44 but I know Mr. Mirabell can’t abide her.—John!—*Calls.* remove my lady’s toilet.—Madam, your servant. My lady is so impatient, I fear she’ll come for me, if I stay.
Mrs. Fainall
I’ll go with you up the backstairs, lest I should meet her.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 好一个了不得的福伊布尔！

**福伊布尔.** 夫人，求您把这个成功，回禀米兰芒特先生。同他讲话，能少叫人瞧见就少瞧见——再说，我看玛伍德夫人在盯我的梢。她对那位朝思暮想（44）；可我知道米兰芒特先生消受不了她。——约翰！——（唤人。）把夫人的梳妆台搬开。——夫人，告退。夫人急成那样，我再待，只怕她要亲自来提人了。

**费诺夫人.** 我同你走后楼梯，免得撞见她。

*（同下。）*
### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
**Lady Wishfort’s** closet.

***Mrs. Marwood** alone.*
Mrs. Marwood
Indeed, Mrs. Engine, is it thus with you? Are you become a go-between of this importance? Yes, I shall watch you. Why this wench is the *passe-partout*,45 a very master-key to everybody’s strong box. My friend Fainall, have you carried it so swimmingly? I thought there was something in it; but it seems it’s over with you. Your loathing is not from a want of appetite then, but from a surfeit. Else you could never be so cool to fall from a principal to be an assistant, to procure for him! A pattern of generosity, that I confess. Well, Mr. Fainall, you have met with your match.—O man, man! Woman, woman! The devil’s an ass: if I were a painter, I would draw him like an idiot, a driveller with a bib and bells. Man should have his head and horns, and woman the rest of him. Poor, simple fiend!—“Madam Marwood has a month’s mind, but he can’t abide her.”—’Twere better for him you had not been his confessor in that affair, without you could have kept his counsel closer. I shall not prove another pattern of generosity; he has not obliged me to that with those excesses of himself, and now I’ll have none of him. Here comes the good lady, panting ripe, with a heart full of hope, and a head full of care, like any chemist upon the day of projection.46

===Chinese===
威什福特夫人的内室。

*（**玛伍德夫人**独自一人在场。）*

**玛伍德夫人.** 好啊，机关太太（Mrs. Engine），原来你混到这一步了？竟当上了这等要紧的牵线人？好，我要盯你的梢。这丫头，原来是把万能钥匙（passe-partout）（45），一把开各家保险柜的主钥匙。我的朋友费诺，你行事竟这样顺风顺水？我早疑心里头有名堂；看来你同她这段孽缘是了啦。你那份恶心，不是胃口欠佳，是吃撑了的厌食；不然你绝不会凉得这么快：从正主儿跌成帮闲，竟倒替他去拉起皮条来！好一位大方的楷模，我认了。罢了，费诺先生，你算遇上对手了。——哦，男人，男人！女人，女人！魔鬼其实是头蠢驴：我若是画师，定把他画成个白痴，一个叼着围嘴、挂着铃铛淌口水的傻子。该叫男人顶着脑袋和犄角，女人领走他剩下的部分。可怜又天真的小鬼！——「玛伍德夫人一片痴心，可他消受不了她。」——那桩事上，你本不该做他的听罪神父，除非你把他的机密锁得更严实。要我再来当一回大方楷模，没门；他既不曾把自己那样过量地豁出去，我如今一个子儿也不要他了。好夫人来了，喘匀了气：一颗心装满指望，一颗脑袋塞满盘算，活像个掐着点金投料时辰（46）的炼金术士。

===Original===
*To her **Lady Wishfort**.*
Lady Wishfort
O dear Marwood, what shall I say for this rude forgetfulness? But my dear friend is all goodness.
Mrs. Marwood
No apologies, dear madam. I have been very well entertained.
Lady Wishfort
As I’m a person, I am in a very chaos to think I should so forget myself. But I have such an olio of affairs, really I know not what to do.—Foible!—*Calls.* I expect my nephew Sir Wilfull ev’ry moment too.—Why, Foible!—He means to travel for improvement.

===Chinese===
*（**威什福特夫人**至她跟前。）*

**威什福特夫人.** 哦，亲爱的玛伍德，这般无礼的怠慢，教我说什么好？好在亲爱的朋友浑身都是好心。

**玛伍德夫人.** 不必赔不是，亲爱的夫人。我自娱自乐，打发得很好。

**威什福特夫人.** 我以人格担保，想我竟这样忘乎所以，心里乱成一团麻。可我手里这一大摊子杂烩事务，实在不知从何下手。——福伊布尔！——（唤人。）我那侄儿威尔福爵士，也随时会到。——嗳，福伊布尔！——他此番出门游历，为的长长见识。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
Methinks Sir Wilfull should rather think of marrying than travelling at his years. I hear he is turned of forty.
Lady Wishfort
Oh, he’s in less danger of being spoiled by his travels. I am against my nephew’s marrying too young. It will be time enough when he comes back, and has acquired discretion to choose for himself.
Mrs. Marwood
Methinks Mrs. Millamant and he would make a very fit match. He may travel afterwards. ’Tis a thing very usual with young gentlemen.
Lady Wishfort
I promise you I have thought on’t—and since ’tis your judgment, I’ll think on’t again. I assure you I will; I value your judgment extremely. On my word, I’ll propose it.

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 依我看，威尔福爵士那把年纪，与其想游历，不如想想成家。听说他年过四十了。

**威什福特夫人.** 哦，游历嘛，把他带坏的危险还小些。我反对侄儿成家太早。等他回来，学出些挑拣的眼力，也不为迟。

**玛伍德夫人.** 依我看，米勒曼特同他倒是一对天造地设。婚后再游历不迟，少年爷们时兴这个。

**威什福特夫人.** 不瞒您说，我动过这个念头——既然这是您的裁断，我就再动一回。您放心，我一定动；您的裁断，我看得极重。我说话算话，这就去提。

===Original===
*Enter **Foible**.*
Lady Wishfort
Come, come, Foible—I had forgot my nephew will be here before dinner—I must make haste.
Foible
Mr. Witwoud and Mr. Petulant are come to dine with your ladyship.
Lady Wishfort
Oh dear, I can’t appear till I am dressed. Dear Marwood, shall I be free with you again, and beg you to entertain ’em? I’ll make all imaginable haste. Dear friend, excuse me.

===Chinese===
*（**福伊布尔**上。）*

**威什福特夫人.** 来，来，福伊布尔——我忘了，侄儿饭前就到——得抓紧了。

**福伊布尔.** 威特伍德先生和佩特兰特先生，来陪夫人用饭了。

**威什福特夫人.** 哎呀，不等穿戴齐整，我出不去见人。亲爱的玛伍德，容我再放肆一回，求您替我招呼他们？我尽快赶，有多快赶多快。亲爱的朋友，失陪失陪。

### Scene III / 第三场

===Original===
A room in **Lady Wishfort’s** house.

***Mrs. Marwood**, **Mrs. Millamant**, and **Mincing**.*
Mrs. Millamant
Sure, never anything was so unbred as that odious man.—Marwood, your servant.
Mrs. Marwood
You have a colour; what’s the matter?
Mrs. Millamant
That horrid fellow Petulant has provoked me into a flame: I have broke my fan—Mincing, lend me yours; is not all the powder out of my hair?
Mrs. Marwood
No. What has he done?

===Chinese===
威什福特夫人家中一室。

*（**玛伍德夫人**、**米勒曼特**与**明辛**上。）*

**米勒曼特.** 真的，天底下再没见过这么没教养的东西，那个可厌的男人。——玛伍德，你安。

**玛伍德夫人.** 你脸上有潮红；怎么了？

**米勒曼特.** 那个可恨的佩特兰特，把我气得七窍生烟：扇子都叫我折了——明辛，把你的借我；我头发上的粉，是不是全抖落了？

**玛伍德夫人.** 没有。他怎么了？

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Nay, he has done nothing; he has only talked. Nay, he has said nothing neither; but he has contradicted everything that has been said. For my part, I thought Witwoud and he would have quarrelled.
Mincing
I vow, mem, I thought once they would have fit.
Mrs. Millamant
Well, ’tis a lamentable thing, I swear, that one has not the liberty of choosing one’s acquaintance as one does one’s clothes.
Mrs. Marwood
If we had that liberty, we should be as weary of one set of acquaintance, though never so good, as we are of one suit, though never so fine. A fool and a doily stuff would now and then find days of grace, and be worn for variety.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 嗐，他没怎么；光动嘴。嗐，连嘴上也没说出个什么；可人家说什么，他都驳。说真的，我还当威特伍德同他要吵起来了。

**明辛.** 真的，小姐，我一度当他们真要交手呢。

**米勒曼特.** 唉，说起来可怜：挑相识，竟不能像挑衣裳一样由着自个儿。

**玛伍德夫人.** 果真有那份自由，再好的相识，处旧了也一样叫人厌，正如再好的衣裳，穿旧了一样。傻瓜同多伊利呢，隔三差五还能讨着宽限，权当换个花样穿一穿。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
I could consent to wear ’em, if they would wear alike; but fools never wear out. They are such *drap de Berri*47 things! Without one could give ’em to one’s chambermaid after a day or two!
Mrs. Marwood
’Twere better so indeed. Or what think you of the playhouse? A fine gay glossy fool should be given there, like a new masking habit, after the masquerade is over, and we have done with the disguise. For a fool’s visit is always a disguise, and never admitted by a woman of wit, but to blind her affair with a lover of sense. If you would but appear barefaced now, and own Mirabell, you might as easily put off Petulant and Witwoud as your hood and scarf. And indeed ’tis time, for the town has found it, the secret is grown too big for the pretence. ’Tis like Mrs. Primly’s great belly: she may lace it down before, but it burnishes on her hips.48 Indeed, Millamant, you can no more conceal it than my Lady Strammel can her face, that goodly face, which in defiance of her Rhenish-wine tea49 will not be comprehended in a mask.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 他们若也能一视同仁地磨损，倒也罢了；可傻瓜永不磨损。简直是贝里粗呢（47）！又不能穿个一两天，就赏给自家的贴身丫头！

**玛伍德夫人.** 这般固然妥当。再不然，戏园子你看如何？光鲜锃亮的傻瓜，就该送到那儿去，好比化装舞会散了场，那身新行头便送了人，咱们同那副伪装也一刀两断。因为傻瓜的来访，本就是一重化装的伪装；有才情的女人肯接待他，无非拿他障眼，遮掩同一位于见识的情人的私情。你如今索性亮出素面，认下米兰芒特，脱掉佩特兰特和威特伍德，就会同摘下兜帽、解下头巾一样容易。也确是时候了：城里人已经识破，秘密长得太大，那层幌子包不住了。就像普里姆利太太的大肚皮：前头勒得下去，胯上可就鼓得发亮（48）。说真的，米勒曼特，这事你瞒不住，正如斯特拉梅尔夫人瞒不住她那张脸——那张堂堂的好脸，任凭她灌莱茵酒茶（49）保养，也终究收不进面罩里去。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
I’ll take my death, Marwood, you are more censorious than a decayed beauty, or a discarded toast.50 Mincing, tell the men they may come up. My aunt is not dressing here; their folly is less provoking than your malice. *Exit **Mincing**.* The town has found it! what has it found? That Mirabell loves me is no more a secret than it is a secret that you discovered it to my aunt, or than the reason why you discovered it is a secret.
Mrs. Marwood
You are nettled.
Mrs. Millamant
You’re mistaken. Ridiculous!
Mrs. Marwood
Indeed, my dear, you’ll tear another fan, if you don’t mitigate those violent airs.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 我敢拿命打赌，玛伍德，你这刻薄劲，赛过人老珠黄的美人、过气的祝酒美人（50）。明辛，去说男人们可以上来了。我姨妈不在这儿梳妆；男人家的蠢，还没你的毒气人。（**明辛**下。）城里人识破了！识破了什么？米兰芒特爱我，算不得秘密；正如你把这层捅给我姨妈，不是秘密；你为何去捅，同样不是秘密。

**玛伍德夫人.** 您恼了。

**米勒曼特.** 你想错了。可笑！

**玛伍德夫人.** 真的，亲爱的，再不收收这股泼辣劲儿，您又要折断一把扇子。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
O silly! ha! ha! ha! I could laugh immoderately. Poor Mirabell! His constancy to me has quite destroyed his complaisance for all the world beside. I swear I never enjoined it him to be so coy—If I had the vanity to think he would obey me, I would command him to show more gallantry—’tis hardly well-bred to be so particular on one hand and so insensible on the other. But I despair to prevail, and so let him follow his own way. Ha! ha! ha! Pardon me, dear creature, I must laugh; ha! ha! ha! Though I grant you ’tis a little barbarous; ha! ha! ha!
Mrs. Marwood
What pity ’tis so much fine raillery, and delivered with so significant gesture, should be so unhappily directed to miscarry.
Mrs. Millamant
Heh? Dear creature, I ask your pardon—I swear I did not mind you.
Mrs. Marwood
Mr. Mirabell and you both may think it a thing impossible, when I shall tell him by telling you—
Mrs. Millamant
Oh dear, what? For it is the same thing, if I hear it—ha! ha! ha!
Mrs. Marwood
That I detest him, hate him, madam.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 哦，傻话！哈！哈！哈！我能笑个没够。可怜的米兰芒特！他对我一人忠心，把对全世界的殷勤赔了个精光。我起誓，我可没吩咐过他这样冷淡——我若有那份虚荣，料他肯听话，我倒要下令，叫他多拿出些骑士风度——独独对一人热、对旁人一概冷，这也算不得怎么体面。可我不指望说得动他，由他去罢。哈！哈！哈！饶恕我，亲爱的，我忍不住要笑；哈！哈！哈！虽说我认了，这确实有点狠；哈！哈！哈！

**玛伍德夫人.** 可惜呀，这么精美的俏皮话，配上这么传神的身段，竟不幸瞄错了方向，尽付东流。

**米勒曼特.** 嗯？亲爱的，恕我失敬——我起誓，方才没把你放在心上。

**玛伍德夫人.** 等我借您转告他之时，米兰芒特先生同您二位，怕都要当它是天方夜谭——

**米勒曼特.** 哎呀，是什么？左右我听见了，就等于他听见了——哈！哈！哈！

**玛伍德夫人.** 便是我嫌恶他、恨他，夫人。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
O madam, why, so do I—and yet the creature loves me, ha! ha! ha! How can one forbear laughing to think of it.—I am a sibyl if I am not amazed to think what he can see in me. I’ll take my death,51 I think you are handsomer—and within a year or two as young. If you could but stay for me, I should overtake you—but that cannot be.—Well, that thought makes me melancholic.—Now I’ll be sad.
Mrs. Marwood
Your merry note may be changed sooner than you think.
Mrs. Millamant
D’ye say so? Then I’m resolved I’ll have a song to keep up my spirits.
*Reenter **Mincing**.*
Mincing
The gentlemen stay but to comb, madam, and will wait on you.
Mrs. Millamant
Desire Mrs.—that is in the next room, to sing the song I would have learnt yesterday. You shall hear it, madam. Not that there’s any great matter in it—but ’tis agreeable to my humour.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 哦，夫人，可不是么，我也恨——可那家伙偏爱我，哈！哈！哈！想起来怎么忍得住笑。——想想他究竟看上我哪一点，我不惊奇才怪。我拿性命打赌（51），你比我标致——再过一两年，连岁数也追平了。你若肯等等我，我保管追上你——可惜等不得。——唉，这个念头叫我忧郁。——这会儿我要伤感了。

**玛伍德夫人.** 您这支欢快调子，改腔怕比您想的早。

**米勒曼特.** 当真？那我拿定主意，点一支歌，撑住我的兴致。

*（**明辛**又上。）*

**明辛.** 几位先生正梳头，梳好就来奉陪，小姐。

**米勒曼特.** 叫隔壁屋里那位——那位太太，把我要学的那支歌唱起来。您听听，夫人。倒不是里头有什么大讲究——只是合我的脾胃。

===Original===
**Song**
Love’s but the frailty of the mind
When ’tis not with ambition joined;
A sickly flame, which if not fed, expires,
And feeding, wastes in self-consuming fires.
’Tis not to wound a wanton boy
Or am’rous youth, that gives the joy;
But ’tis the glory to have pierced a swain,
For whom inferior beauties sighed in vain.
Then I alone the conquest prize,
When I insult a rival’s eyes;
If there’s delight in love, ’tis when I see
That heart, which others bleed for, bleed for me.

===Chinese===
**歌**

爱情不过心神的孱弱，
若不与雄心结伴相佐；
一炬病焰：不添薪便熄，
添了薪，又在自噬的烈焰里枯息。

快活不在箭伤顽童，
也不在箭中少年情种；
荣耀是射穿一位情郎——
多少逊色的美人，为他空断肠。

惟我独占这征服的战利，
当我耀武于情敌的眼底；
爱情若有欢愉，只在此际：
万人流血谋的那颗心，为我血流不止。

===Original===
*Enter **Petulant** and **Witwoud**.*
Mrs. Millamant
Is your animosity composed, gentlemen?
Witwoud
Raillery, raillery, madam; we have no animosity. We hit off a little wit now and then, but no animosity. The falling out of wits is like the falling out of lovers:—we agree in the main,52 like treble and bass.—Ha, Petulant?
Petulant
Aye, in the main. But when I have a humour to contradict—
Witwoud
Aye, when he has a humour to contradict, then I contradict too. What, I know my cue. Then we contradict one another like two battledores; for contradictions beget one another like Jews.

===Chinese===
*（**佩特兰特**与**威特伍德**上。）*

**米勒曼特.** 二位先生的敌意，消停了没有？

**威特伍德.** 打趣，打趣，夫人；我们没什么敌意。我们时不时碰出点才情的火星，可没什么敌意。才子们的失和，同恋人们的失和是一路：——大体上我们协和（52），好比高音部配低音部。——嗯，佩特兰特？

**佩特兰特.** 是，大体上。可赶上我想抬杠的时候——

**威特伍德.** 是，赶上他想抬杠，我也就跟着抬。怎么，我的戏份我熟。那时节我们就对抬，像两只羽毛球拍；因为矛盾生矛盾，一茬接一茬，像犹太人。

===Original===
Petulant
If he says black’s black—if I have a humour to say ’tis blue—let that pass—all’s one for that. If I have a humour to prove it, it must be granted.
Witwoud
Not positively must—but it may—it may.
Petulant
Yes, it positively must, upon proof positive.
Witwoud
Aye, upon proof positive it must; but upon proof presumptive it only may.—That’s a logical distinction now, madam.

===Chinese===
**佩特兰特.** 他若说黑是黑——赶上我兴致，说它是蓝——由它去——横竖一回事。我兴致来了要论证，那就必须准。

**威特伍德.** 倒未必是必须——可以是——可以是。

**佩特兰特.** 不，就是必须，凭实证。

**威特伍德.** 是，凭实证是必须；凭推定么，就只是可以。——这可是一条逻辑学的分别，夫人。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
I perceive your debates are of importance, and very learnedly handled.
Petulant
Importance is one thing and learning’s another; but a debate’s a debate, that I assert.
Witwoud
Petulant’s an enemy to learning; he relies altogether on his parts.
Petulant
No, I’m no enemy to learning; it hurts not me.
Mrs. Marwood
That’s a sign indeed it’s no enemy to you.
Petulant
No, no, it’s no enemy to anybody but them that have it.

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 我看二位的辩论非同小可，而且处理得极有学问。

**佩特兰特.** 重要是一回事，学问是另一回事；可辩论就是辩论，这话我坚持。

**威特伍德.** 佩特兰特是学问的仇人；他全凭天分行事。

**佩特兰特.** 不，我不是学问的仇人；它伤不着我。

**玛伍德夫人.** 这倒真是个凭据：学问同你没有仇。

**佩特兰特.** 不不，它同谁都无仇，就是同有学问的人过不去。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Well, an illiterate man’s my aversion: I wonder at the impudence of any illiterate man to offer to make love.
Witwoud
That I confess I wonder at, too.
Mrs. Millamant
Ah, to marry an ignorant that can hardly read or write.
Petulant
Why should a man be any further from being married, though he can’t read, than he is from being hanged? The ordinary’s paid for setting the psalm,53 and the parish priest for reading the ceremony. And for the rest which is to follow in both cases, a man may do it without book—so all’s one for that.
Mrs. Millamant
D’ye hear the creature?—Lord, here’s company; I’ll begone.
*Exit.*
*Enter **Sir Wilfull Witwoud** in a riding dress, followed by **Footman**.*

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 唉，没学问的男人最叫我反胃：那些目不识丁的竟敢开口求爱，这脸皮叫我称奇。

**威特伍德.** 这个，我招认，我也称奇。

**米勒曼特.** 嗐，去嫁一个大字识不了半升的蠢物。

**佩特兰特.** 男人不识字，怎么会就离成婚远一层？他离上绞架也没远一层呀。定赞美诗，有牧师领班领俸（53）；念婚书，有教区牧师领俸。至于两桩事随后该来的那些节目，男人不用翻书也照样办——所以横竖一回事。

**米勒曼特.** 听听这家伙。——天哪，来人了；我走了。

*（下。）*

*（**威尔福·威特伍德爵士**骑装打扮上，**男仆**随后。）*

===Original===
Witwoud
In the name of Bartlemew and his fair,54 what have we here?
Mrs. Marwood
’Tis your brother, I fancy. Don’t you know him?
Witwoud
Not I.—Yes, I think it is he—I’ve almost forgot him; I have not seen him since the Revolution.
Footman
*To **Sir Wilful**.* Sir, my lady’s dressing. Here’s company, if you please to walk in, in the meantime.
Sir Wilful
Dressing! What, it’s but morning here, I warrant, with you in London; we should count it towards afternoon in our parts down in Shropshire:—why, then, belike my aunt han’t dined yet, ha, friend?

===Chinese===
**威特伍德.** 凭巴塞洛缪同他的市集（54）起誓，这是打哪儿来的一位？

**玛伍德夫人.** 我看是你兄弟。你认不得？

**威特伍德.** 认不得。——嗯，大概是他——我几乎把他忘了；自打革命年起，我就没见过他。

**男仆.**（向**威尔福爵士**）老爷，我家夫人正梳妆。这儿有客，您请先进来坐坐。

**威尔福爵士.** 梳妆！怎么，你们伦敦这会儿才刚是早晨，我担保；搁我们什罗普郡老家，都得算下半晌了：——那么这么说，我姨妈还没用饭，嗯，朋友？

===Original===
Footman
Your aunt, sir?
Sir Wilful
My aunt, sir! Yes my aunt, sir, and your lady, sir; your lady is my aunt, sir.—Why, what dost thou not know me, friend? Why, then, send somebody hither that does. How long hast thou lived with thy lady, fellow, ha?
Footman
A week, sir; longer than anybody in the house, except my lady’s woman.
Sir Wilful
Why, then, belike thou dost not know thy lady, if thou seest her, ha, friend?
Footman
Why, truly, sir, I cannot safely swear to her face in a morning, before she is dressed. ’Tis like I may give a shrewd guess at her by this time.

===Chinese===
**男仆.** 您的姨妈，老爷？

**威尔福爵士.** 我的姨妈，老爷！正是我的姨妈，老爷，也是你家的夫人，老爷；你家夫人就是我的姨妈，老爷。——怎么，你认不得我，朋友？那就打发个认得的人来。你伺候你家夫人多久了，伙计，嗯？

**男仆.** 一个礼拜，老爷；除了夫人的贴身女仆，算宅里最久的了。

**威尔福爵士.** 那么这么说，你就是见了你家夫人的面，多半也认不得，嗯，朋友？

**男仆.** 嗐，说实在的，老爷，早上她没梳妆之前，她的脸，我可不敢打包票。到这会儿，多半能有个八九不离十的猜法。

===Original===
Sir Wilful
Well, prithee try what thou canst do; if thou canst not guess, enquire her out, dost hear, fellow? And tell her her nephew, Sir Wilfull Witwoud, is in the house.
Footman
I shall, sir.
Sir Wilful
Hold ye, hear me, friend, a word with you in your ear: prithee who are these gallants?
Footman
Really, sir, I can’t tell; here come so many here, ’tis hard to know ’em all.
*Exit.*
Sir Wilful
Oons, this fellow knows less than a starling: I don’t think a’ knows his own name.
Mrs. Marwood
Mr. Witwoud, your brother is not behindhand in forgetfulness. I fancy he has forgot you too.
Witwoud
I hope so. The devil take him that remembers first, I say.

===Chinese===
**威尔福爵士.** 好，那你就试试你的本事；猜不出，就去打听出来，听见没有，伙计？告诉她，她的侄儿威尔福·威特伍德爵士到了。

**男仆.** 遵命，老爷。

**威尔福爵士.** 站住，听着，朋友，咬个耳朵：这几位风流君子，是何方神圣？

**男仆.** 说真的，老爷，说不上来；来的爷们太多，认不全。

*（下。）*

**威尔福爵士.** 见鬼，这家伙肚里的货，比一只椋鸟还少：我看他连自个儿姓什么都不晓得。

**玛伍德夫人.** 威特伍德先生，论健忘，令兄也不落人后。我看他把您也忘了。

**威特伍德.** 我巴不得。谁先想起来谁遭瘟，我说。

===Original===
Sir Wilful
Save you, gentlemen and lady.
Mrs. Marwood
For shame, Mr. Witwoud; why won’t you speak to him?—And you, sir.
Witwoud
Petulant, speak.
Petulant
And you, sir.
Sir Wilful
No offence, I hope? *Salutes **Mrs. Marwood**.*
Mrs. Marwood
No, sure, sir.
Witwoud
This is a vile dog, I see that already. No offence! Ha! ha! ha! To him, to him, Petulant, smoke him.55

===Chinese===
**威尔福爵士.** 列位先生、太太，都安。

**玛伍德夫人.** 不害臊么，威特伍德先生；怎么不搭理他？——还有您，先生。

**威特伍德.** 佩特兰特，你说。

**佩特兰特.** 你才该说呢，先生。

**威尔福爵士.** 没有冒犯吧，希望？（向**玛伍德夫人**行礼。）

**玛伍德夫人.** 当然没有，先生。

**威特伍德.** 这是个土包子恶狗，我一眼就看出来了。「没有冒犯」！哈！哈！哈！上，上，佩特兰特，拿烟熏他（55）。

===Original===
Petulant
It seems as if you had come a journey, sir; hem, hem. *Surveying him round.*
Sir Wilful
Very likely, sir, that it may seem so.
Petulant
No offence, I hope, sir?
Witwoud
Smoke the boots, the boots, Petulant, the boots: ha! ha! ha!
Sir Wilful
Maybe not, sir; thereafter as ’tis meant,56 sir.
Petulant
Sir, I presume upon the information of your boots.
Sir Wilful
Why, ’tis like you may, sir: if you are not satisfied with the information of my boots, sir, if you will step to the stable, you may enquire further of my horse, sir.

===Chinese===
**佩特兰特.** 瞧您这样子，像是刚赶了路，先生，咳咳。（围着他上下打量。）

**威尔福爵士.** 很像是这么回事，先生，瞧着像。

**佩特兰特.** 没有冒犯吧，希望，先生？

**威特伍德.** 熏那靴子，那靴子，佩特兰特，那靴子：哈！哈！哈！

**威尔福爵士.** 也许没有，先生；全看话里存的什么心（56），先生。

**佩特兰特.** 先生，我这是就您靴子提供的情报，放胆推测。

**威尔福爵士.** 嗐，你尽管推测，先生：先生若对我靴子的口供不满足，劳步上马厩，还可向我那匹马，进一步盘问，先生。

===Original===
Petulant
Your horse, sir! Your horse is an ass, sir!
Sir Wilful
Do you speak by way of offence, sir?
Mrs. Marwood
The gentleman’s merry, that’s all, sir. *Aside.* ’Slife, we shall have a quarrel betwixt an horse and an ass, before they find one another out.—*Aloud.* You must not take anything amiss from your friends, sir. You are among your friends here, though it—may be you don’t know it. If I am not mistaken, you are Sir Wilfull Witwoud?
Sir Wilful
Right, lady; I am Sir Wilfull Witwoud, so I write myself; no offence to anybody, I hope; and nephew to the Lady Wishfort of this mansion.

===Chinese===
**佩特兰特.** 你那匹马，先生！你那匹马是头驴，先生！

**威尔福爵士.** 你这话，是存心冒犯么，先生？

**玛伍德夫人.** 这位先生说着玩呢，仅此而已，先生。（旁白）我的命，眼看要有一场马同驴的口角，还不知道彼此是谁。——（高声）朋友说的话，您可不能句句计较，先生。这儿在座的都是您的朋友，虽说是——您兴许还不晓得。我没认错的话，您是威尔福·威特伍德爵士？

**威尔福爵士.** 正是，夫人；我是威尔福·威特伍德爵士，落款签名就这么写；但愿没冒犯谁；也是这座宅子威什福特夫人的侄儿。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
Don’t you know this gentleman, sir?
Sir Wilful
Hum! What, sure ’tis not—yea by’r lady but ’tis—s’heart, I know not whether ’tis or no—yea, but ’tis, by the Wrekin. Brother Anthony! What, Tony, i’faith! What, dost thou not know me? By’r lady, nor I thee, thou art so becravated and so beperiwigged.—S’heart, why dost not speak? Art thou o’erjoyed?
Witwoud
Odso, brother, is it you? Your servant, brother.
Sir Wilful
Your servant! Why, yours, sir. Your servant again—s’heart, and your friend and servant to that—and a—*puff* and a flap-dragon for your service, sir, and a hare’s foot and a hare’s scut57 for your service, sir, an you be so cold and so courtly!

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 您认得这位先生么，先生？

**威尔福爵士.** 唔！怎么，保不齐不是——嗬，凭圣母起誓，正是——哎呀，我说不清是不是——嗯，就是，凭雷金山起誓。安东尼兄弟！嗬，托尼，真是你！怎么，你认不得我了？凭圣母起誓，我也认不得你了：你围脖围得、假发扣得，也太齐全。——哎呀，怎么不说话？喜糊涂了？

**威特伍德.** 哎呀，兄弟，是你？您安，兄弟。

**威尔福爵士.** 您安！嗐，您安，先生。再敬您一安——哎呀，还要搭上朋友兼仆人的一安——外加——（噗）——一颗火酒里抢的葡萄干，敬您，先生；再搭上一只兔子脚、一条兔子尾（57），敬您，先生——您既然这样冷冰冰、酸溜溜地讲客套！

===Original===
Witwoud
No offence, I hope, brother.
Sir Wilful
S’heart, sir, but there is, and much offence!—A pox, is this your inns o’ court breeding, not to know your friends and your relations, your elders, and your betters?
Witwoud
Why, brother Wilfull of Salop,58 you may be as short as a Shrewsbury cake, if you please. But I tell you ’tis not modish to know relations in town.:you think you’re in the country, where great lubberly brothers slabber and kiss one another when they meet, like a call of sergeants.59—’tis not the fashion here; ’tis not, indeed, dear brother.
Sir Wilful
The fashion’s a fool and you’re a fop, dear brother. S’heart, I’ve suspected this—by’r lady I conjectured you were a fop, since you began to change the style of your letters, and write in a scrap of paper gilt round the edges, no bigger than a subpoena. I might expect this when you left off “Honoured brother,” and “Hoping you are in good health,” and so forth, to begin with a “Rat me, knight, I’m so sick of a last night’s debauch.” Ods heart, and then tell a familiar tale of a cock and a bull, and a whore and a bottle, and so conclude. You could write news before you were out of your time,60 when you lived with honest Pimple Nose, the attorney of Furnival’s Inn.61—you could intreat to be remembered then to your friends round the reckan.62 We could have gazettes then, and Dawks’s Letter,63 and the Weekly Bill,64 till of late days.

===Chinese===
**威特伍德.** 没有见怪的意思罢，兄弟。

**威尔福爵士.** 哎呀先生，见怪了，见大怪了！——遭瘟的，这就是你们律师学院调教出来的规矩？认不得朋友亲眷、长上尊长了？

**威特伍德.** 嗐，萨洛普（58）来的威尔福兄弟，您尽可以像什鲁斯伯里饼一样又薄又脆，随您的便。可我告诉您，城里认亲，不兴。您当还在乡下呢：乡下那地方，傻大个儿的兄弟们一见面又啃又亲，活像一伙点卯的警吏（59）——城里不时兴这个；真不时兴，亲爱的兄弟。

**威尔福爵士.** 时髦是个傻瓜，你是个花花公子，亲爱的兄弟。哎呀，我早有疑心——凭圣母起誓，打你一起改写信的路数，我就断定你成了花花公子：写的纸片儿，边上烫着金，小得同张传票差不多。你那时丢开「敬爱的兄弟」啦、「想来贵体康泰」啦这一套，开头改成「气煞我也，爵士，昨晚荒唐得我直恶心」，我就该料到今日。哎呀个天，随后再讲一段公鸡同公牛、婊子同酒瓶的家常，就此收梢。你没出师那阵子（60），跟着菲尔纳尔旅馆（61）那位诚实的疙瘩鼻律师当差，那时节你连新闻都会写——还会绕着酒账（62），央人代向各路朋友问好。那时咱们还有公报看，有多克斯新闻信（63），有每周死亡表（64），直到近来才断了。

===Original===
Petulant
’Slife, Witwoud, were you ever an attorney’s clerk? Of the family of the Furnivals? Ha! ha! ha!
Witwoud
Aye, aye, but that was but for a while. Not long, not long; pshaw, I was not in my own power then. An orphan, and this fellow was my guardian; aye, aye, I was glad to consent to that man to come to London. He had the disposal of me then. If I had not agreed to that, I might have been bound ’prentice to a feltmaker in Shrewsbury: this fellow would have bound me to a maker of felts.
Sir Wilful
S’heart, and better than to be bound to a maker of fops, where, I suppose, you have served your time, and now you may set up for yourself.
Mrs. Marwood
You intend to travel, sir, as I’m informed?

===Chinese===
**佩特兰特.** 我的命，威特伍德，你当过律师的书记？菲尔纳尔一门子的？哈！哈！哈！

**威特伍德.** 是是，可那不过一阵子。不长，不长；咳，那时我做不得自己的主。孤儿一个，这家伙是我监护人；是是，我巴不得点头，跟了那个人上伦敦。那时，我的处置权在他手里。我若不点头，早被绑去什鲁斯伯里学制毡了：这家伙铁了心，要把我往毡子里头绑。

**威尔福爵士.** 哎呀，绑去制毡，也强过绑给一位制造花花公子的师傅——依我看，你在他那儿已经满了师，如今尽可自立门户。

**玛伍德夫人.** 听说先生有意游历？

===Original===
Sir Wilful
Belike I may, madam. I may chance to sail upon the salt seas, if my mind hold.
Petulant
And the wind serve.
Sir Wilful
Serve or not serve, I shan’t ask license of you, sir, nor the weathercock your companion. I direct my discourse to the lady, sir.—’Tis like my aunt may have told you, madam? Yes, I have settled my concerns, I may say now, and am minded to see foreign parts. If an how that the peace holds, whereby, that is, taxes abate.65
Mrs. Marwood
I thought you had designed for France at all adventures.
Sir Wilful
I can’t tell that; ’tis like I may, and ’tis like I may not. I am somewhat dainty in making a resolution, because when I make it I keep it. I don’t stand shill I, shall I, then; if I say’t, I’ll do’t. But I have thoughts to tarry a small matter in town, to learn somewhat of your lingo first, before I cross the seas. I’d gladly have a spice of your French as they say, whereby to hold discourse in foreign countries.

===Chinese===
**威尔福爵士.** 多半是真，夫人。主意若是不改，兴许要上咸水洋面上走一遭。

**佩特兰特.** 还得风肯伺候。

**威尔福爵士.** 风伺候不伺候，都不劳您恩准，先生；也不劳您那位同伴——风信鸡——恩准。我同夫人说话，先生。——我姨妈想必同您提起过，夫人？是了，我如今总算把家务料理停当，有心见识见识外国地方。只消——这么说罢——和约撑得住，也就是说，税能减下来（65）。

**玛伍德夫人.** 我还当您破釜沉舟，也要去法国。

**威尔福爵士.** 这说不好；兴许去，也兴许不去。我下决心向来郑重，因为一旦下了，便要守住。我可不站在那里「去呢、不去呢」地哆嗦；话既出口，事就办到。只是我想在城里略盘桓些时日，先学学你们这路话，再漂洋过海。常言说得好，我很乐意沾一星半点你们的法国味儿，好在外国地界对付交谈。

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
Here’s an academy in town for that use.
Sir Wilful
There is? ’Tis like there may.66
Mrs. Marwood
No doubt you will return very much improved.
Witwoud
Yes, refined like a Dutch skipper from a whale-fishing.
*Enter **Lady Wishfort** and **Fainall**.*
Lady Wishfort
Nephew, you are welcome.
Sir Wilful
Aunt, your servant.
Fainall
Sir Wilfull, your most faithful servant.
Sir Wilful
Cousin Fainall, give me your hand.

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 城里正有一所专管这个的学院。

**威尔福爵士.** 有么？多半是有（66）。

**玛伍德夫人.** 您回来时，必然大有长进，这毋庸置疑。

**威特伍德.** 是啊，像出海捕鲸回来的荷兰船长一样精炼。

*（**威什福特夫人**与**费诺**上。）*

**威什福特夫人.** 侄儿，欢迎你。

**威尔福爵士.** 姨妈，您安。

**费诺.** 威尔福爵士，您最忠实的仆人。

**威尔福爵士.** 费诺表弟，把手给我。

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Cousin Witwoud, your servant; Mr. Petulant, your servant—nephew, you are welcome again. Will you drink anything after your journey, nephew, before you eat? Dinner’s almost ready.
Sir Wilful
I’m very well, I thank you, aunt—however, I thank you for your courteous offer. S’heart, I was afraid you would have been in the fashion too, and have remembered to have forgot your relations. Here’s your cousin Tony, belike, I mayn’t call him brother for fear of offence.
Lady Wishfort
Oh, he’s a rallier, nephew—my cousin’s a wit: and your great wits always rally their best friends to choose.67 When you have been abroad, nephew, you’ll understand raillery better.
***Fainall** and **Mrs. Marwood** talk apart.*
Sir Wilful
Why, then, let him hold his tongue in the meantime, and rail when that day comes.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 威特伍德表侄，你安；佩特兰特先生，你安——侄儿，再迎你一回。赶了路，饭前要不要喝点什么，侄儿？饭这就好了。

**威尔福爵士.** 我很好，多谢姨妈——不过这番美意，我心领。哎呀，我还生怕您也赶了时髦，学会「记得把亲戚忘掉」呢。这不是您家托尼表亲么——只怕开罪，不敢叫他兄弟了。

**威什福特夫人.** 哦，他是打趣的行家，侄儿——我这表侄是个才子：而大才子们打趣起来，向来专拣顶好的朋友下手（67）。等你留了洋，侄儿，你就更懂什么叫打趣了。

*（**费诺**与**玛伍德夫人**一旁密谈。）*

**威尔福爵士.** 那么，这期间还是请他闭嘴，等那天到了，再打趣不迟。

===Original===
*Enter **Mincing**.*
Mincing
Mem, I come to acquaint your la’ship that dinner is impatient.
Sir Wilful
Impatient? Why, then, belike it won’t stay till I pull off my boots. Sweetheart, can you help me to a pair of slippers? My man’s with his horses, I warrant.
Lady Wishfort
Fie, fie, nephew, you would not pull off your boots here?—Go down into the hall—dinner shall stay for you.—My nephew’s a little unbred: you’ll pardon him, madam.—Gentlemen, will you walk?—Marwood—
Mrs. Marwood
I’ll follow you, madam—before Sir Wilfull is ready.
*Exeunt all but **Mrs. Marwood** and **Fainall**.*

===Chinese===
*（**明辛**上。）*

**明辛.** 小姐，我来回话：饭等得不耐烦啦。

**威尔福爵士.** 不耐烦？嗐，那它多半等不得我脱靴子了。心肝儿，能替我寻双便鞋不？我那仆人，保管看着马呢。

**威什福特夫人.** 呸，呸，侄儿，你总不至于要在这儿脱靴子？——下楼，上大厅去——饭等得起你。——我这侄儿欠些教养：夫人多担待。——列位先生，请挪步？——玛伍德——

**玛伍德夫人.** 我随您就来，夫人——保管赶在威尔福爵士收拾停当之前。

*（余人俱下，惟留**玛伍德夫人**与**费诺**。）*

===Original===
Fainall
Why, then, Foible’s a bawd, an errant, rank matchmaking bawd. And I, it seems, am a husband, a rank husband, and my wife a very errant, rank wife—all in the way of the world. ’Sdeath, to be a cuckold by anticipation, a cuckold in embryo! Sure I was born with budding antlers like a young satyr, or a citizen’s child. ’Sdeath, to be outwitted—to be out-jilted—out-matrimonied!—If I had kept my speed like a stag, ’twere somewhat—but to crawl after, with my horns like a snail, and be outstripped by my wife—’tis scurvy wedlock.
Mrs. Marwood
Then shake it off: you have often wished for an opportunity to part—and now you have it. But first prevent their plot—the half of Millamant’s fortune is too considerable to be parted with to a foe, to Mirabell.
Fainall
Damn him! that had been mine—had you not made that fond discovery—that had been forfeited, had they been married. My wife had added lustre to my horns by that increase of fortune: I could have worn ’em tipt with gold, though my forehead had been furnished like a deputy-lieutenant’s hall.68
Mrs. Marwood
They may prove a cap of maintenance69 to you still, if you can away with your wife. And she’s no worse than when you had her:—I dare swear she had given up her game before she was married.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 这么说，福伊布尔是个皮条婆，一个地道地道、够味够味的撮合山皮条婆。而我呢，据说是个丈夫，个顶够味的丈夫，我太太是个如假包换、够味到家的太太——一桩桩一件件，全依着「如此世道」。该死，竟做先期而至的王八、胚胎里的王八！敢情我生下来就顶着冒芽的犄角，像头小山魈，或是市井人家养的娃。该死——斗心眼输了个精光——连同人家自己的手段也输——连婚都叫人抢先结了！——我若像公鹿一路保住脚程，倒也罢了——偏要顶着犄角、像蜗牛在后头爬，还叫我老婆超了车——这婚姻，腌臜透了。

**玛伍德夫人.** 那就抖掉它：你不是常常巴望有个脱身的机会么——如今有了。可先得破了他们的计——米勒曼特那一半家私太可观，断不能落入仇人、落入米兰芒特之手。

**费诺.** 去他的！那份原该归我——若非你发痴去戳穿——他们若成了亲，那一半早缴了没。我太太那笔进项，本可以给我的犄角镀金：哪怕我这脑门，挂得像郡尉副官的厅堂（68），我也戴得起金尖的。

**玛伍德夫人.** 那对犄角，兴许还做得你的仪礼冠（69），只消你受得住太太。她同你上手那天比，一分没差：——我敢起誓，出阁之前，她早收了猎场。

===Original===
Fainall
Hum! That may be.
Mrs. Marwood
You married her to keep you; and if you can contrive to have her keep you better than you expected, why should you not keep her longer than you intended?
Fainall
The means, the means.
Mrs. Marwood
Discover to my lady your wife’s conduct; threaten to part with her!—my lady loves her, and will come to any composition to save her reputation. Take the opportunity of breaking it just upon the discovery of this imposture. My lady will be enraged beyond bounds, and sacrifice niece, and fortune and all at that conjuncture. And let me alone to keep her warm: if she should flag in her part, I will not fail to prompt her.

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 唔！兴许。

**玛伍德夫人.** 你娶她，原指望她养你；既然有本事叫她养得比预想的更丰，何不多留她几年、超乎原定？

**费诺.** 计策，计策呢。

**玛伍德夫人.** 把你太太的行径，抖给老太太；扬言要休了她！——老太太疼她，为保她的名声，什么条件都肯讲。就掐着这场骗局戳穿的当口抖出来。老太太必怒到没了边，在那个节骨眼上，外甥女、家私，一总牺牲个干净。给她续火的事，交给我：她的戏一塌，我保管给她提词。

===Original===
Fainall
Faith, this has an appearance.
Mrs. Marwood
I’m sorry I hinted to my lady to endeavour a match between Millamant and Sir Wilfull; that may be an obstacle.
Fainall
Oh, for that matter, leave me to manage him; I’ll disable him for that, he will drink like a Dane. After dinner I’ll set his hand in.70
Mrs. Marwood
Well, how do you stand affected towards your lady?71

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 说实话，这事有模样。

**玛伍德夫人.** 只悔我向老太太提过，撮合米勒曼特同威尔福爵士；那怕要成个障碍。

**费诺.** 哦，那桩交给我；我保管废了他的功——他喝起酒来，赛过丹麦人。饭后，我引他上手（70）。

**玛伍德夫人.** 那么，你如今对你那位夫人，是何心肠（71）？

===Original===
Fainall
Why, faith, I’m thinking of it.—Let me see—I am married already; so that’s over. My wife has played the jade with me; well, that’s over too. I never loved her, or if I had, why that would have been over too by this time—jealous of her I cannot be, for I am certain; so there’s an end of jealousy. Weary of her I am and shall be—no, there’s no end of that—no, no, that were too much to hope. Thus far concerning my repose. Now for my reputation: as to my own, I married not for it; so that’s out of the question. And as to my part in my wife’s—why, she had parted with hers before; so, bringing none to me, she can take none from me: ’tis against all rule of play that I should lose to one who has not wherewithal to stake.
Mrs. Marwood
Besides you forget, marriage is honourable.
Fainall
Hum! Faith, and that’s well thought on: marriage is honourable, as you say; and if so, wherefore should cuckoldom be a discredit, being derived from so honourable a root?
Mrs. Marwood
Nay, I know not; if the root be honourable, why not the branches?
Fainall
So, so; why this point’s clear—well, how do we proceed?

===Chinese===
**费诺.** 嗐，实说，我正想着呢。——我看看——婚，我结过了；那一章翻过去了。我太太给我当了婊子；好，那一章也翻过去了。我从没爱过她；就算爱过，到这会儿也翻过去了——醋是吃不成的，因为我拿得准；吃醋一章，完结。腻，我是腻了、也要腻下去——不，这一章没有完结——不不，完结了，就太便宜了。我的安寝，账目至此。再说名声：我自家那份，婚不是为它结的；不在话下。至于我在她那份里的股份——嗐，她那份早出手了；她既没带来一股，也就抽不走我一股：赌规不许可——哪有输给一个连赌注都拿不出的对手。

**玛伍德夫人.** 再说，你忘了，婚姻是体面的。

**费诺.** 唔！是了，这话提得好：婚姻是体面的，如你所说；既然如此，王八之名既从这么体面的根上发出来，何以倒成了污点？

**玛伍德夫人.** 嗐，我哪知道；根既体面，枝叶何不体面？

**费诺.** 是了，是了；这一条辩论明白——好，下一步怎么走？

===Original===
Mrs. Marwood
I will contrive a letter which shall be delivered to my lady at the time when that rascal who is to act Sir Rowland is with her. It shall come as from an unknown hand—for the less I appear to know of the truth the better I can play the incendiary. Besides, I would not have Foible provoked if I could help it, because, you know, she knows some passages. Nay, I expect all will come out. But let the mine be sprung first, and then I care not if I am discovered.
Fainall
If the worst come to the worst—I’ll turn my wife to grass. I have already a deed of settlement of the best part of her estate, which I wheedled out of her, and that you shall partake at least.
Mrs. Marwood
I hope you are convinced that I hate Mirabell now? You’ll be no more jealous?
Fainall
Jealous! no—by this kiss—let husbands be jealous, but let the lover still believe; or if he doubt, let it be only to endear his pleasure, and prepare the joy that follows, when he proves his mistress true. But let husbands’ doubts convert to endless jealousy; or if they have belief, let it corrupt to superstition and blind credulity. I am single and will herd no more with ’em. True, I wear the badge, but I’ll disown the order. And since I take my leave of ’em, I care not if I leave ’em a common motto to their common crest.
All husbands must or pain or shame endure;
The wise too jealous are, fools too secure.
*Exeunt.*

===Chinese===
**玛伍德夫人.** 我来伪造一封信，掐着那个要扮罗兰爵士的无赖，陪在老太太身边的时辰送到。落款无名——因为我对真相装得越浑，越放得开手脚去放火。再者，能不惹福伊布尔，就不惹，你晓得，她手里捏着几段旧事。不，我料着迟早全要兜出来。只是先得把地雷引爆；到时候，败露不败露，我都无所谓了。

**费诺.** 真到最坏的一步——我放我太太吃草去。她产业里顶肥的一块，让与契约早攥在我手里，是我连哄带骗弄出来的；那一份，你至少好分上一股。

**玛伍德夫人.** 如今，你总信了我恨米兰芒特罢？不再吃醋了？

**费诺.** 吃醋！不——凭这一吻——让丈夫们吃醋去；情人，只管信。就算疑，也只为给欢愉添味，好铺垫随后证得情人心诚时，那一番狂喜。至于丈夫们的疑心，任它烂成没完的醋；他们若信，任它霉成迷信、瞎子般的轻信。我如今独身一个，再不同他们合群。不错，徽章我戴着，可这门会籍，我不认了。既然就此别过，我就送他们一句通用的题铭，配他们那顶通用的纹章罢——

天下做丈夫的，苦或辱总要受一遭；
智者疑心忒重，痴人提防忒少。

*（同下。）*

## Act IV / 第四幕

### Scene I / 第一场

===Original===
A room in **Lady Wishfort’s** house.

***Lady Wishfort** and **Foible**.*

===Chinese===
威什福特夫人家中一室。

*（**威什福特夫人**与**福伊布尔**在场。）*

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Is Sir Rowland coming, say’st thou, Foible? And are things in order?
Foible
Yes, madam. I have put wax lights in the sconces, and placed the footmen in a row in the hall, in their best liveries, with the coachman and postillion to fill up the equipage.
Lady Wishfort
Have you pulvilled the coachman and postillion, that they may not stink of the stable when Sir Rowland comes by?
Foible
Yes, madam.
Lady Wishfort
And are the dancers and the music ready, that he may be entertained in all points with correspondence to his passion?
Foible
All is ready, madam.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 罗兰爵士这就到了，你说，福伊布尔？各项事情都停当了？

**福伊布尔.** 停当了，夫人。壁灯盏里插了亮蜡，男仆们在大厅里站成一排，都穿顶好的号衣，车夫同马前骑手也点齐，凑足整套扈从。

**威什福特夫人.** 车夫同马前骑手，你给他们熏过香粉没有？免得罗兰爵士打跟前过，他们一身马厩臭。

**福伊布尔.** 熏过了，夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 舞师同乐师也都齐了？好叫他桩桩件件受着款待，处处应得上他那股情热？

**福伊布尔.** 都齐了，夫人。

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
And—well—and how do I look, Foible?
Foible
Most killing well, madam.
Lady Wishfort
Well, and how shall I receive him? In what figure shall I give his heart the first impression? There is a great deal in the first impression. Shall I sit?—no, I won’t sit—I’ll walk—aye, I’ll walk from the door upon his entrance, and then turn full upon him—no, that will be too sudden. I’ll lie—aye, I’ll lie down—I’ll receive him in my little dressing-room; there’s a couch—yes, yes, I’ll give the first impression on a couch—I won’t lie neither, but loll and lean upon one elbow, with one foot a little dangling off, jogging in a thoughtful way—yes—and then as soon as he appears, start, aye, start and be surprised, and rise to meet him in a pretty disorder—yes—oh, nothing is more alluring than a levee from a couch in some confusion. It shows the foot to advantage, and furnishes with blushes and re-composing airs beyond comparison. Hark! There’s a coach.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 还有——嗯——我这副模样怎样，福伊布尔？

**福伊布尔.** 销魂夺命地好，夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 那么，我该怎么接他？摆一副什么身段，去给他那颗心头一个印象？头一个印象里名堂大着呢。我坐着？——不，不坐——我走动——对，他一进门我就打门口迎上去，再整个人转过来对着他——不，那太突然了。我躺着——对，躺下——就在我的小梳妆室里接他；那儿有一张卧榻——对对，头一个印象就在卧榻上给——也不真躺，斜倚着，撑一只胳膊肘，一只脚搭在榻沿外头，若有所思地轻轻颠着——对——等他一露面，惊起，对，惊起来，满脸讶色，起身迎他，带三分可人的凌乱——对——哦，再没有比一场微含狼狈的卧榻接见更撩人的了。最显脚的好处，又平白奉上红晕同重整衣香鬓影的风情，无与伦比。听！马车到了。

===Original===
Foible
’Tis he, madam.
Lady Wishfort
Oh dear—has my nephew made his addresses to Millamant? I ordered him.
Foible
Sir Wilfull is set in to drinking, madam, in the parlour.
Lady Wishfort
Ods my life, I’ll send him to her. Call her down, Foible; bring her hither. I’ll send him as I go—when they are together, then come to me, Foible, that I may not be too long alone with Sir Rowland.
*Exit.*
*Enter **Mrs. Millamant** and **Mrs. Fainall**.*

===Chinese===
**福伊布尔.** 是他，夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 哎呀——我侄儿向米勒曼特开了口没有？我是吩咐过他的。

**福伊布尔.** 威尔福爵士在客厅里坐定灌上酒了，夫人。

**威什福特夫人.** 我的命！我这就把他打发到她那儿去。把她叫下来，福伊布尔；领她到这儿来。我走的时候顺路遣他过去——他们一到了一处，你就来回报我，福伊布尔，省得我同罗兰爵士独处得太久。

*（下。）*

*（**米勒曼特**与**费诺夫人**上。）*

===Original===
Foible
Madam, I stayed here to tell your ladyship that Mr. Mirabell has waited this half hour for an opportunity to talk with you; though my lady’s orders were to leave you and Sir Wilfull together. Shall I tell Mr. Mirabell that you are at leisure?
Mrs. Millamant
No—what would the dear man have? I am thoughtful and would amuse myself—bid him come another time.
There never yet was woman made,
Nor shall, but to be cursed.
*Repeating and walking about.*
That’s hard!
Mrs. Fainall
You are very fond of Sir John Suckling72 today, Millamant, and the poets.
Mrs. Millamant
He? Aye, and filthy verses—so I am.

===Chinese===
**福伊布尔.** 小姐，我特意在这儿守着，为回您一声：米兰芒特先生等了这半个钟头，只为寻个机会同您说话；虽说夫人的吩咐，是叫您同威尔福爵士独处一处。要不要回米兰芒特先生一声，说您得空了？

**米勒曼特.** 不——这位亲爱的人儿还要什么？我正想着心事，要自遣自遣——叫他改天再来罢。

自古就没有造出过女人，
将来也不会——除非造来给人咒。

*（且行且吟。）*

这话可狠！

**费诺夫人.** 你今天倒同萨克林爵士（72）亲热得很，米勒曼特，还同那班诗人。

**米勒曼特.** 他？是呀，还有那些下流诗句——可不是么。

===Original===
Foible
Sir Wilfull is coming, madam. Shall I send Mr. Mirabell away?
Mrs. Millamant
Aye, if you please, Foible, send him away—or send him hither—just as you will, dear Foible.—I think I’ll see him—shall I? Aye, let the wretch come.
*Exit **Foible**.*
Thyrsis, a youth of the inspired train.73
*Repeating.*
Dear Fainall, entertain Sir Wilfull—thou hast philosophy to undergo a fool; thou art married and hast patience—I would confer with my own thoughts.
Mrs. Fainall
I am obliged to you that you would make me your proxy in this affair; but I have business of my own.
*Enter **Sir Wilfull**.*

===Chinese===
**福伊布尔.** 威尔福爵士来了，小姐。要不要打发米兰芒特先生走？

**米勒曼特.** 唔，随你的便，福伊布尔，打发走罢——或者领他上来——全由你，亲爱的福伊布尔。——我想还是见一见——见不见呢？唔，叫那可怜虫来罢。

*（**福伊布尔**下。）*

瑟西斯，灵感一族的少年（73）。

*（吟。）*

亲爱的费诺，替我招架威尔福爵士——你有哲学，受得住一个傻子；你结过婚，有的是耐性——我要同我自己的念头密谈密谈。

**费诺夫人.** 承您抬举，要拿我当这桩事的替身；只是我自有我自己的事。

*（**威尔福爵士**上。）*

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
O Sir Wilfull, you are come at the critical instant. There’s your mistress up to the ears in love and contemplation; pursue your point, now or never.
Sir Wilful
Yes, my aunt will have it so—I would gladly have been encouraged with a bottle or two, because I’m somewhat wary at first, before I am acquainted. *This while **Mrs. Millamant** walks about repeating to herself.* But I hope, after a time, I shall break my mind—that is, upon further acquaintance—so for the present, cousin, I’ll take my leave. If so be you’ll be so kind to make my excuse, I’ll return to my company—
Mrs. Fainall
Oh, fie, Sir Wilfull! What, you must not be daunted.
Sir Wilful
Daunted? No, that’s not it; it is not so much for that—for if so be that I set on’t I’ll do’t. But only for the present, ’tis sufficient till further acquaintance, that’s all—your servant.
Mrs. Fainall
Nay, I’ll swear you shall never lose so favourable an opportunity, if I can help it. I’ll leave you together and lock the door.
*Exit.*

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 哦，威尔福爵士，您来得正是节骨眼上。您的意中人正一头扎进情思冥想里，没到耳朵也到了眉毛；进军罢，过了这村没这店。

**威尔福爵士.** 是，我姨妈非要这样——我本乐意先受一两瓶酒的壮胆，因为起头、还没混熟之前，我多少有些拘谨。（这一会儿，**米勒曼特**只管踱来踱去，自吟自哦。）不过我希望，过些时候，我能开得了口——就是说，等再熟些——所以眼前，表姐，我告退了。若蒙您好心替我告个罪，我就回我那班酒友那儿去——

**费诺夫人.** 哎呀，呸，威尔福爵士！怎么，您可不能打退堂鼓。

**威尔福爵士.** 退堂鼓？不，不是那么回事；倒不全为这个——因为只要我打定了主意，我就办。只是眼前嘛，等再熟些再办也不迟，如此而已——您安。

**费诺夫人.** 不，我起誓，只要我拦得住，您就休想错过这么个好时机。我把你们俩留在一处，还要把门锁上。

*（下。）*

===Original===
Sir Wilful
Nay, nay, cousin—I have forgot my gloves. What d’ye do?—S’heart, a’has locked the door indeed, I think—nay, cousin Fainall, open the door—pshaw, what a vixen trick is this? Nay, now a has seen me too.—Cousin, I made bold to pass through as it were—I think this door’s enchanted.
Mrs. Millamant
*Repeating.*
I prithee spare me, gentle boy,
Press me no more for that slight toy.74
Sir Wilful
Anan? Cousin, your servant.
Mrs. Millamant
*Repeating.*
That foolish trifle of a heart.
Sir Wilfull!
Sir Wilful
Yes—your servant. No offence, I hope, cousin?
Mrs. Millamant
*Repeating.*
I swear it will not do its part,
Though thou dost thine, employ’st thy power and art.
Natural, easy Suckling!

===Chinese===
**威尔福爵士.** 嗳，嗳，表姐——我的手套忘拿了。你这是做什么？——哎呀，我看这回真锁上了——嗳，费诺表姐，开门——呸，这算哪门子泼妇促狭劲儿？嗳，这下她可瞧见我了。——表姐，我本是斗胆打这儿借个道——我看这门是中了邪了。

**米勒曼特.**（吟。）

我求你饶了我，温柔少年，
别再逼我讨那件轻巧的玩物（74）。

**威尔福爵士.** 嗯哼？表妹，您安。

**米勒曼特.**（吟。）

那颗唤作心的傻玩意儿。

——威尔福爵士！

**威尔福爵士.** 是——您安。没有见怪的意思罢，表妹？

**米勒曼特.**（吟。）

我起誓它不肯尽它的本分，
任你尽你那份，任你使尽威能同手段。

——自然又流畅，好个萨克林！

===Original===
Sir Wilful
Anan? Suckling? No such suckling neither, cousin, nor stripling: I thank Heaven I’m no minor.
Mrs. Millamant
Ah, rustic, ruder than Gothic!
Sir Wilful
Well, well, I shall understand your lingo one of these days, cousin; in the meanwhile I must answer in plain English.
Mrs. Millamant
Have you any business with me, Sir Wilfull?
Sir Wilful
Not at present, cousin—yes, I made bold to see, to come and know if that how you were disposed to fetch a walk this evening, if so be that I might not be troublesome, I would have sought a walk with you.
Mrs. Millamant
A walk! What then?
Sir Wilful
Nay, nothing—only for the walk’s sake, that’s all.

===Chinese===
**威尔福爵士.** 啊？吃奶的？这儿可没有什么吃奶的娃，表妹，也没有什么乳臭小子：我感谢上天，我早不是个没成年的人了。

**米勒曼特.** 啊，乡气，比哥特人还粗野三分的乡气！

**威尔福爵士.** 好罢，好罢，早晚有一天，表妹，你们这路行话我总会听得懂；眼下我只好拿大白话回敬。

**米勒曼特.** 您找我可有事，威尔福爵士？

**威尔福爵士.** 眼下没有，表妹——有，我斗胆跑这一趟，是来探问，您今儿傍晚可有意出来走走；只要不讨人嫌，我本想陪着您散一回步。

**米勒曼特.** 散步！然后呢？

**威尔福爵士.** 嗐，没什么——单为散步而散步，如此而已。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
I nauseate walking: ’tis a country diversion; I loathe the country and everything that relates to it.
Sir Wilful
Indeed! Ha! Look ye, look ye, you do? Nay, ’tis like you may—here are choice of pastimes here in town, as plays and the like, that must be confessed indeed.
Mrs. Millamant
Ah, *l’étourdi*! I hate the town too.
Sir Wilful
Dear heart, that’s much—ha! that you should hate ’em both! Ha! ’tis like you may! There are some can’t relish the town, and others can’t away with the country—’tis like you may be one of those, cousin.
Mrs. Millamant
Ha! ha! ha! Yes, ’tis like I may.—You have nothing further to say to me?
Sir Wilful
Not at present, cousin.—’Tis like when I have an opportunity to be more private—I may break my mind in some measure—I conjecture you partly guess—however, that’s as time shall try. But spare to speak and spare to speed, as they say.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 我一想走路就作呕：那是乡下的消遣；我厌恶乡下，还有同乡下沾边的一切。

**威尔福爵士.** 当真！哈！您瞧，您瞧，您厌恶？嗐，多半是了——城里这儿消遣可挑可拣，好比看戏之类，这倒真得认。

**米勒曼特.** 啊，冒失鬼（l’étourdi）！城里我也厌恶。

**威尔福爵士.** 了不得，这可奇了——哈！您竟两样都厌恶！哈！多半是了！有人受用不了城里，也有人消受不了乡下——表妹，您多半正兼着这两路。

**米勒曼特.** 哈！哈！哈！是，多半是。——您没有别的话要同我说了？

**威尔福爵士.** 眼下没有，表妹。——往后赶上个机会、僻静些——我兴许能把心思吐露个几分——我猜您也猜着几分——横竖，日子自会见分晓。不过俗话说：省下开口，省下栽跟头。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
If it is of no great importance, Sir Wilfull, you will oblige me to leave me: I have just now a little business—
Sir Wilful
Enough, enough, cousin. Yes, yes, all a case.75—when you’re disposed: now’s as well as another time; and another time as well as now. All’s one for that—yes, yes; if your concerns call you, there’s no haste: it will keep cold as they say.—Cousin, your servant—I think this door’s locked.
Mrs. Millamant
You may go this way, sir.
Sir Wilful
Your servant; then with your leave I’ll return to my company.
*Exit.*
Mrs. Millamant
Aye, aye; ha! ha! ha!
Like Phoebus sung the no less amorous boy.76
*Enter **Mirabell**.*

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 这若不是什么了不得的大事，威尔福爵士，就劳您成全，别搅扰我了：我眼下有点小事——

**威尔福爵士.** 够了，够了，表妹。是，是，横竖一回事（75）。——您几时得空几时说：眼下同一个时辰一样好；一个时辰同眼下也一样好。横竖一回事——是，是；您的事召唤您，就不急：俗话说，搁凉了也坏不了。——表妹，您安——我看这门还锁着。

**米勒曼特.** 您打这边走，先生。

**威尔福爵士.** 您安；那么恕我告退，回我那班酒友去了。

*（下。）*

**米勒曼特.** 是，是；哈！哈！哈！

像福玻斯歌唱过的，那同样多情的少年（76）。

*（**米兰芒特**上。）*

===Original===
Mirabell
“Like Daphne she, as lovely and as coy.” Do you lock yourself up from me, to make my search more curious? Or is this pretty artifice contrived, to signify that here the chase must end, and my pursuit be crowned, for you can fly no further?
Mrs. Millamant
Vanity! No—I’ll fly and be followed to the last moment; though I am upon the very verge of matrimony, I expect you should solicit me as much as if I were wavering at the grate of a monastery, with one foot over the threshold. I’ll be solicited to the very lastn nay, and afterwards.
Mirabell
What, after the last?
Mrs. Millamant
Oh, I should think I was poor and had nothing to bestow if I were reduced to an inglorious ease, and freed from the agreeable fatigues of solicitation.
Mirabell
But do not you know that when favours are conferred upon instant and tedious solicitation, that they diminish in their value, and that both the giver loses the grace, and the receiver lessens his pleasure?
Mrs. Millamant
It may be in things of common application,77 but never, sure, in love. Oh, I hate a lover that can dare to think he draws a moment’s air independent on the bounty of his mistress. There is not so impudent a thing in nature as the saucy look of an assured man confident of success: the pedantic arrogance of a very husband has not so pragmatical an air. Ah! I’ll never marry, unless I am first made sure of my will and pleasure.
Mirabell
Would you have ’em both before marriage? Or will you be contented with the first now, and stay for the other till after grace?

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 「她像达佛涅，同样可爱，同样拿乔。」您把自个儿锁起来避着我，是要叫我这场寻访更添一层金贵？还是这番玲珑的机关，专为示意：追逐到此必须收场，我的苦追就此加冕——因为您再也飞不动了？

**米勒曼特.** 虚荣！不——我要一路逃，一路有人追，追到最后一刻；哪怕我已站在婚姻的门坎上，我也指望您苦苦哀求，同我站在修道院的栅门前、一只脚已跨过门槛那时一样。要人求到最后一刻——不，过后还要求。

**米兰芒特.** 什么，过了最后一刻还求？

**米勒曼特.** 哦，我若被贬进一种不体面的安逸，从求婚那桩可人的辛苦里放了出来，我就该想：我穷了，一无所有可赏了。

**米兰芒特.** 可您岂不晓得：恩宠若是经了当场又冗长的苦求才赐下来，身价就要折损；赐的人折了恩意，受的人也减了乐趣？

**米勒曼特.** 寻常通用的事物上（77）兴许如此，可在爱情里，我敢说，断乎不会。哦，我最恨那种情人：竟敢以为自己喘的一口气，都不靠女主人的恩赏。天生天化，再没有比这更不要脸的东西了——一个稳操胜券的男人那副放肆的得意嘴脸；连十足的丈夫那股迂腐的自大，也没这样好为人师的酸气。啊！我绝不嫁人，除非先拿得稳我的意愿同我的欢愉。

**米兰芒特.** 您要两样都在婚前到手？还是眼下先领头一样，另一样且等谢过饭恩再领？

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Ah, don’t be impertinent.—My dear liberty, shall I leave thee? My faithful solitude, my darling contemplation, must I bid you then adieu? Aye-h, adieu—my morning thoughts, agreeable wakings, indolent slumbers, all ye *douceurs*, ye *sommeils du matin*,78 adieu?—I can’t do’t, ’tis more than impossible—positively, Mirabell, I’ll lie abed in a morning as long as I please.
Mirabell
Then I’ll get up in a morning as early as I please.
Mrs. Millamant
Ah! Idle creature, get up when you will—and d’ye hear, I won’t be called names after I’m married; positively I won’t be called names.
Mirabell
Names!
Mrs. Millamant
Aye, as wife, spouse, my dear, joy, jewel, love, sweetheart, and the rest of that nauseous cant, in which men and their wives are so fulsomely familiar—I shall never bear that—good Mirabell, don’t let us be familiar or fond, nor kiss before folks, like my Lady Fadler and Sir Francis; nor go to Hyde Park together the first Sunday in a new chariot, to provoke eyes and whispers, and then never be seen there together again, as if we were proud of one another the first week, and ashamed of one another ever after. Let us never visit together, nor go to a play together, but let us be very strange and well-bred. Let us be as strange as if we had been married a great while, and as well-bred as if we were not married at all.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 啊，别胡搅蛮缠。——我亲爱的自由，难道要撇下你了？我忠实的清静，我心爱的遐想，难道就此同你们道别了？唉——别了——我的晨间思绪、可人的初醒、慵懒的酣睡，你们这一干甜头（douceurs）、这晨来的酣眠（sommeils du matin）（78），就此别过了？——我办不到，这比办不到还办不到——明明白白，米兰芒特，我早上爱赖到几点，就赖到几点。

**米兰芒特.** 那我早上爱起多早，就起多早。

**米勒曼特.** 啊！懒骨头，随你什么时候起——还有，你听着，我婚后不许人拿那些名目叫我；断然不许拿那些名目叫我。

**米兰芒特.** 名目！

**米勒曼特.** 是呀，什么老婆、媳妇、亲爱的、心肝、宝贝、肉儿、甜心，还有那一整套叫人作呕的行话——男人同他们的太太，就是这么腻腻歪歪地你亲我热——我一辈子受不得那个——好米兰芒特，咱们别亲昵、别腻歪，也别当着人亲嘴，学法德勒夫人同弗朗西斯爵士那一对；也别头一个礼拜天坐了新马车双双上海德公园，招人眼目、惹人嚼舌，往后却再不一同在那儿露面——好像头一个礼拜还拿对方当体面，往后一辈子倒拿对方当羞耻。咱们永不一同拜客，也不一同看戏，要做得生分而文雅。生分，像结婚了大半辈子；文雅，像压根儿没结过婚。

===Original===
Mirabell
Have you any more conditions to offer? Hitherto your demands are pretty reasonable.
Mrs. Millamant
Trifles!—As liberty to pay and receive visits to and from whom I please; to write and receive letters, without interrogatories or wry faces on your part; to wear what I please, and choose conversation with regard only to my own taste; to have no obligation upon me to converse with wits that I don’t like, because they are your acquaintance, or to be intimate with fools, because they may be your relations. Come to dinner when I please, dine in my dressing-room when I’m out of humour, without giving a reason. To have my closet inviolate; to be sole empress of my tea-table, which you must never presume to approach without first asking leave. And lastly, wherever I am, you shall always knock at the door before you come in. These articles subscribed, if I continue to endure you a little longer, I may by degrees dwindle into a wife.
Mirabell
Your bill of fare is something advanced in this latter account.—Well, have I liberty to offer conditions—that when you are dwindled into a wife, I may not be beyond measure enlarged into a husband?
Mrs. Millamant
You have free leave: propose your utmost, speak and spare not.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 您还有别的条款要提么？到如今为止，您开的价都还算通情理。

**米勒曼特.** 芝麻绿豆！——诸如：出门拜客、在家会客，随我高兴；写信收信，不许你盘问，也不许你皱眉；穿戴随我的意，拣谈话的伴儿只依我自个儿的口味；不因为是你相识的才子，就担着情分勉强我同我讨厌的人应酬；也不因为兴许是你亲戚，就逼我同蠢货亲热。我爱几时上桌就几时上桌，心里不痛快，就在梳妆室独自用饭，不消交代缘故。我的小闺房不许擅入；茶桌我是独一无二的皇后，你不先行请准，永远不得近前。末了一条：不论我在哪儿，你进门之前必得先敲门。这几条画了押，我再勉强将就你些时日，兴许能一步一步缩水，缩成一个妻子。

**米兰芒特.** 您这份菜单，越列到后头价钱越见涨。——好，我可不可以也提几条——免得您缩水成了妻子之后，我胀过了头，胀成一个丈夫？

**米勒曼特.** 尽管提：把您顶苛刻的都端出来，只管说，别留情。

===Original===
Mirabell
I thank you.—*Imprimis*, then, I covenant that your acquaintance be general; that you admit no sworn confidant or intimate of your own sex; no she friend to screen her affairs under your countenance, and tempt you to make trial of a mutual secrecy. No decoy-duck to wheedle you a fop-scrambling to the play in a mask—then bring you home in a pretended fright, when you think you shall be found out—and rail at me for missing the play, and disappointing the frolic which you had to pick me up and prove my constancy.
Mrs. Millamant
Detestable *imprimis*! I go to the play in a mask!
Mirabell
*Item*, I article, that you continue to like your own face as long as I shall, and while it passes current with me, that you endeavour not to new coin it. To which end, together with all vizards for the day, I prohibit all masks for the night, made of oiled skins and I know not what—hog’s bones, hare’s gall, pig water, and the marrow of a roasted cat.79 In short, I forbid all commerce with the gentlewomen in what-d’ye-call-it court. *Item*, I shut my doors against all bawds with baskets, and pennyworths of muslin, china, fans, atlases, etc.—*Item*, when you shall be breeding—
Mrs. Millamant
Ah, name it not.
Mirabell
Which may be presumed, with a blessing on our endeavours—
Mrs. Millamant
Odious endeavours!
Mirabell
I denounce against all strait lacing, squeezing for a shape, till you mould my boy’s head like a sugar-loaf, and instead of a man-child, make me father to a crooked billet. Lastly, to the dominion of the tea-table I submit—but with proviso, that you exceed not in your province, but restrain yourself to native and simple tea-table drinks, as tea, chocolate, and coffee. As likewise to genuine and authorised tea-table talk—such as mending of fashions, spoiling reputations, railing at absent friends, and so forth—but that on no account you encroach upon the men’s prerogative, and presume to drink healths, or toast fellows; for prevention of which, I banish all foreign forces, all auxiliaries to the tea-table, as orange-brandy, all aniseed, cinnamon, citron, and Barbados waters,80 together with ratafia and the most noble spirit of clary—but for cowslip-wine, poppy-water, and all dormitives, those I allow.—These provisos admitted, in other things I may prove a tractable and complying husband.

===Chinese===
**米兰芒特.** 谢您。——那末，*首条*（Imprimis），我约定：您的相识须是泛泛之交；不得收纳同性中立过誓的心腹、闺中腻友；不得有什么女知己，借您的脸面遮掩她的私情，勾着您也试一试彼此守密的滋味。不得有诱鸭，哄得您同哪位花花公子一道、蒙着面罩连滚带爬挤去看戏——再装出一副吓破了胆的样子把您送回家，只怕事情败露——回头还骂我不该误了那场戏，败了那场兴——那场专为捞我出来、验一验我忠心的顽意儿。

**米勒曼特.** 可恶的首条！我蒙着面罩挤去看戏！

**米兰芒特.** *其二*，我约定：您得同我一样长久地喜欢您自个儿的脸；只要它在我这儿还通行无阻，您就别费心思回炉重铸。为此，连同白天用的一切面罩，我一并禁止一切夜间面膜——油皮子做的，天晓得还和了什么——猪骨头、兔子的胆囊、猪尿水、烤猫的骨髓（79）。总而言之，那座「叫什么名字来着宫」里的太太们，我一概禁止来往。*其三*，我闭门谢客：一切提篮子的皮条婆，连同一便士一便士论卖的细布、瓷器、扇子、地图册等等，一概挡驾。——*其四*，等您日后有了身孕——

**米勒曼特.** 啊，这个提也休提。

**米兰芒特.** 这事原可指望——只要咱们的努力蒙天祝福——

**米勒曼特.** 可恨的努力！

**米兰芒特.** 我严词声讨一切紧束捆身、为身段勒腰：勒得我儿子的脑袋尖成一块糖面包，不给我生个大胖男婴，倒叫我当上一根歪柴棍的爹。末一条：茶桌的统治权，我甘拜下风——但有言在先：您不得越出行省，须安分于茶桌本土的、素朴的饮料，如茶、巧克力、咖啡。同样，只许谈地道而经认可的茶桌话题——诸如修改时装呀、败坏名声呀、编排不在场的朋友呀，等等——但无论如何不得僭越男人们的特权，不得妄图敬酒、祝颂爷们；为防微杜渐，我驱逐一切外国军队、一切驰援茶桌的辅助部队，如橘子白兰地、各色茴香酒、肉桂酒、香橼酒、巴巴多斯水（80），连同拉塔菲亚同最高贵的鼠尾草烈酒——至于樱草酒、罂粟水同一切安眠药水，那些我准了。——这些但书若蒙俯准，其余事项，我或可做一个驯顺听话的好丈夫。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Oh, horrid provisos! Filthy strong waters! I toast fellows, odious men! I hate your odious provisos.
Mirabell
Then we’re agreed. Shall I kiss your hand upon the contract? And here comes one to be a witness to the sealing of the deed.
*Enter **Mrs. Fainall**.*
Mrs. Millamant
Fainall, what shall I do? Shall I have him? I think I must have him.
Mrs. Fainall
Aye, aye, take him, take him, what should you do?
Mrs. Millamant
Well then—I’ll take my death I’m in a horrid fright—Fainall, I shall never say it—well—I think—I’ll endure you.
Mrs. Fainall
Fie, fie, have him, and tell him so in plain terms: for I am sure you have a mind to him.
Mrs. Millamant
Are you? I think I have—and the horrid man looks as if he thought so too—well, you ridiculous thing you, I’ll have you—I won’t be kissed, nor I won’t be thanked—here, kiss my hand though.—So, hold your tongue now, don’t say a word.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 哦，可怕的但书！下作的烈酒！我会去祝颂爷们，可恶的男人！我恨死你这些可恶的但书。

**米兰芒特.** 那么咱们讲定了。可容我在这纸契约上，吻您的手？正巧来了位见证，好看这纸文书用印。

*（**费诺夫人**上。）*

**米勒曼特.** 费诺，我可怎么办？要不要收了他？我看非收不可。

**费诺夫人.** 是，是，收下他，收下他，不然您还想怎样？

**米勒曼特.** 那么着——我敢拿命赌，我怕得没个底——费诺，这话我说不出口——好罢——我想——我就将就你罢。

**费诺夫人.** 呸，呸，收了他，直截了当告诉他：因为我拿得准您心里有他。

**米勒曼特.** 拿得准么？我想是有——这可怕的男人瞧那副神气，像是也这么想——好罢，你这个可笑的东西，我收了你——不许亲我，也不许谢我——喏，这只手倒许你亲一亲。——好了，闭嘴，一个字也别说。

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
Mirabell, there’s a necessity for your obedience: you have neither time to talk nor stay. My mother is coming; and in my conscience if she should see you, would fall into fits, and maybe not recover time enough to return to Sir Rowland, who, as Foible tells me, is in a fair way to succeed. Therefore spare your ecstasies for another occasion, and slip down the back stairs, where Foible waits to consult you.
Mrs. Millamant
Aye, go, go. In the meantime I suppose you have said something to please me.
Mirabell
I am all obedience.
*Exit.*
Mrs. Fainall
Yonder Sir Wilfull’s drunk, and so noisy that my mother has been forced to leave Sir Rowland to appease him; but he answers her only with singing and drinking—what they may have done by this time I know not, but Petulant and he were upon quarrelling as I came by.
Mrs. Millamant
Well, if Mirabell should not make a good husband, I am a lost thing: for I find I love him violently.
Mrs. Fainall
So it seems; for you mind not what’s said to you.—If you doubt him, you had best take up with Sir Wilfull.
Mrs. Millamant
How can you name that superannuated lubber? foh!
*Enter **Witwoud**.*

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 米兰芒特，这回您非服从不可：连说话的工夫、逗留的工夫都没有了。我母亲来了；凭良心说，她要撞见您，当场就要厥过去，只怕缓不过劲儿来，赶不回去陪罗兰爵士——据福伊布尔说，他那头正是一帆风顺。所以您那阵狂喜，留着改日再发作，悄悄打后楼梯溜下去，福伊布尔在那儿候着，有事同您商议。

**米勒曼特.** 是，去罢，去罢。不过我想，方才您总算说了几句讨我喜欢的话。

**米兰芒特.** 我通身上下都是恭顺。

*（下。）*

**费诺夫人.** 那边威尔福爵士喝醉了，闹得那么凶，我母亲只得撂下罗兰爵士去安抚他；可他只拿唱歌同灌酒回敬她——这会儿他们闹到哪一步，我不知道，只是我打那儿过的时候，他同佩特兰特眼看就要吵起来。

**米勒曼特.** 唉，米兰芒特若做不成一个好丈夫，我就毁了：因为我发觉我爱他爱得凶。

**费诺夫人.** 看得出；人家同你说话，你都没听见。——你要是信不过他，顶好改接威尔福爵士的茬。

**米勒曼特.** 你怎么提得起那个超龄的粗胚！呸！

*（**威特伍德**上。）*

===Original===
Mrs. Fainall
So, is the fray made up that you have left ’em?
Witwoud
Left ’em? I could stay no longer—I have laughed like ten Christ’nings. I am tipsy with laughing—if I had stayed any longer I should have burst—I must have been let out and pieced in the sides like an unsized camlet.81 Yes, yes, the fray is composed; my lady came in like a *noli prosequi*,82 and stopped the proceedings.
Mrs. Millamant
What was the dispute?
Witwoud
That’s the jest: there was no dispute. They could neither of ’em speak for rage; and so fell a sputtering at one another like two roasting apples.
*Enter **Petulant**, drunk.*
Witwoud
Now, Petulant? All’s over, all’s well? Gad, my head begins to whim it about—why dost thou not speak? Thou art both as drunk and as mute as a fish.

===Chinese===
**费诺夫人.** 怎么样，吵完了，你才丢下他们？

**威特伍德.** 丢下他们？我实在待不住了——我笑得赶上一连十场婴儿洗礼。我笑醉了——再待下去我就要笑破了——得放我出去，腰身两边打上补丁，像一匹没浆过的驼毛呢（81）。是，是，架吵拢了；夫人进来，像一纸「中止诉讼」（noli prosequi）（82），把官司拦下了。

**米勒曼特.** 争的是什么？

**威特伍德.** 笑就笑在这儿：根本没争。两人气得都说不出话，就这么对着喷唾沫星子，活像两只炉上烤的苹果。

*（**佩特兰特**醉上。）*

**威特伍德.** 怎样，佩特兰特？完事了，太平了？老天，我脑袋开始打转了——你怎么不说话？你又醉又哑，哑得像条鱼。

===Original===
Petulant
Look you, Mrs. Millamant—if you can love me, dear Nymph—say it—and that’s the conclusion—pass on, or pass off—that’s all.
Witwoud
Thou hast uttered volumes, folios, in less than *decimo sexto*, my dear Lacedemonian.83 Sirrah, Petulant, thou art an epitomizer of words.
Petulant
Witwoud—you are an annihilator of sense.
Witwoud
Thou art a retailer of phrases, and dost deal in remnants of remnants, like a maker of pincushions—thou art in truth (metaphorically speaking) a speaker of shorthand.
Petulant
Thou art (without a figure) just one half of an ass, and Baldwin yonder,84 thy half-brother, is the rest.—A Gemini of asses split would make just four of you.85
Witwoud
Thou dost bite, my dear mustard-seed; kiss me for that.
Petulant
Stand off—I’ll kiss no more males—I have kissed your twin yonder in a humour of reconciliation till he *Hiccups.* rises upon my stomach like a radish.

===Chinese===
**佩特兰特.** 您听着，米勒曼特——您若能爱我，亲爱的仙女——就说出口——就此收梢——过下去也罢，走开去也罢——就这么回事。

**威特伍德.** 你吐出来的可是整套书、整架对开本，缩在三十二开里，亲爱的拉栖代梦人（83）。小子，佩特兰特，你是个缩字如金的节略家。

**佩特兰特.** 威特伍德——你是个灭义如扫的湮灭家。

**威特伍德.** 你是个片语零售商，专卖零头里的零头，像个做针插的娘们——说实话（打个比方说），你是速记腔的演说家。

**佩特兰特.** 你（这可不是打比方）恰好是半头驴，那边你那位异母兄弟鲍德温（84），是剩下的那半。——一对双子驴拦腰一劈，正好做出你们四个（85）。

**威特伍德.** 你这话带刺，亲爱的芥菜籽；为这个赏我一吻。

**佩特兰特.** 站开——男的我不亲了——方才讲和那一阵，我同你那位孪生的亲了好久，亲得他——（打嗝。）——在我胃里往上冒，像根小萝卜。

===Original===
Mrs. Millamant
Eh! filthy creature! what was the quarrel?
Petulant
There was no quarrel—there might have been a quarrel.
Witwoud
If there had been words enow between ’em to have expressed provocation, they had gone together by the ears like a pair of castanets.
Petulant
You were the quarrel.
Mrs. Millamant
Me!
Petulant
If I have a humour to quarrel, I can make less matters conclude premises.—If you are not handsome, what then? If I have a humour to prove it? If I shall have my reward, say so; if not, fight for your face the next time yourself—I’ll go sleep.
Witwoud
Do, wrap thyself up like a woodlouse, and dream revenge.—and, hear me, if thou canst learn to write by tomorrow morning, pen me a challenge.—I’ll carry it for thee.
Petulant
Carry your mistress’s monkey a spider!—Go flea dogs and read romances. I’ll go to bed to my maid.
*Exit.*
Mrs. Fainall
He’s horridly drunk.—How came you all in this pickle?
Witwoud
A plot! a plot! to get rid of the knight—your husband’s advice; but he sneaked off.

===Chinese===
**米勒曼特.** 嗳！下作东西！吵的到底是什么？

**佩特兰特.** 没吵架——本可能吵一架。

**威特伍德.** 他们俩之间的话，若够得上表达挑衅，早就扭作一团开打了，劈啪作响，像一副响板。

**佩特兰特.** 吵的就是你。

**米勒曼特.** 我！

**佩特兰特.** 我若来了吵架的兴致，再鸡毛的事也能凑成前提。——您若不标致，那又怎样？我兴致来了偏要论证呢？我若该领赏，就言语一声；不赏，下回您那张脸，您自个儿去打——我睡觉去了。

**威特伍德.** 去罢，学只鼠妇把自个儿蜷起来，梦里报仇去。——还有，听着，明儿早上你若能学会写字，替我草一封挑战书。——我替你捎去。

**佩特兰特.** 给你女主人的猴子送蜘蛛去罢！——捉狗虱、读传奇去罢。我上我家丫头的床去。

*（下。）*

**费诺夫人.** 他醉得不成样子。——你们这一伙怎么闹成这副德行？

**威特伍德.** 一条计！一条计！为的是脱开那位爵士——你家先生出的主意；可他自个儿倒先溜了。

### Scene II / 第二场

===Original===
The dining room in **Lady Wishfort’s** house.

***Sir Wilfull** drunk, **Lady Wishfort**, **Witwoud**, **Mrs. Millamant**, and **Mrs. Fainall**.*
Lady Wishfort
Out upon’t, out upon’t! At years of discretion, and comport yourself at this rantipole rate!
Sir Wilful
No offence, aunt.
Lady Wishfort
Offence! as I’m a person, I’m ashamed of you—foh! How you stink of wine! D’ye think my niece will ever endure such a Borachio!86 You’re an absolute Borachio.
Sir Wilful
Borachio?
Lady Wishfort
At a time when you should commence an amour, and put your best foot foremost—
Sir Wilful
S’heart, an you grutch me your liquor, make a bill—give me more drink, and take my purse—*Sings.*
Prithee fill me the glass,
Till it laugh in my face,
With ale that is potent and mellow;
He that whines for a lass
Is an ignorant ass,
For a bumper has not its fellow.
But if you would have me marry my cousin—say the word, and I’ll do’t—Wilfull will do’t, that’s the word—Wilfull will do’t, that’s my crest—my motto I have forgot.
Lady Wishfort
My nephew’s a little overtaken, cousin—but ’tis drinking your health.—O’ my word, you are obliged to him.

===Chinese===
威什福特夫人家中的饭厅。

*（**威尔福爵士**醉，**威什福特夫人**、**威特伍德**、**米勒曼特**与**费诺夫人**在场。）*

**威什福特夫人.** 呸，羞煞，羞煞！到了懂事的年纪，还这般撒野放泼！

**威尔福爵士.** 没有冒犯的意思，姨妈。

**威什福特夫人.** 冒犯！我以人格担保，你都替我臊得慌——呸！你这一身酒臭！你当我外甥女受得了这么一个酒皮囊（86）！你十足是个酒皮囊。

**威尔福爵士.** 酒皮囊？

**威什福特夫人.** 正当你该着手谈情、该把你顶拿得出的一只脚跨在前头的时候——

**威尔福爵士.** 哎呀，您若舍不得给我酒，记账——只管拿酒来，把我的钱袋拿去——（唱。）

请你替我把盏斟个满，
斟得酒笑上我的脸，
麦酒又烈又醇和；
为个妞儿哭鼻子的，
是头没见识的蠢驴，
一大杯下肚，世间无双。

可你们要叫我娶我表妹——只管发话，我就办——威尔福说办就办，就这句——威尔福说办就办，这是我的纹章——铭词我可忘了。

**威什福特夫人.** 我侄儿有点喝过了头，外甥女——不过他喝的是祝您康健。——真的，您该承他的情。

===Original===
Sir Wilful
In vino veritas, aunt.—If I drunk your health today, cousin—I am a Borachio. But if you have a mind to be married, say the word and send for the piper; Wilfull will do’t. If not, dust it away, and let’s have t’other round.—Tony!—Ods-heart, where’s Tony!—Tony’s an honest fellow, but he spits after a bumper, and that’s a fault—*Sings.*
We’ll drink and we’ll never ha’ done, boys,
Put the glass then around with the sun, boys,
Let Apollo’s example invite us;
For he’s drunk every night,
And that makes him so bright,
That he’s able next morning to light us.
The sun’s a good pimple, an honest soaker, he has a cellar at your antipodes. If I travel, aunt, I touch at your antipodes—your antipodes are a good rascally sort of topsy-turvy fellows. If I had a bumper I’d stand upon my head and drink a health to ’em.—A match or no match, cousin with the hard name?—Aunt, Wilfull will do’t. If she has her maidenhead let her look to ’t; if she has not, let her keep her own counsel in the meantime, and cry out at the nine months’ end.
Mrs. Millamant
Your pardon, madam, I can stay no longer—Sir Wilfull grows very powerful. Eh! how he smells! I shall be overcome if I stay.—Come, cousin.
*Exeunt **Mrs. Millamant** and **Mrs. Fainall**.*

===Chinese===
**威尔福爵士.** 酒后吐真言（In vino veritas），姨妈。——表妹，我今天若敬过您的酒——我就是个酒皮囊。可您若有心嫁人，言语一声，叫人把风笛手请来；威尔福说办就办。不然，干掉它，再来一轮。——托尼！——哎呀个天，托尼哪儿去了！——托尼是个老实人，可他灌完一大杯要啐一口，那是个毛病——（唱。）

咱们喝呀，喝个没完，伙计们，
传杯跟着太阳转，伙计们，
阿波罗的榜样邀了咱们；
他夜夜喝得醉醺醺，
才照得他这般亮堂堂，
第二天清早还能替咱们点灯。

太阳是个好疙瘩、一位老实的灌酒客，他在你们脚底下对跖的地方还藏着座酒窖。姨妈，我日后游历，定要在您的对跖点靠一靠岸——您那些对跖人，是一伙满不错的、倒栽葱的泼皮货。手里若有一大杯，我就倒竖蜻蜓，敬他们一个。——成不成亲，这位名字拗口的表妹？——姨妈，威尔福说办就办。她的贞操若还在，叫她自个儿看好；若不在，这会子且守着自个儿的秘密，九个月满头再叫唤罢。

**米勒曼特.** 恕我失陪，夫人，我待不下去了——威尔福爵士的威风越长越大了。哎！他这股味儿！再待我就要熏倒。——走罢，表姐。

*（**米勒曼特**与**费诺夫人**同下。）*

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Smells! He would poison a tallow-chandler and his family! Beastly creature, I know not what to do with him.—Travel, quotha; aye, travel, travel, get thee gone, get thee but far enough, to the Saracens, or the Tartars, or the Turks!—for thou art not fit to live in a Christian commonwealth, thou beastly pagan!
Sir Wilful
Turks, no; no Turks, aunt: your Turks are infidels, and believe not in the grape. Your Muhammadan, your Mussulman is a dry stinkard—no offence, aunt. My map says that your Turk is not so honest a man as your Christian. I cannot find by the map that your Mufti is orthodox—whereby it is a plain case that orthodox is a hard word, aunt, and *Hiccups.* Greek for claret.—*Sings.*
To drink is a Christian diversion,
Unknown to the Turk or the Persian.
Let Muhammadan fools
Live by heathenish rules,
And be damned over teacups and coffee.
But let British lads sing,
Crown a health to the King,
And a fig for your Sultan and Sophy.
Ah, Tony!
*Enter **Foible**, who whispers to **Lady Wishfort**.*

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 味儿！他能把造蜡烛的师傅连他一家老小都熏死！畜生东西，我真不知拿他怎么办。——游历，亏他说得出；是啊，游历游历，滚罢，滚得越远越好，滚到萨拉森人、鞑靼人、土耳其人那里去！——你这畜生的异教徒，不配在基督教邦国里活！

**威尔福爵士.** 土耳其人，不；不要土耳其人，姨妈：您那些土耳其人是异教徒，不信葡萄。您那些穆罕默德徒、您那些穆斯林，是干巴巴的臭家伙——没有冒犯的意思，姨妈。我的地图上说，您那土耳其人，不如您那基督徒诚实。我在地图上查不出您那穆夫提是正统——由此可知正统是个艰深的字眼，姨妈，是——（打嗝。）——希腊话的红酒。——（唱。）

喝酒是基督徒的消遣，
土耳其人波斯人全不懂。
让穆罕默德的傻子们
守异教徒的规矩去，
守着茶杯咖啡，咒他们下地狱。
不列颠的少年只管唱，
斟满一杯祝君王，
苏丹索菲——不值一枚无花果。

啊，托尼！

*（**福伊布尔**上，向**威什福特夫人**耳语。）*

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
*Aside to **Foible**.*—Sir Rowland impatient? Good lack! what shall I do with this beastly tumbril?—*Aloud.* Go lie down and sleep, you sot!—or as I’m a person, I’ll have you bastinadoed with broomsticks.87—Call up the wenches.
Sir Wilful
Ahey! Wenches, where are the wenches?
Lady Wishfort
Dear Cousin Witwoud, get him away, and you will bind me to you inviolably. I have an affair of moment that invades me with some precipitation—you will oblige me to all futurity.
Witwoud
Come, knight.—Pox on him, I don’t know what to say to him.—Will you go to a cock-match?
Sir Wilful
With a wench, Tony? Is she a shakebag, sirrah? Let me bite your cheek for that.
Witwoud
Horrible! He has a breath like a bagpipe!—Aye, aye; come, will you march, my Salopian?88
Sir Wilful
Lead on, little Tony—I’ll follow thee, my Anthony, my Tantony. Sirrah, thou shalt be my Tantony, and I’ll be thy pig. *Sings.*
And a fig for your Sultan and Sophy.
*Exeunt **Sir Wilfull** and **Witwoud**.*
Lady Wishfort
This will never do. It will never make a match—at least before he has been abroad.
*Enter **Waitwell** disguised as **Sir Rowland**.*

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.**（向**福伊布尔**旁白。）——罗兰爵士等急了？天哪！这辆烂粪车可怎么打发？——（高声）躺下睡去，你这酒鬼！——不然我以人格担保，叫人拿笤帚棍打烂你的脚底（87）。——把丫头们都叫来。

**威尔福爵士.** 嗬！丫头们，丫头们都哪儿去了？

**威什福特夫人.** 亲爱的威特伍德表侄，把他带走罢，你就把我永永远远拴在情分上了。我有一桩要紧事，火燎眉毛般压上来——你要成全我，成全到永久。

**威特伍德.** 来罢，爵士。——瘟的，我真不知拿他说什么。——去看斗鸡，去不去？

**威尔福爵士.** 带个妞儿去么，托尼？她是一只斗鸡袋么，小子？为这话叫我咬你一口腮帮子。

**威特伍德.** 可怕！他这口气活像一具风笛！——是，是；来，肯开步走了么，我的萨洛普人（88）？

**威尔福爵士.** 头里带路罢，小托尼——我跟着你，我的安东尼，我的圣安东尼。小子，你就做我的圣安东尼，我就做你那口猪。（唱。）

苏丹索菲——不值一枚无花果。

*（**威尔福爵士**与**威特伍德**同下。）*

**威什福特夫人.** 这可不成。这门亲做不成——至少，得等他留过洋。

*（**韦特韦尔**扮作**罗兰爵士**上。）*

===Original===
Lady Wishfort
Dear Sir Rowland, I am confounded with confusion at the retrospection of my own rudeness!—I have more pardons to ask than the pope distributes in the year of jubilee. But I hope where there is likely to be so near an alliance, we may unbend the severity of decorum, and dispense with a little ceremony.
Waitwell
My impatience, madam, is the effect of my transport; and till I have the possession of your adorable person, I am tantalised on the rack, and do but hang, madam, on the tenter of expectation.
Lady Wishfort
You have excess of gallantry, Sir Rowland, and press things to a conclusion with a most prevailing vehemence.—But a day or two for decency of marriage—
Waitwell
For decency of funeral, madam! The delay will break my heart—or if that should fail, I shall be poisoned. My nephew will get an inkling of my designs and poison me—and I would willingly starve him before I die—I would gladly go out of the world with that satisfaction. That would be some comfort to me, if I could but live so long as to be revenged on that unnatural viper!
Lady Wishfort
Is he so unnatural, say you? Truly I would contribute much both to the saving of your life, and the accomplishment of your revenge.—Not that I respect myself; though he has been a perfidious wretch to me.
Waitwell
Perfidious to you!
Lady Wishfort
O Sir Rowland, the hours that he has died away at my feet, the tears that he has shed, the oaths that he has sworn, the palpitations that he has felt, the trances and the tremblings, the ardours and the ecstasies, the kneelings and the risings, the heart-heavings and the hand-gripings, the pangs and the pathetic regards of his protesting eyes!—Oh, no memory can register.
Waitwell
What, my rival? Is the rebel my rival?—a’dies.
Lady Wishfort
No, don’t kill him at once, Sir Rowland, starve him gradually, inch by inch.
Waitwell
I’ll do’t. In three weeks he shall be barefoot; in a month out at knees with begging an alms.—He shall starve upward and upward, ’till he has nothing living but his head, and then go out in a stink like a candle’s end upon a save-all.

===Chinese===
**威什福特夫人.** 亲爱的罗兰爵士，回想我自个儿的失礼，我惭愧得五内翻腾！——我要讨的赦免，比教皇在大赦年里散发的还多。不过我想，眼看要做这么亲近的姻亲，咱们大可松一松规矩的紧箍，免掉这一星星繁文缛节。

**韦特韦尔.** 我的急切，夫人，是我的狂喜结出的果子；一日不把您这可敬可拜的人儿弄到手，我就受着刑架的酷刑煎熬，夫人，吊在企望的张布架上，干熬。

**威什福特夫人.** 您的殷勤过了度，罗兰爵士，催着事情收梢，那股猛劲儿谁也挡不住。——只是为着婚事的体面，宽限一两天——

**韦特韦尔.** 体面的该是葬礼，夫人！再拖就要拖碎我的心——不然，我也会叫人毒死。我侄儿探得我的盘算，就要下毒——我倒乐意死前先饿死他——我巴不得带着这份满足离了人世。那于我倒是一桩安慰，只要我活得够久，报得着那条毒蛇逆种！

**威什福特夫人.** 他这样忤逆，你说？说真的，为救您的命、成全您的复仇，我都愿意多多出力。——倒不是顾惜我自个儿；虽说他待我是个背信弃义的坏蛋。

**韦特韦尔.** 背信弃义，待您！

**威什福特夫人.** 哦罗兰爵士，他在我脚下憔悴掉的那些时辰，他洒过的泪，他发过的誓，他心口感到过的悸动，那一场场失魂同战栗，那一阵阵炽情同狂喜，那一跪跪同一起起，那心潮翻涌同十指紧攥，那阵阵绞痛，他那双赌咒发誓的眼睛里动人的顾盼！——哦，什么记忆也登记不下。

**韦特韦尔.** 什么，我的情敌？那反贼是我的情敌？——气煞我也。

**威什福特夫人.** 不，别一下子杀了他，罗兰爵士，慢慢地饿他，一寸一寸地饿。
